# Character State

**HOW TO READ THIS FILE. Append-only; each batch appends a block and does not replace the one before. The authoritative block for the current phase is the last one, headed *VOLUME 07, BATCH 0004 — ten days, ten people, and nobody in them got stronger*, and anything older that disagrees with it is superseded, and the next phase is `workspace/volume-07/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`. **Where this file and a chapter disagree about a day, a name or a figure, the chapter is right.** It is over 400 KB because forty batches have appended to it; read the last block and not the file.**

Compact live state for the next batch. **Updated after Batch 0006 (Chapters 48–50), which closes Volume 01.** Private want, current pressure, physical state, and what the last seven chapters cost them. **Owen Park is dead as of the thirty-eighth day and his entry below is a record, not a plan.** The full character bible is `bible/characters.md`; the long arc is `bible/premise.md`.

## Adrian Vale — 32, protagonist

- **Stage 1, Witness.** Perceives the nearest join-line and understands one direct Veyran promise at a time. Cannot create a working, open a threshold, or hold a place. **Unbound — no workway, refused three times.** The reason on the keeper's board was taken off by him with a cloth in daylight on the sixteenth day of the new month and is now only in the book.
- **Mark:** the pale fragment-mark is in the maintenance seam. Under it, separate, two inches of hair-thin black opened on the second night, now **forty-nine days old and unmoved by one sixteenth of an inch.** He looked at it in lamplight in the gallery on the night of the forty-seventh day and it was the same, and he looked at it again on the forty-eighth.
- **Seat: GONE. The term ended at noon on the forty-ninth day**, in daylight, because a term is a term and a date is a date, with no room convened and nothing renewed, and the terms went with it. **A vacant seat is not a fault.** The bar on him stands against whoever fills it, and it may not be filled without a room, in daylight, with a date on it.
- **Barred from the instrument he committed to:** not on the roll of persons who may open the distributed working, at his own request, in his own hand, with the date on it, and nobody whose standing comes from him may open, inspect, sign or suspend it. **He could not get that accepted for two hours and ten minutes and the sentence that got it accepted was said by Priya Shah and not by him, and it was about a page and not about heroism.**
- **Debt:** a season of the bottom field, entered day 3, continued and enlarged on day 30, now **forty-seven days old with no final figure**; the whole of the Reed Reach's standing charge as well, returned to him by Nine Elms on the thirty-sixth. A season of that field has now been entered in the book **seven times**, and the notary has said the other half of her sentence: **a column re-entered at its new size seven times in one month is a column being kept honest, and that is the first thing in five weeks she has been able to enter that she is not ashamed of.**
- **Commitment: performed, by other people, on the forty-ninth day, and he performed nothing.** He was four feet back on the sill of the third bay with a rule and a stub of chalk, and a docker made him say out loud, in the local language and to about twenty men, that he was not in it. **He is party to the trial licence and is given nothing by it, and that is on the page in the notary's hand.** **The licence says nobody answers for the next test**, and he said the sentence himself: *a licence with my name on the top of it is a document that only this city has ever been frightened of.*
- **What he has learned about himself in the closing three chapters:** that an answer is in a person and a rule is in a page; that **a question is not a post**; that the difference between a price and a wage is the difference between a man and a slot; and that **a seat is refused by a room and not by a man**, and that he said the word *class* in front of two hundred and forty people and was stopped by a document rather than by a woman, and that the word he meant was *people*.
- **What he has learned about himself in Batch 0005:** that a rule about a person and a post about a person are the same shape of rule and only one of them is still standing; that **a rule which names a man is a chain and a chain is a thing you ask a man to hold by name**; that a true reason and a fair reason are not the same sentence and the room can always use the fair one; that **he has been the fair reason for five weeks and somebody else has to find his**; that a sentence in a room is not a wall; that the owing in a crew is the mechanism and not the fairness; and that **he does not think of things that are about his own death, which is a fault and not a virtue, and a woman of twenty-four has entered it as one.**
- **Voice:** shorter, worse at pretending, and getting better at stopping. Slips: *chair* (corrected in public by Tamsin Quill on the thirty-fifth), *cohort* (Priya Shah, a hall, the twenty-sixth), **\*charter\*** (Tamsin Quill, a yard, the forty-seventh), and **\*class\*** (the twenty-fifth day of the new month, a room of two hundred and forty people, **corrected by a procedure and not by a woman** — the bar from the twenty-sixth day read the document back as an error, and the word he meant was *people*, and about two hundred and forty people heard the difference). Never says *quest*. The volume ends on a boy of nine asking him whether the next ones are going to be players, and his entered answer is that he does not have one and is going to find one and **that the answer is not going to be the word.**
- **Body:** the cut hand healing. Headache behind the left eye. About four hours a night. **Fifty days in this world. He said *yes* in a room on the forty-seventh day and could not sleep, and on the fiftieth day he was asked a question by a child and had not found an answer, and he wrote that down in his own hand because it is a fact about him and not a fact about the city.**

## Tamsin Quill — 29, anchorwright

- **State:** working partner, not lover, at the end of the volume. She corrected him in public four times in twenty days, the fourth of them by procedure rather than by voice. She was at the anchor side of the second bay for the eleven minutes of decided work on the forty-ninth day and has entered, in her own words, that **she did not feel a working**: *a thing with four of the four things and no man in it is not a working, it is forty men doing a Tuesday's job on a named tide, and it closed a join because a Tuesday's job closed it, and I did not do anything.* She wrote the next inspection date in the public ledger on the fiftieth day while a page naming Adrian as the route's first witness lay two hundred steps below under a piece of ordinary river stone. She is the first public source of the word that is the shape of this volume's problem, and she **asked herself not to enter it for two days and then gave her reason in a room with four people in it**, which is the first time she has entered a reason of her own about a thing she did.
- **What she did this batch:** named the distribution of the problem in a hall of two hundred and forty by saying one word after reading a document aloud twice; proposed on a walk-round that the ordinary work of the Lock of Salt **is** the working that closes the join and that **the hand in it is the crew and not a man**, which has no instance in this world; and said on a walk-round, "I did not bring him down here to use him for it. I brought him down here because I am going to tell him something and I want him to be standing next to a thing that cannot be opened, and I want him to look at it while I say it."
- **The old form of the oath, on the page for the first time:** it binds **the gate and the standing of the man in it**; it was taken out in Saltwake sixty years ago; a man in it died because the standing was a man and the man could not be got and the gate did not open; about four hundred people were on the wrong side of a river in a flood month; there is no stone anywhere and the list is in Aurel. **Four generations of women keeping that ground have said four words and a fifth for sixty years and the fifth is not that word.**
- **She declined twice to find his sentence for him** and said why: that she said the true one in a hall of three hundred and fifty on the thirty-ninth and about nine people wrote it down and none of them used it, and a sentence in a room is not a wall.
- **Exposed flank:** line seven of the boundary table, and the whole of the old form of the oath, which she read in a hall and which nobody in that hall had heard before.
- **On the fiftieth day of the new month** she was the one who said the word *nobody* could not be filled in, she was the one who put it on the record that Marrow did not put his hand up, and she did not put her own name on the roster of the twenty-four day of the flood. **She is Unbound-adjacent: she holds a register, not a licence, and no instrument in Volume 01 gives her a seat.**
- **Not yet:** she does not hold page forty-one and has not been asked for it. **Sera Vail holds it.**

## Mina Okoye — 29, first-cohort medic

- **Injuries:** none new. Working four days a week in Saye's room.
- **What changed in Batch 0005:** **her sentence was used three times in that batch as a load-bearing rule about forty men** — that four of them will be hurt before March, one at a time, on an ordinary morning, and no instrument in this world can stop it — and every use credited her, and it is still hers and nobody has claimed it. The sixth clause of Adrian's form was used on the forty-seventh day to stop a promise standing in for a page, and the sentence that did it was not hers and she was in the room and said so afterwards.
- **In the closing three chapters:** her sentence is quoted, in her own words and credited to her, inside the four settled pages of Owen Park's death, and it is the only sentence in the book about forty men that has been entered as a finding-adjacent thing in a document that cannot be shortened. **She is the reason nobody may write *he died because*, and she has never been thanked for it.**
- **Pressure:** her rule now runs in three parts — numbers as *what we said*; names as *what the person said about themselves*; and the harm clause, which is now a clause against a city and against a promise. **She reads the Crown's notice aloud to dying men.**
- **Want:** to get back to her family and to be proven right about something other than a river. **Fear:** that a hold she did not call in time is a working nobody agreed to, and there are about nine holds a day happening in a resonant field in a yard under a chapel.

## Dante Kuo — 35, Hearthguard captain in everything but name

- **Injuries:** right forearm reduced day 10, fine. Right shoulder went on day 31, reduced twice, not settable. Untouched in this batch. He will not be able to put both hands above his head; he will not notice for a year. He does not die in this volume.
- **What changed:** nothing on the page this batch, and that is deliberate and is now a pressure. **The rescue argument is still owed and Saye's clock has gone quiet and he has not had a scene in seven chapters.** He was put on paper work by a man in a good coat for five days in Batch 0004 without being told and the shoulder did not go.
- **Untouched through the closing three chapters and that is now a pressure, not a mercy.** He can be useful in Volume 02 and the man who made him useful is in every room. **No scene may make him die and no scene may make him whole.**

## Priya Shah — 24, first public record

- **Injuries:** forearm, healed.
- **What changed, and it is the largest single change in the book:** **she has now twice produced the sentence that made a thing possible in this city.** The fifth word in a yard at ten to six on the fortieth day. **And on the forty-seventh day, the refusal to let a promise stand in for a page** — *do not promise, promises have no date and no price and nobody can check them in four months, sign the paper* — said in English in front of about two hundred people, with the reason that a promise binds one person to another person and there is no page, and the only man she has ever watched make a promise in this city is under a board with a lease he never fulfilled.
- **And then, when the bar on Adrian could not be accepted for two hours and ten minutes because nine people thought he was building a martyr, she said the sentence that got it accepted, and it was about documents and not about heroism: a bar on a man is a thing this city can only obey while the man is standing in the room; meaning it is not a term; what I am asking for is the page.** And she asked for it to be entered that **he did not think of it in two hours and that he does not think of things that are about his own death, and that this is a fault in a man and not a virtue, and she would like it entered as a fault.** **She was twenty-four and she got there before he did and she wrote it in the margin of a slate the night before.**
- **In the closing three chapters** she produced the volume's last new civic sentence, to a Crown assessor of strangers with a pen in his hand and a column four lines high: *you have got a column four lines high. Write *died here on the thirty-eighth day of the flood; the account is four pages and is in the fourth volume; the four pages may be read by any person who asks for them and a copy is made at a person's cost. That is the whole of what a column four lines high is for, and it is true, and if a clerk in Aurel cannot read that then a clerk in Aurel does not get a reason.* And she said the word she said on the forty-seventh day in a room of two hundred and forty people: **it is a page, I do not care what you meant, I am asking for the paper.**
- **Standing:** her log now carries a refused gatekeeper offer, a refused Brass Measure deal, a refused workway she wanted, Owen's refused offer, Adrian's second refusal, the public statement she did not write, three withdrawals by Jonah Pike, a clause that stopped a promise, and a clause that put a man off a roster.
- **Fear:** that the second group will be called her people, and that she will enjoy it. And now: that she is becoming a person whose sentences make rooms change, and that she has not decided whether that is a service or a capture.

## Jonah Pike — 42, the colonial mirror

- **Injuries:** none. He arrived dry and has stayed dry.
- **What changed:** **he argued the Crown's form in nine sentences in a yard on the forty-sixth day and was right in eight of them, and the ninth was not about Aurel and Adrian had been waiting three weeks for it to be.** Adrian said the tenth out loud — a Crown advocate is a man who is expensive and he is not on this side of the water — and then said to him that he is not wrong to want a structure and that this city does not have to want it in order to be a place where a man can want a structure out loud without being asked to leave. Jonah said **say that again in a month.** Adrian said **I will not.** Both are in the book.
- **Untouched in the closing three chapters.** He argued the Crown's form in nine sentences and was right in eight, Adrian said the tenth out loud, and Jonah said *say that again in a month* and Adrian said **I will not.**
- **Want:** a safe, recognisable settlement where the seven can live without depending on an indifferent Earth. He has not got it and he has begun to suspect the settlement is the thing that arrives instead of the home. **The White Ledger is four volumes away and this is where it starts.**
- **His ninety-per-cent rule:** he is right about nine parts in ten of almost everything, and the tenth part is always the same part, and the tenth part is always a local woman's word for a social fact. *Renna Ord's word is* order. *Dante Kuo's is* cheap. **In Chapter 46 the tenth part was Adrian's own sentence about a man who is expensive, and Jonah took it without flinching, and that is new and is not good.**

## Noor Haddad — 27, quartermaster

- **Injuries:** none new.
- **What changed:** her column carried the reading of about ninety hands on the seventeenth day of the new month, and a docker of about fifty-eight said the reason was **because I am tired**, and Noor worked out in about an hour that the ninety hands were a quarterly payment and not a Crown, and Priya Shah had it entered so that in four months nobody can read that this city chose a single-author Crown containment and have it be even nearly true. **She has had her scene and it was the right size and it was about money.**
- **Pressure:** the whole of the crew's shift is now owed by the city, and the city's credit is a field, and a quartermaster is the person in the room who has to say that out loud before anybody else notices.

## Owen Park, 36, security contractor — DEAD, day 38, permanently, and BURIED

- **Died at about half past four in the afternoon of the thirty-eighth day of the flood, in the low room by the salt wharf**, and was buried on the **forty-first day, the Sunday, in the ordinary ground behind the ordinary wall**, in the ground where Hettis Roke's grandfather is buried and where eight of the graves have no name stone. **That ground now holds twelve graves.**
- **The burial happened.** The slab is a flat one out of the yard's waste pile, set on a bed of gravel, four inches proud, entered by Vask as left so that it can lean. **The name is cut twice, in two languages, in two hands: OWEN PARK by Peth Lomas, and OWEN PARK again by Hettis Roke, upside down, because he is not a mason and nobody in that ground said so to him.** About a hundred and forty people. Hesper Saye read the four pages in about nineteen minutes, in full, in the language of the room, not summarised, and did not cry, and stopped for about two seconds at the item about the shoulder. **About eleven people in that ground did not know what to do with their hands for the whole of it, because a reading is not a ceremony, and about nine of them were holding a coffin-board at the end of it and the carpenter's boy, who is seventeen, has entered that nobody told him what to do.**
- **A man is a scar in a person.** It is in his own account, it is his own, it is the eleventh item, and Roke said the other half of it out loud at the grave — a line in a place can be mended with work and money and a mark in a person cannot, because nobody steps over a hand — and Vask came about halfway through the reading and stayed and has entered no reason for it.
- **The two people who asked Hesper Saye afterward whether she was all right** are in the book.
- **No respawn, no return, and the mark does not transfer.** There are two marks in the world and one of them is his, and it is the first black scar carried in a person's own return mark and the first one that ends a life. Eli Mercer's is not one.
- **Unresolved and owed:** a letter to Hattie Park in Dunedin that cannot be sent, and a man in a good coat who offered a Crown channel on the fortieth day and has been neither refused nor accepted and has not mentioned it in seven days. A twelve-month lease with two conditions he never met; Delya Crane has entered that the conditions stand. A boy of nine who followed him at six feet. A statement on a loft wall with an empty chain of three names. **And a ground with twelve graves and eight name stones, and one of the twelve is a Hearthguard's grandfather, and it is in Roke's own yard, and Roke cut one of the two languages upside down and has not said one word about his eight.**

## Ivenn Marrow — 41, Quiet Choir's First Reader

- **Public face:** Crown Law scholar from Aurel, in the city nine weeks, the man who arrived before the Crown's clerks. Charming, patient, useful, and the only person in Saltwake not frightened by the floor.
- **What he did in this batch, four things, all of them in public and none of them convictable:**
  - **He offered the third option in a more attractive form, for about two hours, in a room in a house with a burnt lintel, with no document.** The containment held by Adrian, requiring **nothing of Aurel** — no workway, no mark, no stipend, no advocate, no seal, no office, **no Crown** — entered in this city's own book in this city's own words, with his own undertaking under his own hand that the Crown has no part in it, and Adrian's answer on the containment not answerable by any other person in Saltwake. **He did not ask for obedience and he did not ask for a mark and he asked for a seat**, and a seat is a place to be angry at and has to be legible to a person who does not like you, and **that is the one argument in this city Adrian could not get round and did not say he could not get round for two hours.**
  - **He said, in about four seconds, and then again in English so that it would be in the book in both:** *I did not have a man to die. I have nineteen years of other people's records, and a great many of them are about men who did not die.* **And his face did not do anything**, which Roke entered as the first time in nineteen years he has seen a face do nothing on a true thing, and Adrian entered that **a man who has kept the record for nineteen years and is asking to be the only hand that answers is not asking for a seat** and that he does not have the name for it and is not going to invent one in a room.
  - **He was the most useful man in a room of two hundred and forty people for nine hours on the forty-seventh day while the thing he wanted was refused in that room.** He carried a bench for a woman of seventy and put it at the side and not the back. He read the fourth condition twice for a gate-hand who cannot hear in a big room, slowly, in the local language, repeating *at the ordinary volume* twice. He got forty cups of water out and **asked a docker to carry them and to keep four of them, and she kept four of them.** He said one true sentence across nine feet of floor to Adrian, in front of two people, and it is entirely true: *that you have said the truest sentence anybody has said in this room in five weeks, and I would like to enter that you said it, and I am not going to offer you anything, and I would like somebody to notice that I have spent forty days being the most useful man in every room in this city and that this morning I had nothing to be useful with, and that I do not think that was luck.* **He said nothing wrong.**
  - **He was not convicted and was not accused and offered no document**, and he has still not mentioned the letter to Hattie Park.
- **What nobody suspects:** that the prompts are his. He is the only man in Saltwake with both reason and access. **His complicity must stay plausible and unproven to the end of the volume and he must stay publicly useful. No public scene may convict him.** He is now, in Adrian's own notebook, the man Adrian cannot work out — *I do not know what a man in a good coat does on a day when the thing he came here for did not happen, and that is the first honest thing I have been able to write about him in forty-seven days, and it is not a thing I can use, and he knew that it would not be a thing I could use, because he is very good at rooms, and he offered it to me anyway.*

## Working cast

- **Sera Vail** — 50, Lantern Concord notary. In this batch she **wrote the ninth page of the trial instrument**, answered the Registry's demand a second time in nine lines that are not the form required, **stopped Adrian in the middle of the best speech he has given in this city and made him say it as what he said because it was a finding**, entered that the two instruments that look as though they agree do not agree, **entered that the show of hands was a show of hands and not a count and that a room that has been asked to choose in twenty minutes has chosen what it was given and not what it wanted**, entered that a cost column re-entered six times in a month is a column this city can afford, and entered that she **cannot enter a man's request to be removed from the terms of a post** because a man who asks to be taken off a duty in the room where the duty is being decided has understood the room. She holds page forty-one and has not mentioned it. **She is the moral centre of the volume and she is not doing it knowingly.**
- **Hettis Roke** — Hearthguard of the outer ward, nineteen years, waxed slate. **He cut the second language on a grave upside down and said in advance that nobody in that ground was to come and say so to him and not to the man under it.** He entered that a face did not do anything. **He gave Sabra Vint a true answer in a doorway that was not the answer** — *eight of the eleven behind this wall have no stone and I did not ask you in a yard because a man who uses a woman of sixty-one's nine years to get at his own eight is doing a thing I would think less of him for* — and she said: *that is a true sentence, it is not the answer, and I have never once been answered with a sentence about somebody else's nine years.* **He said no and that is entered and he is not going to say anything else about it.**
- **Ottoline Vask** — 34 years in Saltwake, the technical authority, and the author of three load-bearing sentences this batch: *a floor that has come open once is not a pattern, it is a hole*; *the forty words are the toll and they are the pattern and a city cannot spend a thing and be its own foundation at the same time*; *it is a widening and not a rota, and if a name goes at the top of it I will read it out in a yard*. She also **entered her own decision about a prop in the near gate in her own hand at the seventh hour, before the number was read**, and put the new chalked mark on the wall herself, and held the chalk for a boy of nineteen because the stub was wet, and read back the two documents and the crew's reasons and the six conditions in English for people who would not have had them, and refused to soften one word. She has still never been able to get her hands on the nineteen stones.
- **Nyla Ferrow** — keeps the lock chapel, about seventy, four generations of women in the chalk on one side of a door. **On the sixteenth day of the new month she washed her own three lines off her own board in daylight in front of about a hundred and forty people with a cloth and a bucket and read out what came off before she took it off**, and she **did not** wash off the sentence a man had written about himself, and the reason is entered: *washing a man's own writing off a board is a decision and not a keeping.* At about the eighth hour of the evening she put the four words and the fifth back on and nothing else is on the keeper's side of that board. **She said four words to Adrian in a month and every one of them mattered, and the fifth of them was in a converted salt store about forty people and it was that a person who asks to be taken off a duty in the room where the duty is being decided has understood the room.**
- **Nell Bray** — docker, felt hat, about sixty. Said a record is not a fence; said *we had not been asked and we were not going to say* as one of three people who had known for a month that a crew was the answer; **asked for Adrian's *I do not know* to be entered on a walk-round and got it**; defined the seat again in front of about two hundred people and was right; and did not argue for anything in the review because the sentence had been said.
- **Ovin Sarr** — 19, out of the day crew, **eight watches in three weeks as of the forty-third day**. **He wrote *works* in the last column of the tide table on the forty-third day with a stick of chalk, small, with the *k* pressed too hard**, and asked *is that the right word* and was told *it is the only one anybody has ever written in that column*, and said *it is not the only word*, and the whole exchange is in the book. **He was given the bottom field on the forty-third day as a day's work and refused it in nine seconds in front of about forty people**: *a field is not a day's work; a field is a thing a man is asked for again next season, and a day's work is a thing a man hands back.* He **voted against** the distributed working on the forty-sixth. He has the number in his hand on the wall of the second bay and he does not know that nine people in a gallery decided about four seconds after a step in the join that it was him.
- **Renna Ord** — boat-coat dock worker, fifty-eight, and the author of the shape of the closing working. See the thread file. **She holds the twenty-third and twenty-fourth readings' place in the tide table history, wrote *works* against the thirty-third in a Saturday morning, said the sentence about a lock that a guild can close, and has been entered as one of nine people who knew for a month that a crew was the answer and was not asked and did not say.**
- **Sabra Vint** — about sixty-one, of the upper road, house with a burnt lintel, **cannot read**, nine years of paper with nineteen shapes on it and eight of them different. **She said the truest thing anybody has said about her own paper: she writes the shapes and has never once written what she thinks the shapes mean, and somebody has to be the one who writes that down, and she is sixty-one and would like to stop.** And on the same night she said the number **nineteen** to a man on a road for the first time in nine years and he said goodnight.
- **Aldis Kerrow** — assessor of strangers, a very good coat, a narrow mouth. Read the three instruments of the seventeenth day of the new month aloud in about eleven minutes without editorialising once. Argued for keeping a post on the grounds that a seat with a person in it is the only thing this city has produced in forty days that a clerk in Aurel can be sent.
- **Marek Osley** — salt factor, about forty-eight. **He supplied the sentence that made the review of the post turn: a liability can be got rid of in a room and only a liability can be got rid of in a room.** He has to answer for the bottom field in March.
- **Elsa Skell** — 44, of Corrow, works the bottom field, has not spoken to Adrian since the eighteenth day. **In a hall of two hundred and forty people on the seventeenth day of the new month she said the reason the first instrument has nothing in it** — close it and put a lid on it is what you do to a bucket — **and named what happened on the twenty-fifth: a good working at that gate moved the water under that floor in exactly that direction and put eleven people out of it, and the man whose shoulder went that morning is under a board and it was his hand that went on the flagstone and he took a child's weight off it.** And she argued for keeping a post, because she is owed one season and she is not going to be the woman who makes it two.
- **Wenna Cray** — 41, of Silt Row, page one against his name. **Entered the third statement of error in the history of the book on the forty-seventh day, against a person, naming him, four sentences, and every one of them is correct**, and had it answered by *I stand by it and I can say no more*, and answered it herself with the shortest sentence in the batch.
- **Ilda Marsh** — 70, of Nine Elms, who wrote the fourth line and used it and is the reason a whole season is on a man from another world. Argued for keeping a post on the ground that a village with nothing in it has nothing to point at.
- **Hesper Saye** — 58, bone-setter. **Read four pages at a graveside without stopping and did not cry.** Has told four hundred men things about a shoulder or a scalp and been wrong about ninety of them, and this was the ninety-first, and she is not letting the figure be rounded.
- **Delya Crane, Sesh Brack, Hessa Ruun, Ilda Marsh, Osten Fenn, Gwil Merrivale, Nesta Gower, Rennet Vaughan, Ceri Vaughan, Peth Lomas, Hesk, Ferris, Teodor Blaz, Wren Halloway, Perry Lask, the girl of seventeen who takes the entries down** — all as in the bible, all on the page, none of them offstage without a reason.
- **Renner Sallow** — the Registry's second clerk at Aurel, at that table since the fourth day of the flood, and the Crown's face in this volume. He said in front of two hundred and forty people that the third instrument would satisfy his office and that the two documents from it are not the same instrument. He is the eighth entry in his own book of fields seen in a stranger's language. **He has never been asked to stop and is not going to be, and this city has not convicted him of anything and is not going to.**

## The closing three chapters, in one place

**Ottoline Vask** put her two sentences in front of a room of two hundred and forty people for the second time and **they were not answered**, and the answer to them was nine words from a docker and the room did not find it satisfactory and the crew did not care. **She also said the mason's sentence of the volume at the pale edge with a bar in it: a closed join does not get narrower, it gets dressed.** And she said the other half of the notary's sentence first, about a column being kept honest, and the half about the cost of the forty words is not a season and never will have a figure.

**Hettis Roke** went down two hundred steps with a gauge, said what he was looking at and said that the instrument in this city that has ever worked is four people turning up in the wrong weather and not a gauge; **watched the working from the step calling a number every eleven minutes into a slate**; read the end of the seat out loud at noon on the forty-ninth day; and on the fiftieth day **wrote the nineteen stones into an empty cell on a stranger's wall and put a single number at the bottom of it, and said nine seconds about why, and has still not been asked about his own eight in nine years.**

**Nyla Ferrow** dictated a five-item form for watching a hole in a floor that cannot mend it, with a price on it, in her own words, with the word *form* in the book next to her name, and **asked the question that had been unasked for nine years**.

**Wenna Cray** refused a man a place on the list of nine and was right, said *then somebody had better put him in the form* and was right, and asked *fact or fault* in a wet gallery for the second time in nine days.

**Renna Ord** changed *refused* to *not owing*, named the cheapness of a stipend and why, answered Vask in nine words, and after fifty days was asked what she decided and gave the smallest answer in the book.

**Ovin Sarr** had his own name struck off the top of the roll's second page in daylight for a true reason, and wrote that word once and then put the chalk down rather than write it every time.

**Ferris**, a gate-hand of about fifty, who said in a yard that he would rather say no for a week and be asked again than say yes for good, **did not come back for the second shift on the day the working was done**, because he has not been paid, and there is no entry and no fault and nobody went to his door, and **the next evening, at about the seventh hour, before the tide, he wrote *works* in the box against the fiftieth day in a hand of about fifty years old, and nobody asked him what he wrote.**

**Eli Mercer** said yes once, to a page, and said four words when he came back, and was not asked a second question.

**Sabra Vint** put nine years of paper on a wall in two hands that are not hers and said the number nineteen to a boy of nine and told him that not asking for nine years is a different thing from not knowing.

**Ivenn Marrow** did not put his hand up, entered that he was going to and does not know why he did not, and **Adrian Vale cannot tell whether it was a choice or a technique and has entered that he cannot and that it is not a thing he can use.**

**The girl of seventeen** took down the fourth page, the fifth item of the form, the two witnesses, the opening, and the whole of the licence, and the width of her column is entered as a fact for the third time in the same year.

## Offstage but due

- **Nora Vale** (25) — Adrian's sister, mutual-aid logistics, Earthside, Camden, in the flat above a locksmith's. Three missed calls and one unanswered text on the eleventh. Does not know. **His name is on the last page of his notebook and he chose that page every day for forty days and wrote down that he chose it.** She runs a rota on Tuesdays and Thursdays and Saturdays at the Camberwell Larder. **There is no route in this world that a letter can travel. A letter has been written and has not been sent and has not been sent in seven more days.**
- **Hattie Park** — Owen Park's sister, of Dunedin, another hemisphere. Does not know. **He was buried on the Sunday. The letter cannot be sent. A man in a good coat offered a Crown channel on the fortieth day and has been neither refused nor accepted and has not mentioned it since, and on the forty-seventh day he sat in a room for nine hours and offered nothing at all.**
- **Lena Morrow** (22) — Earthside physics student, not yet arrived.
- **Regent Solenne Dary** — Aurel. The letters were Registry, not Regent, and she is not on stage and does not need to be.
- **The nine households of Nine Elms** — refused the charge as well as the receiving, the whole charge is on Adrian, and Ilda Marsh is the author and the user of the fourth line. **The bottom field is the city's credit for the crew's shift and Renna Ord has put the date on it: March.**

---

# Volume 02, Batch 0001 (Chapters 51–60) — what the last ten days cost people

**The header above still describes the end of Volume 01. This section supersedes it for everyone named in it and the entries above are to be read as Volume 01's last state.** Sixty days in this world. **Nobody gained a thing.**

## Adrian Vale — 32, protagonist

- **Stage 1, Witness. Unbound. Barred from every roster. Party to the trial licence and given nothing by it.** Performed no working in this batch. **Cannot open a threshold, create a working, or hold a place.**
- **Mark:** the pale fragment-mark is in the maintenance seam; the palm keeps a thin trace. **The black line in the heel of his right palm is fifty-nine days old and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch.** He looked at it in lamplight on the sixtieth and it is the same, and he has written that neither that fact nor the number on the wall is an answer and that both are the only things he has.
- **Seat: still vacant, still unrenewed.** He is on no roster of the licence and none of the instrument of the twenty-second day of the new month. **He has put his own name in one Crown document**, in a column headed *person to be asked*, in a schedule of crossings, on the thirty-first day of the new month, and said out loud that he had wanted a page with his name at the top of it for five weeks and that this was the cheapest possible version of one and that it is a Crown document.
- **Debt:** the bottom field, entered for the eighth time in one month, **fifty-seven days old and with no figure on the end of it.** The rail's timber is now a second cost on the same column. **The whole of the Reed Reach's standing charge is still on him and Nine Elms' water is still not received.**
- **What this batch cost him, item by item:**
  - **The door is not a road home, and he has said so nine times in nine rooms in one day**, and has entered that he made a promise of fair access in the first week, that it is fair, that it is not possible, and that he has been saying both in one breath for sixty days.
  - **He is a person's name in a Crown column and he wanted it.**
  - **He wrote the word *player* on a market board on the ninth day of this flood and let it stand for forty-five days and said so out loud in about two hundred and forty people**, and a woman of forty-one withdrew a belief about him and not a vote, and a woman of twenty-four wrote fifty-nine words over it in chalk.
  - **His best page does not reach.** *I wrote a rule about a thing and I did not write a rule about the world, and I have spent fifty-seven days telling a city that the difference is pedantry.* **He said it in thirty-one words in front of nine people and then he said in the same room that the page is a mat and that a city puts a mat under a thing that has fallen on the floor.**
  - **He said out loud in a yard that he had a tenth sentence ready and that it was the wrong one**, and that the ninth sentence he was given was better than the tenth, and about two hundred and forty people heard a man admit an error in public for the first time in this world.
  - **He wrote a list of nine names of people who would be angry if a workway were given, and gave it to a man he had met once**, and the man read it and said *then it is not about me*, and two people who are on no other list got onto that page by being in a room and were not asked.
  - **He has written about six pages about what a person may not do and one sentence about what a person may do, and the sentence is twenty-four words long and it is Tamsin Quill's, and she said it once and said it on a stair.**
- **Voice:** four corrections in public are still on the record. **No new slip in this batch and that is entered as a fact and not as a virtue.** He was stopped by a procedure twice and by a woman once.
- **Body:** the cut hand healing. Headache behind the left eye. **About three hours a night for ten days, and he has entered that he is not sleeping because of a knock and has not written down which knock.**
- **Where he is:** two hundred steps above the Lock of Salt at about the eleventh hour of the night of the sixtieth day, having asked out loud what a city does about a knock and had no answer, and about nine people having heard the question.

## Tamsin Quill — 29, anchorwright

- **What she did in this batch:** read out that every standing in this city came out of a room and that there is no place in Saltwake where somebody expects a holder of a workway; **gave a woman of twenty-four words on a stair on the thirty-fourth day of the new month and entered that she said it and not that she found it, and that she had been asked twice this month to find his sentence and had declined twice, and that this was the same work and not the same favour**; and said the sentence that is the chapter's title in her own book, four lines: *go and ask the city what a person with no work is for, and take the answer, and do not offer anybody a seat.*
- **What it cost her:** she has now declined to find his sentence twice and given it once, **and the one she gave was for him and not for a room, and she has entered that difference herself**, and that entry is the most honest thing in the book about their relationship and neither of them has said a word about it out loud.
- **Exposed flank:** unchanged — line seven of the boundary table, and the whole of the old form of the oath. **She now knows what a workway is and that this city cannot host one, and she has said so in a room of nine.**
- **She does not hold page forty-one. Sera Vail holds it. Nobody has mentioned it in ten days.**

## Sera Vail — 50, Lantern Concord notary

- **What she did:** read the licence's second paragraph aloud to nineteen signers and entered that she had not asked one of them what it said; **invented no instrument and entered one that a woman of thirty-four with a bar thought of**; entered that the schedule of crossings is a fourth document and not a version of the other three, **for the fifth time**; wrote **a column headed *person to be asked* out loud as the only seat in nine lines of Crown table**; entered that the honest useless form is the first instrument in the history of this book constructed to be unhelpful; **read the fourth page in full to about two hundred and forty people and said *it works, it is horrible and it is correct, and both of those are the same fact*, which is the first time in twenty-six years she has entered a page working**; and entered the nine words about the column for where the person is.
- **The single most important thing she did this batch was nothing: she did not fill in the last column of the Crown schedule with anybody's name but Adrian's, and she did not stop him, and she entered that the column is for the office and the office is nine hundred miles away and has never sent anybody.**
- **She still holds page forty-one and has not mentioned it. She is the moral centre of both volumes and is not doing it knowingly.**

## Ottoline Vask — 34 years in Saltwake

- **Five load-bearing sentences in ten days, all hers, all uncorrected:** *a price is not a fact about a thing, it is a fact about how many people want the thing on the same morning*; **a workway is a post with a person in it, and the post has not been through a room**; **fill it in, and make every line useless, because a summary is what an office acts on and a summary is where a decision hides**; the four who hold the door must not be the four who need the door, and now they are; and *it is a widening and not a rota* (Volume 01, still standing).
- **What it cost her:** nothing, and that is entered as a finding by Vask herself and not as a compliment. **She signed an inspection of the Narrow Mark on the fiftieth day of the new month and she is one of the four who goes down the rail in the wrong weather, and she and the other three did not agree about the dressed edge and she entered that they did not agree.**
- **She has still never been able to get her hands on the nineteen stones, and a docker of fifty-eight is now copying them into a slate every day and cannot read what she is copying.**

## Renna Ord — 58, boat-coat dock worker, cannot read

- **She holds the two sentences this volume is built on: *it is a docker that is a lock that a guild can close* (Volume 01) and *the four who hold the door must not be the four who need the door, and now they are* (this batch).**
- **She wrote the entry about a man who cannot be right about a number hearing a number, and she did not delete either fact, and it is now the standing rule for everything anybody in this city has ever heard.** Three knocks, four knocks, both entered, neither deleted, and the twenty-first line of the rule is *and a number going up is not a pattern and a number going up is also not nothing.*
- **She is one of the four who change the salt and take the chalk depths and go down the rail with a gauge, and she went down it on her hands and knees with a barrow tied to her back on the twenty-seventh day of the new month because nobody asked her to.**
- **She refused the Brass Measure's fourth term for the fourth time in eleven words, in about two hundred and forty people.** She told Adrian twice that he is not the man who wrote the four. She asked for the page about what happens on the day the crew does not turn up and it worked on the thirty-third day. **She has been asked nine times in sixty days and has answered twice and owes nothing and is going to be asked again.**
- **She picked up the work in the fourth column of a stranger's wall on the thirtieth and has been copying shapes into a slate since, and she cannot read what she is copying, and she has entered that she got tired of waiting and that is not a noble sentence.**

## Wenna Cray — 41, of Silt Row

- **The fourth statement of error in the history of the book**, four sentences, against a person, naming him, every one of them correct, **and the fourth of them withdrew a belief rather than a vote and asked for the form she was using.** All four stands. Adrian stood by it and said he can say no more.
- **She asked the fact-or-fault question in a yard on the twenty-sixth day of the new month and was told it is neither, it is a fact about a shape, and that is the third thing a question can be and he had not had it before.**
- **She refused the eleven-household plan in nine lines without raising her voice and got Priya Shah to enter that it was a good idea.**

## Hettis Roke — 44, Hearthguard of the outer ward, nineteen years in

- **He was asked about his eight graves on the thirtieth day of the new month, in daylight, in about two hundred and forty people, because the fifth item of the form is a question and not a duty. He said no to the form and yes to a yard, and the two are entered separately, and he asked to be asked again on a different day and said he will say no again for about four years.**
- **What he said, in forty seconds, in his own words: the names were written down once, in his grandfather's hand, in the year the ground was made, because a Hearthguard who held a gate was required to give a roll of the dead of that gate to the Crown twice a year; eight got a stone and four got a name cut on a neighbour's stone because there was not money for eight; the book went to Aurel in a wooden case four times a year for nineteen years; and nobody came.** He added that he has known about the four stones for nine years and has never said it in a yard.
- **He wrote down who would be angry if a workway were given, in a notebook, and gave it to a man he had met once, and did not ask first, and entered that he did not ask first.**
- **He went up the upper road for the first time in nine years on the thirty-fifth day of the new month, with a woman of sixty-one and a docker with a slate, and at the eleventh stone he put his hand on a name cut in his grandfather's hand and said four words: *that is my grandfather's*. He entered that his nine years of staying away was not grief but the walking, and that he has told this city grief for nine years and it was the walking, and he said it in about forty people's hearing without being asked.**
- **He is on no list. He has still not been thanked for the nineteen stones and has said he will not be thanked for a number in a cell on a stranger's wall. He has still not said a word about the ground behind the ordinary wall beyond the forty seconds above, and the forty seconds were about a machine.**

## Perry Lask — 41, of the second list — **the first person in this world to hold a workway**

- **Accepted the nineteenth field on the thirty-second day of the new month, in a room over a chandlery, in about four seconds, after saying one sentence first: that she had been waiting since the ninth day of this flood for the wall to say something she recognises, and that she was not going to pretend to a room that she checked it first.**
- **She said the rest of it out loud and it is her sentence and it is the volume's: *a rule that only covers the thing you can see is a rule about a thing and not about the world.*** She had read a licence page in four languages in her life and had broken every one of them.
- **She said *this is a closed beta* on the ninth day of this flood and had it entered that she said it first, and on the twenty-sixth day of the new month she said that a closed beta means the door is shut to everybody who is not already in it and that there are about four of them who cannot leave through it, and that she had been arguing about the definition with a man who designs things for a living and that he is wrong in a way that is useful to her and she has not told him so.**
- **State:** no office, no work, no expectation, nine clauses and nine weaknesses around her, a review date of the fourth day of the month after this one, and **she has not been asked to do a single thing and she has not done one.** She asked, in a room, what she is expected to do, and nobody could answer her, and the girl's of seventeen wrote that in four lines because the column is four lines high and not one word of it is a summary.
- **Adrian has written in a private book that she is the second most useful person in this city and that the first is a docker who cannot read, and he has never said either out loud, and he has also written that he has not forgiven the four seconds and has not been asked to.**

## Teodor Blaz — 31, of the second list, of Leeds

- **Right hand will not close since the twenty-fifth day of the flood, in the yard where the eleven came out. He put his hand into wet stone while about four hundred people were moving and the page that exists about it is four lines long and it is about somebody else.** Hesper Saye: there is nothing in the hand to set and what is wrong with it is not in the hand, and she has not told him his arm is fine because a man who is told his arm is fine stops counting things and then he has nothing.
- **Refused the eighteenth field in four seconds and said the true reason, which was not the good one: that he has not been angry at anybody in twenty-eight days and that if he took the field he would not have to count anything again.**
- **Asked the question nobody had asked him: *if I had said yes, who would have been angry?* — and was given nine names in a notebook, and read them, and said six words: *then it is not about me*, and then said: *it is about the room.***
- **At the end of the batch he said the sentence the volume has to answer: nobody in this city is angry about my hand, what happened is that there was nothing for me to be angry about, and every one of the nine clauses that were written about me is about what happens when a person is angry and not one word of them is about what happens when a person has nothing to be angry about.** He would like somebody in this city to be angry about something in March and does not care what.
- **He counts the double handfuls of salt into a slate and cannot read the shapes on nineteen stones and is copying them anyway.**

## Mina Okoye — 29, first-cohort medic

- **Her sentence is hers alone and is not coined by anybody: a workway is not a power, it is a job, and somebody in the world is entitled to expect a thing of the person who has it and to be angry if they do not get it, and the person who has it cannot stop.** She asked the standing question in a room of nineteen and got the answer that there is no office in this city for a hand that holds things.
- **She also caught Adrian's worst habit and made him say it out loud: that he had refused the field in under a second before he knew who it was for, and that a pale field is not addressed to a person, and that saying no first and understanding later is the single worst habit he owns.**
- **She made a doctor and a householder both say *I do not know what I am proposing* and then say it anyway, in that order, on the twenty-ninth day of the new month, and neither of them had practised it and she entered that as the only evidence in the room.**
- **She said yes quickly to the eleven-household plan being frightening and entered that quick is not the same as right and that she has been wrong ninety times and does not round the figure.**

## Priya Shah — 24, first public record

- **Six sentences that made a thing possible in this city, and the last two are this batch: *then put it through a room*, and fifty-nine words written in chalk over a word in a market.**
- **She asked the four questions of the thirty-first day of the new month** — what is the column for where the person is, and who fills it in, and what is there for a person who is in a column and not in a country, and how many columns are blank and is there anybody whose whole work is to notice — **and none of them has a rhetorical trick in it and all four are unanswered except in part.**
- **She told Adrian in twenty-five words what she had done by putting his name in a Crown column and made him repeat them twice and write them on a slate: *you have made yourself the only person in this city an office can write to, now be honest about how much of that you wanted.** He wrote them down and did not answer them and she has not asked again.
- **She made the girl of seventeen read the honest useless form aloud and enter that it is the first instrument designed to be unhelpful, and she entered that the compliance is dangerous because the eleven names and the sentence about no receiving community now go to Aurel four times a year.**
- **She is not on the roll of strangers and has not been asked about that either.**

## Ottavia Reek — second of the Brass Measure

- **Refused for the fourth time in eleven words, in about two hundred and forty people, in the yard, on the twenty-seventh day of the new month, and it was the best offer anybody in Saltwake has made anybody since the flood.** Thirty tons of salt at the retail price, timber, iron, lime, rope, a boat for a fortnight, and the rail in nine days, against the crew's list, **entered not as a contract but as a note in another hand in a materials account.** She said *then the rail is your rail* without heat. **The yard read it two ways and both readings are entered.** Her four idle elms and a bad winter are on the page and she is not a villain and she is a factor with a quarter and the price went up a third on the twenty-seventh day.

## Jonah Pike — 42, the colonial mirror

- **Came back on the thirty-fourth day of the new month with the only winter money in Saltwake and was refused in eleven seconds, and had it drafted himself, and entered that he knew before he opened it and brought it anyway.**
- **Nine sentences, right in eight, and the ninth is not about Aurel and it is the best thing anybody has said about Adrian in sixty days: you have spent fifty-nine days making sure that none of them has a job, and you wrote the page yourself, and in March one of them is going to be a person with no work in a city with no money, and you have made that a term, and a term is the most durable kind of promise there is.**
- **His fifth and sixth sentences are the front door of the White Ledger: a city in which nothing that goes wrong has a person in it is a city in which nothing that goes wrong can be fixed, and a guild is not a person and can be refused and does not go home and get old and want a different winter.**
- **Adrian said his tenth sentence out loud in a yard and said it was the wrong one. Jonah has not answered. The argument is not closed.**

## Renner Sallow — second clerk of the Registry at Aurel

- **Still never asked to stop and this city has not convicted him of anything and is not going to.** Count is ten. He has a second book at home with a column for refusals and **nineteen in it, and he has never shown it to anybody.**
- **He gave the volume its politics in fourteen words: a fee is a thing that tells you what an office thinks you are.** And: the office does not charge a fee it cannot collect. And he entered that he wrote that sentence and **does not know what it is about and has never written that in any book before.** And he identified the last column of the schedule as the only seat in nine lines of Crown table. **He is the most reliable witness in the book and he is on the wrong side of it.**

## New people, all on the page

- **Bern Zolt, 44** — asked to be returned once; the request is entered with a reason and not a refusal; has asked for a page saying he does not want to go back and has not been argued with.
- **Kirsty Lund, 16** — asked what she wants and said she wants to know what the shapes on the nineteen stones say; read a line of Adrian's own notebook back to him; **one of the two people who got onto the list of nine without being asked, and she is on no roll at all.**
- **Odile Ferrand, 38** — a review with no questions on it is a queue; the other of the two; her surname is on two lines of a narrow column twice and she is on no roll and has not been asked.
- **Curtis Okonkwo, 20** — came out of the chapel floor on the twenty-fifth day of this flood and has asked for nothing in the thirty-five days since, is on no list, has been fed every day by people who did not ask him whether he wanted the food, and asked the question the batch ends on.
- **Gareth Mbeki, 27** — proposed the eleven-household plan in four minutes and it was a good idea and four people were frightened of how good it was.
- **Tam Ockley, 20** — docker's apprentice, flood crew's second page, a name nobody in a yard has said out loud; went down on the rail on the twenty-seventh and could not rise for about nine minutes; hand's width of frostbite; will be all right.
- **A woman who keeps a stall by the wharf** — translates a fifth of Adrian's sentences when he cannot find the words, and did it twice in two days, both times on the day he admitted something.
- **A man in the lower gallery** — reported three knocks and then four, in a dressed join, and was asked whether he was sure of the number and said he was sure of the number and not of the knocks. **His name is entered because he is a person and not a machine for hearing things.**
- **A man in the lower gallery on the thirty-third day** — wrote the sixth word in the last column of the tide table in a hand nobody recognised, and it is not the word that is in the other five.

## Offstage but due

- **Dante Kuo — no scene in ten chapters of a new volume, and the rescue argument is thirty chapters overdue.** He is the most conspicuous silence in the book. **A Batch 0002 writer should give him something, even something small, even a scene in which he is useless and says so.**
- **Nora Vale, 25, of Camden**, above a locksmith's, runs a rota on Tuesdays, Thursdays and Saturdays at the Camberwell Larder. **A letter is in a tin and it is not sent and there is no road.** *Open.*
- **Hattie Park, of Dunedin.** **Thirty-five days since a Crown channel was offered on the fortieth and Marrow has not mentioned it since.** *Open.*
- **Ottoline Vask's two sentences** — the toll and the pattern, and a city cannot spend a thing and be its own foundation at the same time. Unanswered, and not put in front of a room in this batch, and must not be resolved.
- **The nine households of Nine Elms** — refused the charge as well as the receiving, the whole charge is on Adrian, and Ilda Marsh is the author and the user of the fourth line. **The bottom field is a season and a season is settled in March.**
- **The six of the first group and the eleven of the second group** are still named, still with no land rights, no class, no standing and no workway, and one of them now has a workway that the page did not forbid.

---

# Volume 02, Batch 0002 (Chapters 61–70) — what the ten days before the review cost people

**The section above describes the end of Batch 0001. This section supersedes it for everyone named in it. Seventy days in this world. Nobody gained a thing.**

## Adrian Vale — 32, protagonist

- **Stage 1, Witness. Unbound. Barred from every roster. Party to the trial licence and given nothing by it. Performed no working in this batch.** Cannot open a threshold, create a working, or hold a place.
- **Mark:** the black line in the heel of his right palm is **sixty days old on the sixty-first day of the new month and sixty-nine days old at the end of the seventieth, and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch.**
- **Seat: still vacant, still unrenewed.** He is on no roster of the licence and none of the instrument. He has put his own name in one Crown document, in a column headed *person to be asked*, and **he has now been offered the one thing he has never offered anybody in seventy days, which is to be asked, and he was refused in four seconds by the person it was for, and the refusal was correct.**
- **Debt:** the bottom field, **entered for the ninth time in one month, sixty-seven days old, and still with no figure on the end of it.** The whole of the Reed Reach's standing charge is still on him and **Nine Elms' water is not received for the coming quarter.**
- **What this batch cost him, item by item:**
  - **He did the thing he was told to do on a stair and it took four kitchens and a refusal in each**, and what he learned is that the room's word is not *work* and is *turning up*, and that a man who asks a city a question in the wrong language gets four wrong answers and none of them is anybody's fault.
  - **He fetched nineteen lines and nineteen lines are a widening and not a rota**, and about nine people understood that and about two hundred and thirty thought it was a job, and both numbers are on the page as what a man said and not as a count.
  - **He wrote out loud that he has been standing inside a roll of a different kind for sixty-three days without knowing it was a floor.**
  - **He offered a woman a bed and a woman of sixty-one told him why she has not spent a coin in nine years and it was not about money.**
  - **He offered the bar of the twenty-sixth day of the new month against the twentieth field, and the bar did not reach, and the thing that stopped it was a page and nine people and nine seconds**, and he has entered that the next time there may not be a room.
  - **He sat through forty minutes of an argument that was over in nine minutes and then said the real thing and did not resolve it, and he has entered that the refusal he got was correct and that he has no way of telling which of the man's two sentences was the one that mattered.**
  - **He had the last question on the slate written by somebody else and wrote down that he did not ask.**
- **Voice:** no new slip and that is entered as a fact and not as a virtue. He was stopped by a procedure three times and by a woman once.
- **Where he is:** the fourth step of the lower gallery of the Lock of Salt at about the eleventh hour of the night of the seventieth day.

## Tamsin Quill — 29, anchorwright

- **Gave the twenty-four words on the thirty-fourth day of the new month and was not asked twice. They were acted on over nine days.**
- **On the forty-fifth day of the new month, on the walk-round, she took the notebook out of his hand for the second time in nine days and gave him the thing nobody in this book has said better: a city is not a room; a room is people with a page on the table, and a page on the table turns people into the answer to the question, and about two hundred and thirty people hearing a document and not agreeing with it is not a failure of the document.**
- **She told him she was the ninth person in that yard to understand the nineteen lines, and that she had been waiting nine days to tell him, and that what she wants is not a thank you.**
- **And she said: she has been asked four times this month to find his sentence, has declined three times and given it once, the one was for him and not for a room, and she is not going to keep doing it at this rate because she will run out.** That is the closest this relationship has come to saying something about itself and it is a sentence about her own service and not about him.
- **Exposed flank:** unchanged — line seven of the boundary table and the old form of the oath. **She does not hold page forty-one. Sera Vail holds it. Nobody has mentioned it in twenty days.**

## Sera Vail — 50, Lantern Concord notary

- **Did not send the roll of strangers for the coming quarter and entered, on her own motion, that she did not send it, with two reasons — one true and one convenient — and said they are not the same one, and that the true one is that a description is the only protection anybody here has ever been offered by that office.**
- **Read the eleventh questions and said nine words: *I would not sign a page I cannot read.*** Said that reading is not believing and that she has believed four of these and has not believed seven. Said that the difference between a question a city asks when it wants to keep a door and one it asks when it wants to be able to close one is not in the tense and is not in the mood and is in **who is able to answer.**
- **Chose to send the second page to Aurel beside the eleven questions rather than by itself, for them and against herself, in nine seconds, and entered that she would have enjoyed the short answer enormously and has been enjoying it for twenty-six years and that is the whole of what is wrong with a notary.**
- **Drafted a question that is a trap and put it on the slate and told the slate so, and said that a notary who writes a fair question and holds an opinion about the answer is the exact mechanism this volume is about, and that she caught herself in four seconds and that four seconds is faster than she has ever caught anything.**
- **Answered *what ought the number of not-asking reasons to have been* with four hundred and ten things this city has not asked about, and put the twenty-six years on her own side of it.**
- **She still holds page forty-one. She is the moral centre of two volumes and is not doing it knowingly.**

## Ottoline Vask — 34 years in Saltwake

- **Wrote the nineteen lines of what a person in this city may be asked to turn up for in about eleven minutes, in about nine sentences, and said the second sentence in the book that says a working can be made cheaper without being made private and that this time it is about a person instead of a door.** Eight load-bearing sentences in ten days, all hers.
- **Said the finding of the thirty-seventh day in twenty-five words: this city has spent sixty-one days protecting eleven people by describing them, the describing was the whole of the protection, and there is no second thing.** It is the only finding in the history of this book with no number in it.
- **Said on the forty-second day: there is no salt within four hundred miles of this gate but ours, and an office that has just learned that is not being kind, and it is putting a number on us, and it is finding out what we can be made to do, and it is doing it kindly, and she would rather it were rude.**
- **She has still never been able to get her hands on the nineteen stones**, and a docker of fifty-eight has been copying them into slates for ten days and has copied about nine of the nineteen and has run out of slate.

## Renna Ord — 58, boat-coat dock worker, cannot read

- **Has been paid, in salt, for nine weeks of copying shapes she cannot read, and said nine words about it, and the ninth of them is the word for the metal the Crown's fee is in.**
- **Refused the Measure's fifth coat in about eleven seconds for the fourth time and said the rule in English on purpose, and it took the room nine seconds to refuse the twentieth field because a page exists and nine people read it out loud.**
- **Wrote the eleventh question for the review, four words long, on the back of a slate, with her own hand over a girl's fingers as a rest: *what are they called.*** Read it back, **did not like it, and said it stays**, because a question that is visible can be argued with and a question that is not on the slate gets written in a better way by somebody else in four months and means something else.
- **Is now the person the door costs, on the page, in a question of her own, on the fortieth day of the new month, and she has said that if the review answers it the licence is gone and the door is a hole in a wall again, and that she has never once pretended a door was worth a person, and that if she had she would be the wrong kind of man in this city and there is a page about it.**
- **She has been asked eleven times in seventy days and has answered five times, and she still owes nothing and is going to be asked again.**

## Peth Lammay — 24, of the second list, licensed carrier, on no roll

- **Asked the question nobody in this city can answer — does anybody here want that case to go — and nobody answered her, twice.** Then said thirteen words: *I will bring a case whatever is in it. Bring me a case.* And then in about thirty-nine words that she had been a carrier for eleven years and had never once said: **she would rather bring a case with eleven names in it than a case with nothing, and she does not know why.**
- **Read Dante Kuo four lines about a column with his name in it and did not tell him for nine days, and was made to answer for it in a yard in daylight, and was right about four of five things and wrong about the fifth — wrong because it *was* done to him — and said she would do it again and got it on the same line.**
- **Refused to carry Nine Elms' refusal on the Monday and carried it on the Tuesday, and wrote a line on the back of it about what a refusal costs in a family and not in a quarter.**
- **Is not on any roll in this city and has not been asked about that and has not asked.**

## Ivenn Marrow — 41, Crown Law scholar from Aurel, unaccused

- **Answered a question about the fee in a yard, in about nine seconds, without softening it and without offering anything, and said the sentence the city has needed for four months: *an office that will take your goods for its fee is telling you what it thinks you are, and it is also telling you what it thinks you will be*, and said he had never written a sentence like it and that he did not enjoy the writing of it.**
- **Said the useful half of the fee question — that he had been in this city eleven weeks and nobody had ever once asked him what a district is — in a yard and not in a room, and asked for that to be entered.**
- **On the forty-fourth day of the new month he asked Adrian Vale for the first time in two volumes: *what do you want me to be useful for.*** Adrian did not get a clever answer and said so. Marrow then said seventeen words: *I have been useful for a month, and it got me nothing, and I did not notice.* **This is the first sentence anybody has entered about him that is not about the door, and this city has not convicted him of anything and is not going to.**
- **He has still not mentioned the letter to Hattie Park. The count is flood days from the fortieth, on which the channel was offered: it is thirty at the end of Batch 0002, which is day 70.**

## Dante Kuo — 35, Hearthguard captain in everything but name

- **HAD HIS SCENE, on the forty-first day of the new month, and it is the most important new line in the book and it is not resolved.**
- **Right shoulder went on the thirty-first day of this flood, was reduced twice, cannot be set, and DID NOT GO in this batch. No scene made him whole and no scene may.**
- **Has not complained once in seventy days, and the reason is his and is forty-seven words: because a man complained about a shoulder in the fifth week of this flood and a page got written about him, and the page was right, and I have read it, and I would rather be the second kind of man in this city than the first.** The man on that page is a man of the flood crew and is not the man under the board behind the ordinary wall, and the two are in different books and may never be in one sentence.
- **Was put on paper work for five days by a man in a good coat without being told, found out on the fortieth day of the new month, and did not go to anybody: *nobody asked me and I am not going to be the kind of man who goes.** Sixteen words, his.
- **Refused Adrian's offer to be asked, in four seconds, correctly: *you are the only person here who gets asked. You, not me.*** And then the rest of it, forty-three words, asked to be entered whole: *if you ask me it stops being a thing I do and it becomes a thing I am a part of, and I have been in this world sixty-five days and I would like to have done one thing that nobody was keeping.* **Both of those are correct and one of them is the sentence the volume is about and neither man said which.**
- **Is now on the nineteenth line of the page about what a person in this city may be asked to turn up for, and about two hundred and forty people did not notice and about nine did.**

## Perry Lask — 41, of the second list, the first holder of a workway

- **Said yes to the twentieth field in about four seconds and did not say a sentence first, and the not saying is the finding.** The twentieth field says a holder's work may be described in any document in her own words without anybody asking her first and that nobody may be asked to describe it, and **read the third line against the first and it is a licence to be written about.** She was the only person who could say no to it and the offer removed the asking, so the asking was the whole of the cost.
- **Adrian refused it in under a second; the room refused it in nine seconds because a page exists; the acceptance is entered in her own words with a date and nobody has yet asked her whether she meant it.**
- **Is paid on a second page by a man who keeps a stall by the wharf and is named in a Crown schedule, so she is paid and she is described, both, and both are entered.** A man who keeps a stall by the wharf said fourteen words about it: *a wage off a second page is a wage a person can be removed from.*
- **Has been asked nothing.** What a holder will be asked on the fourth day of the month after this one was decided on the fortieth day of the new month and she was told a date existed in nine seconds and said six words: *then ask me on the day.*

## New people, all on the page

- **Nadia Brandt, 29, of the second list** — worked out the case, the roll and the disappearance in about an hour on a salt bin; told a woman of twenty-four and not the man; and then told both of them the true thing, in a room, in nineteen sentences, to two men who were both arguing about the wrong thing: **being useful is not safe and is not unsafe, it is a description.** On the forty-fifth day of the new month she said she was asking and had not known what to do with it, and asked for a line on a slate, and the line is four lines high.
- **Orla Specht, 61, of Silt Row** — eleven ounces under a floorboard and nine years of not spending a coin, and the reason, in seventy-one words, chosen in English so that the sentence would be hers and not his.
- **Ilsa Roke, 51, of the upper road** — the keeper of the first Wayhouse, Hettis Roke's aunt by marriage, on no roll, with a key, who wrote four householders on a chalk board in a yard the way this city does everything and washed it off in daylight after reading it out.
- **Darrel Quist, 37, Crown factor of the reed levy** — used the Crown's own fifth clause correctly, advanced a village's quarter against its own reeds, and entered that nobody asked him to and that he will be asked about it. Not a villain and not convicted of anything.
- **Emrys Vaile, 44, a chandler** — said the worst sentence anybody has said about work in this city and it was nine words, and it was not malice, and he paid on the third day and overpaid by a ninth and the ninth was refused.
- **Hessa Ruun** — read a Crown page in a reed channel and said the second half of the thing nobody says: when a thing is affordable the people who cannot afford it look like the people who do not care, and that is how a quarter of a Reach goes.
- **Ottavia Reek** — said the half of the truth that is bad for her and said it out loud and said she would like somebody to notice.
- **Kirsty Lund, 16** — a chalk-marker, a wage a chandler did not pay, and one of the two people in this city on no roll at all who has been asked nothing this volume.
- **Odile Ferrand, 38** — the other of the two, and the only person in the batch who said the finding about the review out loud before anybody drafted it: *the review is going to be argued about by people who were not in the room, and nobody thought of that on the thirty-eighth day of the new month.*

## Offstage but due

- **The review, in five days.** Day 75, the fiftieth day of the new month. Eleven questions, a second page, a slate, and four words on the back of the slate.
- **Nora Vale, 25, of Camden**, above a locksmith's, runs a rota on Tuesdays, Thursdays and Saturdays at the Camberwell Larder. **A letter is in a tin and it is not sent and there is no road.** *Open.*
- **Hattie Park, of Dunedin.** **Thirty days since a Crown channel was offered on the fortieth day of the flood, and Marrow has not mentioned it since, and Marrow has now asked for something.** The count is flood days from the fortieth and nothing else; **at day 75 it is thirty-five and at day 80 it is forty.** *Open.*
- **Ottoline Vask's two sentences** — the toll and the pattern, and a city cannot spend a thing and be its own foundation at the same time. Unanswered, not put in front of a room in this batch, and must not be resolved.
