**He offered, in nine sentences in a workroom on the twenty-ninth day of the Long Month, to pay four months of the wage of a person with a standing so that the question about a term could be put in the seventh column, and he said the reason out loud on purpose: he would like to stop being the only person in four hundred miles who pays people on a day.** He was refused in about nine minutes. He says he will make the same offer to about four other people in the next ten days and that every one of them will say the same thing and that in about four months the question will be put by somebody who was not paid, and that is the correct outcome and he is disappointed about it.

**He said on the thirtieth day of the Long Month, in a yard, that he is disappointed, and that there is nothing in it he can be argued out of.** He found out in a yard on the same day that he has been running a settlement on the word *season* for nine weeks and that a rota is seven days and nine days of a week is not a week. **He shut the store on a Sunday, the fortieth day of the Long Month, which is the only day a hundred and forty people have had in nine weeks, and he is going to be in a yard on it and not on a raft, and nobody sent for him and he was not asked.**

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf — on nothing for a hundred and ninety-four days

**She named the price of Jonah Pike's offer before anybody said yes or no, and the price is that about a hundred and forty people who are paid a wage on a day will find out in four months that the wage was on a list, and there is no instrument in four hundred miles that takes a person off a list they were not put on.** She said on the twentieth day of the Long Month that a date is the only thing anybody in this world has that travels on its own. She said on the thirty-fifth day of the Long Month, in about nine seconds, that a quarterly return is not a change and that a thing which is not a change is the most dangerous kind of thing there is. **She said on the thirty-ninth day of the Long Month that she will not put a question on a slate and will carry it in her hand eleven miles in the dark, and her slate has not been written on in nine years and it is still not explained.** She said on the thirty-eighth day of the Long Month: *then stop being the only one*, and nobody has worked out what that was for.

## Tamsin Quill — anchorwright, and the mutual public documented check

**She said her cost in six sentences in a room on the thirty-seventh day of the Long Month, and it is in a book in this city in her own words, and it is the first chapter in this volume that is about her and it is not a reconciliation and there is no apology in it.** She named the cost of refusing the thirty-second thing the wall has ever said on the sixty-fourth day of the month after this one, in about four seconds, in front of a man from an office, and the cost is that her name is on the front of a public register and a clerk will write down who read the compact. **She said the cost of the cost is that she has been the person who is right in a room for a hundred and ninety-two days and about four people in that room decided she would say something about the man she has been checking, and she refused, and a man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall then said out loud that she has never once been asked, and she would not be thanked for it and would not be talked at, and he did not say the next thing.** A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf entered that and underlined it and has not said why.

## Hessa Ruun — about fifty, a water-count of the Reed Reach, she counts water on the fingers of her left hand

**She came down the flats on the twenty-fifth day of the Long Month and stood at a gate for about an hour and said out loud that the Reach's record of who may use a water gate is a list of persons and every name on it is alive and it is the only list in four hundred miles that has never once been wrong about a person, and that she has been carrying one reason since the third day of the Long Month and has stopped waiting to find out, and she wrote nothing down and asked for it to be entered that she did not come to ask a household a question about a list.** She named her cost in four sentences on the thirty-seventh day of the Long Month: she is tired of being the only person in this city who keeps one, and about nine people have said that sentence and nobody has done anything about it, and she has done nothing about it.

## Bel Dain — about fifty-nine, who keeps a house and a boy of eight at Redroot

**She gave the boy of eight a day off on the Wednesday before the asking, in advance, without asking anybody, and said she did it because in about nine days there is going to be a great deal less of him in that house.** She went nine weeks without asking anybody where his cot came from and said on the twenty-fifth day of the Long Month that if anybody brings a second one she is going to say no, and that she would like it said in advance. She said in the room on the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month, in about four minutes, that in four months somebody can put her name in a column about this and she cannot stop it and neither can he. **She was asked in that room once this year and it was by mistake.**

## The boy of eight — and nobody improved on him

**He was told on the Monday that on Friday there would be people in the room and he was told the rest of it on the Thursday night by his mother, in a kitchen, alone, and he was not in the room on the Friday.** He has a length of rope he has had since the Sunday, he made eleven knots in nine feet of it in about ten hours, he rang a bell four times on a Saturday because it went off twice, he carried a stone to the windward side of about nine racks faster than a woman of about forty-four, and he got a blister and did not stop. **He asked whether the bell would still be there, and she told him the gate will be there in four months and the only thing that is different is whose turn it is, and he asked for the rope, and the chapter that says so is the one that does not make anything of him.**

## A girl of seventeen — on no roll of anything, and a slate of her own and a plain one

**She stood in a yard at Redroot from the ninth hour to the eleventh hour on the Friday morning and did not go in, and then walked eleven miles and answered three questions in writing that were given to her four days beforehand.** She read a four-sentence question out in the local language on the thirty-ninth day of the Long Month and read it back once for the people at the back from a plain slate and not from her own, because her own has her name at the top of it. **She said the count of silences — ninety, ninety-four, ninety-five — out loud in a store, a room and a room, three times in sixteen days, and said that nobody checks hers.** She put a second figure under a hundred and forty on a wall outside a store on the thirtieth day of the Long Month and said the same four words to nine people: *somebody had to*.

## A woman of about twenty-four — on no roll of anything, the first public record in this city since the second day of the flood

**She put a hundred and forty-one under a hundred and forty on a wall and has not explained it. She said no, in a yard, in daylight, in front of about two hundred and forty who could hear, on the thirty-first day of the Long Month, to writing down the split in the coalition of the seventeen, and it is the first thing she has chosen not to write down because she wanted to.** She named her cost last in a room on the thirty-seventh day of the Long Month: she is the only person in this city who can be checked, and about nine people have now said a cost out loud to her and she does not know whether that is the beginning of the end of the book or the end of the aloneness.

## A woman of about twenty-nine — who keeps a public register

**She said on the thirty-eighth day of the Long Month that a meeting-ground is a number with legs and that a question is better than a delegation and is the first instrument in four hundred miles better than one, and that she did not build it and is not going to say that out loud twice in a year.** She said in a room on the thirty-seventh day of the Long Month that four people and a question have not left for the First Landing, that nobody has been asked whether they will go, and that *to open nothing* is her sentence and she wrote it, and that in about four months somebody is going to say a delegation was sent to open something and the cost of that is that she cannot stop it without making a speech about a person, and a speech about somebody is a thing she does not do.

## A man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall — a slate he writes on when he is told not to

**He said ninety days in a market on the twentieth day of the Long Month and said he had stopped saying the number because the number does not do anything. He asked a man barred from every roster to go and stand in a place, which is free. He said the sentence of the second month of this flood that refused the settlement's offer: a man of thirty-two is not going to let a thing be bought that is a question. He said *then write it and do not read it out*, and *you have never once been asked*, and *somebody had better write it down before a clerk somewhere works out that a word is a date*.**

## A man of about forty-four who keeps a stall — he keeps the cost column in his own book for four years

**He said the cost column out loud in a room on the thirty-seventh day of the Long Month: eleven entries in one month, no figure on the end of any of them, no twelfth, one field paid on the thirteenth day of the Long Month and the column did not move, and that is twenty-four days ago, and the cost is that he has been a column for four years for nobody.** *The first write of this line said twenty-eight; the repair pass recomputed it from the anchor, day 192 less day 168.* He said eight words in a workroom on the thirty-third day of the Long Month: *he is going to stand at the back*, and about nine people wrote it down and about four have not been told.

## A man of about thirty-nine who trades on the Open Hand board — he does not name a gate, a household or a person

**He walked the causeway nine times in a hundred and forty-odd days and found that a gate is not owned and a road is not a gate, and that a question has to go somewhere.** He said in a yard on the thirty-fourth day of the Long Month: *a thing that was going to arrive on a day and did not is a different kind of information from a thing that arrives.* He said on a road on the thirty-fifth day of the Long Month that it came in a cart that was going somewhere else. He said *I do not know. And I cannot check that either* on the thirty-sixth day of the Long Month. **He has been refused by name four times in four years, he stood on a quay for four days in case anything came and what came was salt, and he has said the answer to that about four times in four years and is not going to say it a fifth in a better place.**

## A man of about twenty-nine from the settlement's workroom — he is a MAN and he must not become a woman

**He read a second workway's whole cost out on the twenty-first day of the Long Month and said in that kitchen, before anybody asked him, that he could not tell which of five rooms he had left a sentence out in.** He said the thing nobody in that workroom had thought of on the thirty-eighth day of the Long Month: a person who is findable is a person who can be sent and a person who can be sent is a person who cannot go, and it is one fact written twice. He said a rota is seven days and nine days of a week is not a week. **He still has a book of his own that goes nowhere and he has entered every one of these in it.**

## A man of about thirty-one with a hand that will not close

**He is the only person in a room who says *I do not know* out loud without being asked. He washed the sentence about a person being moved off ground off the back of his own hand with a bucket and a rag and did not wash it off the page, and walked past a woman of about thirty-four at the seventh hour of the evening on the twenty-fifth day of the Long Month and said nine words to nobody: *I do not know whether that is good. I do not know.***

## A man of about thirty-nine and he digs

**He did not take the workway on the twenty-first day of the Long Month and nobody asked him why and he did not say, and two people in that kitchen wanted him to.** He said where the silt goes at the bottom of a causeway on the thirty-second day of the Long Month and then said a second sentence, and nine measures of salt went into the water and a figure of nine hundred and nine went on a page and both numbers were right.

## A woman of about thirty-four from a fourth household on the road

**She took a workway on the twenty-first day of the Long Month with the whole cost read out first and her reason was three sentences long and all three were about a roof, a wage and a Tuesday.** She waited four days with a printed sheet she had not opened so that a person would tell her about it instead of a piece of paper. She said her cost out loud on the thirty-seventh day of the Long Month and said that knowing about the sentence a man of twenty-nine had shortened would not have made her say no, and said so in front of about nine people.

## A woman of about thirty-four in a store on the upper road — she is not on any list in it

**She asked what the date is, in four words, at about the seventh hour of the evening on the thirtieth day of the Long Month, and a man of about forty-two found out he had been running a settlement on a word for nine weeks.** She is not a title, not a rank, not an office and she has not been named.

## The woman of forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms

**Not asked, not approached, not named in any room where it could reach her, and nobody stood outside the fourth house. She is not in this batch at all.** The rule of the Reach has a hole in it shaped exactly like her and Chapter 0167 found the hole and did not fill it, and on the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month one person in a kitchen in Redroot asked for it to be entered that the hole was not filled on that morning and that nobody had gone near it, and it is on the page and nothing else happened.

## Owen Park, Ivenn Marrow, Wenna Cray, Renna Ord, the four readers, the box, the Narrow Mark, the chapel stone, the boards, the compact of eleven lines, the provisional recognition, the seat of holder of the pull, the delegation, the trestle count, the two words in about nine rooms, the twenty-eight words on a slate, the eight names at the foot of a hundred-and-forty-year-old page, and the twenty-fifth reading of the join-line

**None of them moved in these twenty days, and none of them is mentioned.** The word *knock* is at zero and stays at zero for the volume. The box is shut with its strap untied and the writing round the edge of the wax unread. The room at the far end of the Narrow Mark is unlooked at, as in the five batches before it. The measurement column stands at twenty-four readings with one ruled line left. The cost column stands at eleven entries with no figure on the end of any of them and no twelfth. **The word *order* is Jonah Pike's and Renna Ord's and neither of them has ever heard of the other, and no chapter in this batch put them in a room.**

# Volume 04, Batch 0003 — the last seven days of Volume 04

**Every entry below is on a page in Chapters 0191 to 0200. The volume's close record is `state/volume-04-close.md` and the batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-04-batch-0003.md`.**

## Adrian Vale

**He carried a question in four sentences eleven miles up the Reach road in the dark, in a woman's hand, because a woman of about thirty-four would not put it on a slate, and he was not on the flat when it was read out.** He stood on a bank eleven miles above the Redroot cut on the forty-first day of the Long Month at about the fourth hour of the afternoon and did not come down to it, and a man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall did not come and get him and he did not ask him to. **He was asked on the forty-third day of the Long Month whether a Wednesday suited him and said no**, and gave the reason in nine sentences in a book in this city in his own hand with the date on it: a person who is findable is a place a person can be found in, and it has been true since the thirty-sixth of the Long Month, and that morning it cost him a room and a number, and he will never know who checked it. **He is Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing. He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, no chapter offered him a workway and he asked for none.** A share with the post taken out has been in a coat for thirty-two days and is unanswered — given on the thirteenth day of the Long Month, which is day 168, and about twenty-five days of it are on a page in Chapter 0189 on day 193.

## The keeper of the Redroot gate

**She gave the days back on the forty-first day of the Long Month, in daylight, on a flat, with about nine people on it, and nobody asked her.** She spoke first, unasked, and gave all nine days of a week back for the rest of the season, starting then, and said that nobody was giving her anything and nothing was arriving with her name on it, and that she has lost the days and still has the cut. **She is seventeen days into a year of being angry and the year is not over, and she is not going to be argued out of the rest of it by anybody in this city including anybody standing on a bank.** She put a date in chalk on the rail in a fifth hand at about the eighth hour of the evening and the twenty-third and the twenty-fourth are still under it. **She was not in the compact's room on the forty-fifth and nobody went and fetch her and that is entered as a failure and not called anything else.** She has not said she is glad, she has not said she would say it again, she has not been rescued, and **the phrase *the whole of her* is not used about her anywhere in the volume.**

## Bel Dain

**A woman of about fifty-nine who keeps the house at the bottom of the Redroot cut said four sentences on the Monday and they are the cost nobody else in the volume can pay: she is the one who has to live in that house for the next year, and she has not asked the keeper about anything for seventeen days and has asked her about something else every day for seventeen days, and she asked for it to be entered that she said the word *room* and did not say the word *kitchen* once that week.** She gave the boy of eight a day off in advance in the week of the asking without being asked, and on the fortieth of the Long Month he was not on the flat because nobody sent him anywhere.

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf

**She carried the question eleven miles in her hand because she would not put it on a slate, stood on a bank at the ninth hour of a Saturday night and would not put her hand on the keeper's door, and handed four sentences to a water-count's wet left hand because a question has to go somewhere and a slate was the wrong shape for a walk.** She named the price before she said yes on the forty-fourth day of the Long Month, in her own words: for nine years nobody in this city has known what she looks like while she is arguing, and from that day there is a date and a stall and a name on a page, and anybody with a copy can wait at that wharf and she will be the one who comes out. **She asked for it to be entered that she said no for nine years and that this is the yes and that the two are the same person and that she is not a different woman today, and she asked in advance not to be thanked, and nobody thanked her.** On the forty-fifth she had been on nothing for two hundred days, said so first before anybody asked her anything, read eleven lines out in the local language in a room in daylight, and her name is on the front of the compact and is said once in that room and **is deliberately not printed on any page, and §8 of the batch record is binding on every writer after this one.** She has a slate she has carried for nine years and has never written on, put it on the edge of a table in that room and did not write on it, and it is not explained anywhere and is not going to be.

## Jonah Pike

**He lost the export on the Monday night, kept the queue, and put a share in the compact on the same posting as every other share, and he said nine sentences about it on the forty-fourth day of the Long Month in a room with a door, of which eight are the best arguments anybody in this city has heard and the ninth is the word *order*.** He said in the same nine sentences that he is not going to tell the room what he thinks of it and that a man who is wrong out loud is worth more than a man who is right in a yard. He has two weeks of a wage in a chest and said in a yard on the forty-second that after two weeks he does not know, and he said at the front of his own queue on the forty-fifth, in daylight, with a date on the door, that it is longer than the cost of a workway and he is not going to shorten it. **He is a serious rival, is not defeated, is not a villain, and does not leave. At about the sixth hour of the evening on the forty-fifth he is standing at the back of his own queue with his hands in his pockets and nobody turns him out.** He is disappointed, not angry, and the reason is that there is nothing in him anybody can be argued out of, and nobody has thanked him and he said in advance he would not be.

## Renna Ord

**A woman of fifty-eight who cannot read and who reads a join by standing on it twice a day was at the back of the room on the forty-fourth day of the Long Month when a man of forty-two said the word *order*, and she said nothing, and about four people in that room knew the word was hers.** He said in nine sentences that he has known it since the sixth week of this flood and has never heard anybody else use it, and he does not know where he met it. **They have now been in a room twice — Chapter 0169 and Chapter 0198 — and have found out nothing either time, and about nine people in this city have stopped saying it out loud.** She reads nothing and she is one of four readers and no fifth may be named.

## Ivenn Marrow

**He read a number out in a room over a yard on the forty-third day of the Long Month, in daylight, with a date on the door, in his own hand, and it is one hundred and fifty-eight, and it is counted one a day from the fortieth day of this flood and from nothing else.** He does not know what a day of it means, has never pretended to, and has never been asked in four hundred days. **He is neither convicted nor made useless and he is publicly useful, and there is one other line in that book and it is not a number and he has still not mentioned it and nobody in that room asked him about it.** He is neither findable nor a place a person can be found in and has never been either. He is a man of about forty-one and is not a man of forty-two.

## Hessa Ruun

**She stood on a bank at the bottom of the Redroot cut from about the sixth hour of a Saturday evening because she knew a question was coming up that road, took four sentences on the flat of her left hand, and read them out on a flat on the Sunday morning.** She said the rule has no name on it and she is not going to give it one and she is not going to say who built it, which she has said four times in nine years. She entered the days back against the household in a record she has kept for nine years in which every name is alive, said it is a list, and said she is not going to stand on a flat and be the person with the list. She has been tired of being the only person in this city who keeps one for about a hundred and forty-five days and is no more tired than she was.

## The man of about thirty-nine who trades on the Open Hand board

**He said in a room on the forty-fourth day of the Long Month that a person who is not findable cannot be checked and a person who is findable is a place to be found in and that Marrow is neither and has never been either, and he said the raft's refusal to name a household was right for his reason and not the raft's.** He read the fifth line of the compact out again in his own words and then read the ninth line out and said nothing about it, and he has refused by name four times in four years and this is the first sentence of his that has gone into a page with his name on it, and it does not work, and he said so before it went in.

## A man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall

**He wrote the wage page, he said a wage that stops is not a favour that stops, he said a cost read out five times gets shorter and the reader is the one who shortens it, he said nobody has made a list, he did not go and fetch the keeper twice, and on the forty-fifth he said the whole of a hundred and forty-five days of cost at the front of his own queue in daylight with a date on the door and did not shorten it.** He is the person who wrote the nine sentences that became a posting, and the only line anybody in that room loses something by is one he wrote and put his hand to.

## A man of about twenty-nine from the settlement's workroom

**He copied one hundred and fifty-eight onto a wall in chalk in the room it was read in and said it was the first time anything of his had left him and gone somewhere somebody else could stand in front of it, and he still has a book of his own that goes nowhere with about nine entries in it.** He has not been a post and has not been a roster and nobody has offered him anything.

## The boy of eight

**He rang the bell twice on the forty-fifth day of the Long Month and fixed it once without being told how, and about nine adults on that flat have not said anything about him since the twenty-fourth and about four of them have not been able to stop looking at the rail.** Nobody in fifty chapters improved on him.

## Everyone else, in one line each

**A woman of about twenty-four who is on no roll of anything** wrote the three sentences about findability down separately and did not join them up, and wrote the name at the top of a copy of the compact and did not read it out, and said she was the only person in this city who could have stopped that happening and did not. **A woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register** said five sentences distinguishing the compact of eleven lines from this one, for the fourth time, and would not improve on them, and said the delegation has not left and *to open nothing* is her sentence. **A girl of seventeen on no roll of anything** read eleven lines back twice from a plain slate and not from her own, admitted she read the first one too fast, and refused to say her own count of silences in a room where another number had just been read out. **A man of about thirty-four from a fourth household on the road** counted about four hundred and forty people on the flats in a yard and said nine of them are on his rota and the rest have never been on it. **A man of about fifty-two who digs** said the channel will silt and that he cannot say what day the water comes back. **A man of about forty-four who keeps a stall** recited his cost column for the first time in a room and said it is the same thing as a number on a wall and that he cannot make himself say so. **A man of about thirty-one with a hand that will not close** said it went and he paid the same. **A man of about forty-one in a very good coat** was told a figure nine hundred miles away that he did not ask for and is not going to be told either of the two sentences in a market.

## The woman of forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms

**Not asked, not approached, not named, and nobody stood outside the fourth house. She is not in these ten chapters at all.** The hole in the Reach's own rule that is shaped exactly like her was not filled on the forty-first day of the Long Month and was not filled on any of the seven days in this batch.

## Owen Park, the box, the wax, the eight names, the six rooms, the Narrow Mark, the chapel stone, the boards, the twenty-fifth reading, the provisional recognition, the seat of holder of the pull, the cost column, the delegation, and the trestle count's other line

**None of them moved in these seven days and none of them is mentioned, except the trestle count, which is the volume's new question and is on a page.** The word *knock* is at zero and stays at zero for the volume. The box is shut. The room at the far end of the Narrow Mark is unlooked at, in the seventh batch. The measurement column stands at twenty-four readings with one ruled line left. The cost column stands at eleven entries with no figure on the end of any of them and no twelfth, and it was recited once in the whole batch. **The word *order* is Jonah Pike's and Renna Ord's, they have been in a room twice, and neither of them found out either time.**

---

# Volume 05, Batch 0001 — Chapter 0210 boundary, days 201 to 210

**Everything in this block is on a page in Chapters 0201 to 0210. `outline/volume-05.md` does not exist and this batch did not decide what Volume 05 is. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-05-batch-0001.md` and the new rules are `bible/power-system.md` §27. The ten chapters have been through one review repair pass, `reviews/volume-05/batch-0001.md`, and §16 of the batch record is that pass. Three things in this block were established or corrected by it and no character moved: the recorder is a woman and is named as one everywhere including the sentence in Chapter 0201 that had called her a man; the page Adrian Vale's name is on is the compact and is on no wall, and the posting on the wall has not one name on it; and the slate on the store's chest was written and hung on the Saturday morning of the forty-sixth day of the Long Month, day 201.**

## Adrian Vale

**He is Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing, and he is on a page for the first time in two volumes: he said his own name out loud, once, in a room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, in the local language, on the fifty-fourth day of the Long Month, day 209, a Sunday, at about the eighth hour of the morning, in front of about nine people and about two hundred and forty who could hear.** A woman of about twenty-four had her hand on a slate and did not put it down. Nobody wrote it down and about nine people in that hall will say it if anybody asks them. He was fetched by that name at about the seventh hour of the morning that day by a woman who was not sent by anybody, and he answered to it in about a second and has entered that he answered to it quickly, and he walked nine miles on foot to the room because there was nothing to fetch him in. **He could not answer the question he was fetched for, which was whether the ninth line works, and he said so in four sentences and would not improve on them, and about four people in that room wanted a better sentence and about nine of them did not.** He said the sentence he has said since the thirty-sixth day of the Long Month — a person who is findable is a place a person can be found in, being findable is the price of being real and not the penalty for it, and a person who is findable cannot be sent to a gate — and said that what he did was the more expensive of the two instruments because nobody can take a word out of a room. He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark was not opened a fourth time and was not looked at, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, **nobody in this batch offered him a wage, a post, a seat, a roster, a column or a shift and he asked for none**, and a man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall asked for that to go in the record and not be said out loud again. A share with the post taken out has been in a coat for forty-one days and has not been answered. He said in that room that he is not going to be in a room in daylight where somebody asks him about a gate, and nobody did.

## Ivenn Marrow, about forty-one

**He wrote one line in his book on the fiftieth day of the Long Month, day 205, a Wednesday, because he writes one a week, and the number is one hundred and sixty-five, counted one a day from the fortieth day of this flood and from nothing else.** The wall in that room has one hundred and fifty-eight on it with the forty-third day of that month at the top of the column, and the two figures are seven days apart and there are seven in the difference, and the wall is correct for the forty-third and is not wrong and there is no column anywhere for the difference. He was asked what a day of it is and said *I do not know* and said that he has not been asked anything new and that they had asked him the same question with the same face. **The line in that book that is not a number is still a letter to a person in another hemisphere, it has not been sent, it is in no book, and no office in nine hundred miles knows it exists; he turned past it with a finger while a woman of about twenty-four watched, and she did not ask, and it is the second time that page has been seen and not asked about, and he has still not mentioned it.** He is publicly useful and unaccused, he is neither convicted nor made useless, and neither of his two words was guessed aloud by anybody and he has not explained either of them and nobody has asked him to. He said a refusal of the number is not possible for him to give and would be the same five words.

## The keeper of the Redroot gate, about thirty-eight

**She is thirty days into a year of being angry at the end of this batch and the year is not over.** On the forty-eighth day of the Long Month, day 203, a Monday, about nine people walked eleven miles up the road with a reason and a date, and a woman of about fifty-nine who keeps that house came out onto the step and said the day came and that nobody was going, and gave two reasons, the second of which is that a child sent up that bank with a message would make everything the keeper does for a year a thing a household is watching. **Nobody went and about four people in that road have entered that nobody is going to.** She came down to the cut at about the ninth hour of the morning and worked the channel beside about four people from the store she had said yes to the day before, with the board in her left hand, and did not put it down once, and did not say one word to any of them about the forty-eighth day of the Long Month, or about the twenty-fourth, or about a year. She was not rescued, was not argued with, was not given a reason to have done otherwise, has not said she was glad and has not said she would say it again, and **the phrase *the whole of her* was used about her zero times in these ten chapters.** A man of about thirty-two was on the bank above the cut that Monday and did not come down to it.

## Jonah Pike, forty-two

**He is a serious rival, he is not defeated, he does not leave and he is not a villain.** His share is the ninth line of a posting he did not write and it is not above anybody's. He paid the wage on the forty-ninth day of the Long Month out of a chest with a week in it and said in a yard that the wage went out at six and does not matter what line it is on, and asked for that to be said in a yard on a Tuesday morning because in about four months somebody is going to say he put a queue at the bottom of a document, and he did not, and the man who did is standing about nine feet from him. On the fifty-fifth day of the Long Month he stood at the back of his own yard for the third time in three days and said he is going to stop saying out loud that he is not going anywhere, because a thing you keep saying out loud stops being a thing anybody can be angry at, and said that today he was careful for the first time and does not like it. He said the queue is the only thing in the last nine days that has not failed.

## A man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall

**He put the store and its queue at the bottom of the posting on purpose, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, and he has not taken it off the wall in nine days and said in a yard that he is not going to.** He is the man who cannot say what a sack and a place at a fire and a day of the queue are worth, and who said in a room that a posting is a page about labour and a page about labour is not a wage and that about nine of his own sentences have been tested by a wall and the wall has not failed once and nobody has been paid because of it. He was fetched to a room over a market on the fifty-second day of the Long Month with a slip of his own line with a date cut into the corner of it, and told about four people that nobody sent him. He said a hundred and nineteen days is what the thirty-nine men under the near gate have gone unpaid on the forty-ninth, and a hundred and twenty-five on the fifty-fifth, and refused to go and tell them, and gave the same reason as the two times before. He said a refusal that cannot be administered cannot be recorded or counted, and that a right of refusal which cannot be bought out cannot be afforded, and he found the second hole on the day the man of about thirty-nine used the right. He asked for it to be entered that nobody in nineteen days has offered Adrian Vale anything and that nobody should say it out loud again.

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf

**She has been on a page for nine days and on the fifty-third day of the Long Month a man stood in front of her stall with a copy and a second man came with nothing in his hands, and she said in her own words that the price she named on the forty-fourth was being findable while she is arguing and that what she has is being findable on a Saturday morning with nothing to argue about, and that about four people telling about four other people in about eight days is the half with no page and no office nine hundred miles away that can stop it.** She put the slate she has carried for nine years and has never written on down on the boards of her own stall for about a minute in front of about nine people and picked it up again and did not write on it. Her name is on the front of the compact and is not printed in this manuscript; **it was not read out in a room on the fifty-second day of the Long Month, a woman of about twenty-four put her hand flat on that line and read the second name instead, and no chapter of this batch printed it, supplied it, or had a person in a room say it.** She was not thanked and asked in advance not to be thanked and was not thanked. Her slate is not explained and is not going to be explained.

## A woman of about fifty-nine who keeps a house and a boy of eight at the bottom of the Redroot cut

**She is the person who has to be in that house for the rest of a year and she said so in her own words on her own step on the forty-eighth day of the Long Month, and it is a cost and not a consolation and it may not be used to soften anything.** She said the date arrived and that she is not going and is not going to send the boy, and she raised the sack: one came on the twenty-fifth of the Long Month and one is due, the days went back on the forty-first, and nobody has worked out whether a thing given in exchange for days given back is a thing that still comes, and she will not ask because asking is a person saying out loud that she does not trust a man and about four hundred and forty people on the flats would hear about it inside four days. A woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register wrote it on a second slate and read it back once.

## A man of about thirty-nine who trades on the Open Hand board

**He said no to a line on the posting on the fifty-first day of the Long Month, in a yard, in daylight, in front of about nine people, and did not give a reason and did not say which line, and nobody asked him why and it is entered in three places that nobody wanted to.** He has been refused by name four times in four years and has never been in a room where a sentence of his went into a page that did not have his name on it until the Friday of the forty-fifth, and his name and the date are under his own objection in the compact. He said in a yard on the forty-sixth that he would rather have a page with a mistake in it than a wall of good sentences, and on the fifty-first he made one, and said that is the first time in four years that saying no has been the whole of what he had to do. He is the only person in this city who knows how the fourth line is administered and he has said in writing that it cannot be.

## A girl of seventeen who is on no roll of anything

**She is the only one of the nine people in the room on the fifty-second day of the Long Month who is not on the front of a page, and it is because she was careful, and she said in four sentences that it cost her nothing at all, and that being unfindable is a thing she did on purpose and being findable is a thing that was done to somebody else and that neither of those is fair.** Her own slate has her name at the top of it and she read the compact out of a plain slate. She keeps the count of this city's silences and refused to give it in the room on the fiftieth day of the Long Month where a different number had just been written on a wall, and gave a new reason, which is that both counts go up one a day and that about four people have added them together and about nine have taken one from the other and not one of those figures is written in either book. She also said that she would like to be asked, once, by somebody, in a room, on a day somebody chose, whether she is all right, and nobody in that room asked her.

## A woman of about twenty-four who is on no roll of anything

**She fetched Adrian Vale by a name in a yard on the fifty-fourth day of the Long Month and says nobody sent her, and she is the person who has kept the record since the second day of this flood and has never once been on one.** She put her hand flat on the first line of a copy of the compact in a room on the fifty-second and did not read it and read the second name out loud and stopped there. She picked a piece of chalk off the ledge under the posting on the fifty-first day of the Long Month and put it back down because a mark is a record and a record is administration. She wrote the order of a queue at the sixth hour and again at the seventh on the forty-ninth and the two lists are not the same list and she has both. On the fifty-third she said out loud in a yard, at about nine feet from a woman who had been on a page for eight days, that the rule of the Reach has a hole in it shaped exactly like a household, that she has not gone near it and nobody in that yard is going to, and asked for somebody to write down that a hole and a wharf were in the same yard and that nobody joined them up.

## A woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register

**Four people and a question have not left for the ground people call the First Landing, nobody has been asked whether they will go, and the only instruction it has is *to open nothing*, and that is her sentence.** She said it in a room on the fifty-second day of the Long Month for the second time in a room and on the fifty-fifth in a yard, and said she is not going to say it a fourth time because about nine people in this city can now say it. On the fifty-fifth she said she had started to write down who the four of them are, got two names in, stopped, tore the page out, and it is in her own pocket and not in the book, and the reason is that a list of four people who have not been asked is a roll.

## Everyone else, in one line each

**A man of about twenty-nine from the settlement's workroom** said a page with a date on it and a price under every line and no figure under any price is a list of intentions, and a man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall told him it was the price, and neither of them has improved on the other. **A man of about forty-four who keeps a stall** copied the front of the compact onto the back of a slate on a market table on the afternoon of the forty-fifth because he thought a page ought to be in two places, put it on the boards of a stall by a wharf on the fifty-third, said he would rather be laughed at than be a man who has carried a piece of paper about for nine days, and said a cost with no figure on the end of it is a cost and a price with a figure is a promise. **A woman of about thirty-four from a fourth household on the road** gave two numbers about the flats in that order and unjoined, asked how she gets on the posting and was asked whose household she is, and said the only question anybody has asked her she can answer is which side of a channel is whose. **A man of about fifty-two who digs** said six days and then fourteen days, and a rod that goes in further every time and not much, and that he has stopped measuring it because about nine people who are not him have started, and that he can date a bank and cannot date a number. **A woman of about thirty-four in the store on the upper road, who is on no list in it** hung a slate with the fifty-sixth day of the Long Month on the chest on the forty-sixth so that nobody would have to ask, and said the date out loud four times to about four different people on the fifty-fifth. **A woman of about fifty who counts water on the fingers of her left hand** read a whole page with her fingers on it and then said the man who wrote it had put the settlement's own sentence on a wall, and asked that it be entered that she was asked a question in a yard and did not answer it. **A man of about thirty-one with a hand that will not close** read the posting aloud with his finger and got to the ninth line, and said two things have happened on this cut in six days and about nine people in this city have only been able to talk about the one that does not cost anybody anything this week, and on the fifty-fourth he said he did not know whether a name said out loud in a room is good or bad. **A woman of about fifty-six who keeps a shop four streets down from a market** said that about four people in this city have already worked out which road the seventh line is about and have told each other, and that if a person wants to know they can stand at the end of it on a Friday morning in daylight and say so.

## The woman of forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms

**Not asked, not approached, not named, and nobody stood outside the fourth house. She is not in these ten chapters at all.** The hole in the Reach's own rule that is shaped exactly like her was said out loud in a yard on the fifty-third day of the Long Month by a woman of about twenty-four who said she has not gone near it and that nobody in that yard is going to, and the hole is not filled.

## Owen Park, the box, the wax, the eight names, the six rooms, the Narrow Mark, the chapel stone, the boards, the twenty-fifth reading, the provisional recognition, the seat of holder of the pull, the compact of eleven lines, the woman of twenty-nine's four sentences

**None of them moved in these ten days and none of them is mentioned, except the trestle count, which went from one hundred and fifty-eight to one hundred and sixty-five and is on a wall.** The word *knock* is at zero in every chapter from Chapter 0091 to Chapter 0210 and **is not at zero in this manuscript: one hundred and sixteen uses in thirty-four files, seven in Volume 01 and a hundred and nine in Volume 02, the last of them in Chapter 0090. No state file may say it is.** The room at the far end of the Narrow Mark is unlooked at, in the eighth batch, and this batch did not go and look.

## What Chapters 0211 to 0220 added to this cast, and only what is on a page

**Volume 05, Batch 0002. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-05-batch-0002.md` and the rules are `bible/power-system.md` §28. One new person is established and he is established in a mouth, in daylight, on a named day, by his age and his trade and by what he has been doing since the seventh week of this flood. Nobody in these ten chapters is named except Adrian Vale, who is named once, and the house form of this volume is still descriptor and not name.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THE STORE'S BOARD, AND THE HANDLE IS THE BOARD, THE TROUGH AND THE WET CLOTH.** He writes the day's figures on it, the board carries the day before's figures and is written at the seventh hour when he knows what the day was, it hangs about a foot to the left of the posting on the same wall, and about nine people in that yard read the board and about four read the posting. He has been at the front of that queue since the seventh week of this flood, is on no roster of anything including that store, and nobody has ever asked him to do it and nobody has ever paid him. **He said out loud on the sixty-third day of the Long Month that he washes it because the people who cannot read are the ones a figure on a wall is for, and that he had never said it out loud to anybody and he set himself the question and answered it in a yard on a Tuesday, and about four people in that yard wanted him to write the difference between two Tuesdays and about nine of them did not.** *Established on a page, and the device produced no phantom, and a later writer may not give him a name in a room with a date on it or not at all.*
- **A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY WHO CARRIES SACKS, AND THE HANDLE IS A STRAP ACROSS HIS SHOULDER AND A COUNT HE KEEPS BECAUSE IT IS HIS TRADE.** He has carried four sacks up the bank eleven miles from this city to a yard at the bottom of the Redroot cut, put them down in the yard and never gone up to the door, and nobody in that house has ever asked him where one came from and he has never asked. He said that he counted them because that is the trade and not because it is a question, and asked for that to be written in those words and not in better ones, on the ground that if it gets written as him being careful about it then he is a person with a secret instead of a man with a strap. **He said he will not be there for the next one.** *Established on a page, and he is a new person and he is not a message and no chapter sent him with one.*
- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR IN A STORE ON THE UPPER ROAD TURNED A SLATE FACE DOWN, AND THE HANDLE IS THE SLATE AND THE CHEST.** She hung it on the chest on the forty-sixth day of the Long Month so that nobody would have to ask, said the date out loud in a yard five times in ten days, and turned it over on the sixty-fourth day of that month, when the date on it was eight days old. **She gave the reason in a yard in front of about four people and it is that a slate with a day in the past on it is a thing about the past and there is no next date and she is not going to write one.** She had been on nothing for two hundred days before the forty-fifth day of the Long Month and had asked what the date was, in four words, at the seventh hour of the evening on the thirtieth day of that month, and was told nothing, and on the sixty-fourth she said that out loud to a woman of about twenty-four who had her hand on a slate, and said no when she was asked whether to write it down, and the woman of about twenty-four wrote it down anyway and neither of them said the word refusal. **She is still a person somebody can ask, and about four people who had walked nine miles to read the date were told it by her after the slate went over, and that is a different thing and it is not on a slate.** *Open. A later writer may not put the slate back up and may not write a new date on a slate.*
- **A MAN OF FORTY-TWO OPENED THE SETTLEMENT'S CHEST IN A YARD IN DAYLIGHT ON THE FIFTY-SIXTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, AND THE HANDLE IS HIS HANDS IN HIS POCKETS AND THE BACK OF HIS OWN QUEUE.** He said a hundred and twenty-six days once, in a yard, in front of about nine people, attached to the decision that nobody there is going to tell the thirty-nine men under the near gate of the Lock of Salt, and then he carried the chest out of the back of the store himself and put it down in the middle of the yard and there was salt in it. **He said he did not know that until the lid was off, and that he chose a yard over a back room on purpose, and that he cannot give a good reason and the reason he has is that the chest is the settlement's and not his and he cannot do that with salt.** Nobody in that yard asked him what was going to happen and about four of them had decided before the lid was off that they were not going to ask him that in a yard. He is not defeated, he does not leave, he is not a villain, and **on the sixty-third day of the Long Month he said one thing he will do instead of the thing he wants said, and that he worked it out on the fifty-fifth day of that month and said nothing about it for eight days.** *Open, and a wage in salt and what is in the chest now is a thing no chapter has decided.*
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL BY A WHARF CORRECTED A RUMOUR IN A YARD IN DAYLIGHT ON THE SIXTY-FIFTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH WITHOUT SAYING HER NAME, AND THAT IS THE FIRST TIME IN THREE WEEKS SHE HAS SAID ANYTHING IN A YARD THAT ANYBODY COULD HOLD HER TO.** She has been on a page since the forty-fifth day of the Long Month, which is twenty days, and two men had stood in front of that stall on the fifty-third day of that month and a third came on the sixty-fifth with nothing in his hands and the sentence wrong in one word. **She said in four sentences that there is no wall in this city with her name on it and no page anybody can walk to, that a copy is on the back of a slate in more hands than one, and that a word has been in more places than the copy for about eleven days, and that the wall is the one thing neither of them has ever been in.** Nobody thanked her and she had asked in advance not to be thanked. **Her name is on the front of the compact, it is on a page, it is on no page you can read and it is on no wall, and it was not printed, not supplied, and not said aloud by anybody in a room or in a yard in these ten chapters, and the correction was made without it.** *Active and it is the batch's engine and it is not resolved.*
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON THE OPEN HAND BOARD SAID THE OTHER HALF OF HIS OWN SENTENCE IN A YARD BY A WHARF ON THE SIXTY-FIFTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH AND SAID HE WAS NOT GOING TO DO ANYTHING ABOUT IT.** He said seventy-two days out loud in a market on the sixty-second day of that month, which is the first figure anybody has put on the delegation of four people and a question, and attached a decision to it, which is that he will not say it again and will not add to it and will not correct it. He said in a yard on the sixty-third day of that month, in a market in this city, that the ninth line is the only line of the posting anybody can check and it is the only line that was not about anybody's labour. **He is a serious rival and Volume 05 may not defeat him and he may not become a villain, and he is not defeated and he is not a villain, and what he is is a man who has said out loud that there is nothing in him anybody can be argued out of and has then done nothing.**
- **ADRIAN VALE WALKED NINE MILES INTO A YARD ON THE SIXTY-FOURTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH AND WAS NOT FETCHED.** He came up that road on foot in Chapter 0211, read the figures off the board, said *nine*, did not stand in the queue, and went back down nine miles. In Chapter 0219 he walked nine miles into a yard where about nine people could have said his name, none of them did, he did not say it himself, he stood about nine feet from a chest for about nine minutes, he was asked nothing about a gate and nothing else, and he was offered nothing, and he said he came because he wanted to see the other half of it, and the other half of it is a woman turning a slate over. **He remains Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing. He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark was not opened a fourth time and was not looked at, in the ninth batch, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, no chapter offered him a workway and he asked for none, no chapter turned the name he did not choose into a qualification and no chapter asked him about a gate.** A share with the post taken out is fifty-one days in a coat and is unanswered, and he said on the sixty-fourth day of the Long Month that he is not going to start on a Wednesday because a woman has turned a slate over. *Active, and about nine people can still fetch him and this was the first ten days in which nobody has.*
- **THE KEEPER OF THE REDROOT GATE IS FORTY-ONE DAYS INTO A YEAR OF BEING ANGRY AT THE END OF BATCH 0002 AND THIRTY-SEVEN DAYS INTO IT ON THE SIXTY-FIRST DAY OF THAT MONTH, AND THE YEAR IS NOT OVER, AND ON THE DAY SHE NAMED NOBODY WENT AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.** She was at the edge of a yard at the bottom of the Redroot cut at about the fourth hour of the afternoon and did not come into it, and said one sentence about a sack and it was where to put it. **She is not rescued, is not argued with, is not given a reason to have done otherwise, has not said she is glad, has not said she would say it again, and the phrase *the whole of her* was used about her zero times in these ten chapters and does not occur in any chapter from Chapter 0091 to Chapter 0220. No child was sent anywhere in this batch.** *Open, and a date is not to be allowed to close it.*
- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE WHO KEEPS THAT HOUSE SAID THE SACKS HAVE NEVER BEEN COUNTED AND THAT THAT IS ON PURPOSE, AND SHE GAVE THE REASON IN HER OWN WORDS: A COUNT OF SACKS IS A COUNT OF WHAT THE DAYS BOUGHT, AND THE DAY SOMEBODY COUNTS THAT IS THE DAY IT IS A PRICE, AND IT IS NOT A PRICE, IT IS A SACK ON A STEP.** A sack came up the bank on the sixty-first day of the Long Month and it is the fourth a man of about forty who carries sacks has carried, and he has counted it and nobody in that house has. **Nobody in that yard asked her the other thing, and about nine people in it have said since that they were not going to be the one, and a woman who carries sacks up a bank and does not come up to the door is the reason nobody had to.** *Open and nobody may count them and nobody may ask.*
- **THE BOY OF EIGHT TIES A ROPE IN A YARD AND NOBODY HAS SAID ONE WORD ABOUT IT SINCE THE SEVENTH WEEK OF THIS FLOOD, AND ON THE SIXTY-FIRST DAY OF THE LONG MONTH HE AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE WHO KEEPS THAT HOUSE ARE THE ONLY TWO PEOPLE IN THAT YARD STILL DOING THE THING THEY DID ON THE EIGHTH DAY OF THAT MONTH, AND HE LOOKED AT THE GROUND WHEN FOUR PEOPLE WANTED HIM SAY THE OTHER HALF OF SOMETHING.** *Nobody improved on him and no chapter may.*
- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FOUR WHO IS ON NO ROLL OF ANYTHING WROTE A FIGURE DOWN AND RUBBED IT OUT, AND SAID SHE DID NOT OWN IT.** She wrote the seventy-two days in a market on the sixty-second day of the Long Month and rubbed it out in one movement, and gave the reason, which is that a list of four people who have not been asked is a roll and the woman of about twenty-nine tore a page out of her own book for that reason. **On the sixty-fourth she was asked whether to write down the fact that a woman had turned a slate over and was told no and wrote it down anyway, in four lines, with the date on it in her own hand, and joined it to nothing.** *Active, and the device is unchanged and she is still not on a page and nobody has thanked her for writing any of it.*
- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR FROM A FOURTH HOUSEHOLD ON THE ROAD HAS SAID OUT LOUD THAT SHE IS GOING TO ASK FOR A LINE, IN A ROOM, WITH A DATE ON THE DOOR, AND THE DAY SHE CHOSE CAME AND WENT AND SHE DID NOT DO IT.** She said it at the top of a cut on the fifty-ninth day of the Long Month, attached to **about four hundred and forty**, and the reason she gave for wanting a room is that a yard is a thing four people remember and a room is a thing a person can be held to. **She came nine sentences about the seventh line of the compact at the top of that cut and did not finish the ninth, and the ninth was the only one that was not true, and she has not been to the store in a yard to ask anybody anything.** *Open, and nobody may have her ask in a yard, and nobody may ask the man who built the causeway.*
- **A MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS HAS A BEAM AND TWO PANES HE MADE HIMSELF OUT OF A LENGTH OF IRON AND TWO LENGTHS OF WOOD, AND A ROD, AND A HOLE NINE FEET FROM THE ONE EVERYBODY MEASURES THAT NOBODY HAD MEASURED.** He said a sack and a ninth of a sack and said the ninth and not the number, and that the iron came off a gate and the wood off a rack and he cannot say whose either. He stopped measuring on the fifty-fifth day of the Long Month and started again on the fifty-ninth, and gave the reason out loud, which is a beam and a ninth, and said that a hole four people measure is a column about a hole. He came eleven miles on the sixty-first day of that month for a rod he never had and eleven miles on the sixty-fifth day of it to say that he has got nothing. *Open, and the four weeks is still a direction and not a number.*
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER HAS A PAGE IN HER OWN POCKET WITH A DATE ON THE BACK OF IT AND TWO NAMES ON THE FRONT OF IT AND NO THIRD, AND SHE HAS SAID THE ONE SENTENCE SHE HAS THREE TIMES AND NOT A FOURTH, AND IT IS IN THE PROSE OF NONE OF THE TEN CHAPTERS.** She was on a step and in a yard twice in these ten days and said both times that she had been in that yard three times and had not said it once. **Nobody in either place asked her about the four and nobody asked whether they will go, and a man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall said he will not carry it to a fourth place and calls that being useful.** *Open and carried and nobody may ask.*
- **A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL CAME UP A BANK WITH A BASKET HE DID NOT NEED TO BRING, WORKED OUT WHAT HE WOULD WRITE AT THE END OF A LINE FOR A SACK, FOUND THERE IS NO FIGURE AT THE END OF THAT SENTENCE, AND DID NOT OPEN A TWELFTH ENTRY IN HIS COLUMN.** He also said in a yard on the sixty-fifth day of the Long Month that a third copy of the front of the compact would be the second rumour, and that he said no to it, and that the distinction between a copy and a rumour is one he made up on a Thursday morning. *The column is still eleven entries, no figure on the end of any of them, and there is still no twelfth, and it was not recited in these ten chapters.*
- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL SAID WHAT THE NINTH LINE IS WORTH, AND SAID HE WOULD TAKE IT OFF THE PAGE AND IS NOT GOING TO, AND SAID THAT A DATE IS NOT A CLOCK, AND SAID THAT A PAGE WITH ONE HANDLE ON IT IS NOT A BETTER PAGE THAN A PAGE WITH NONE.** He has not put a sentence on a page since the fiftieth day of the Long Month and has not said out loud that he is going to stop, and he has been asked to undo a thing in a yard and has said no twice, and in neither case was it said in a market and both were said to a person who had made the thing herself. *Open, and the ninth line is where it was on the forty-sixth day of the Long Month.*
- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SHOP FOUR STREETS DOWN FROM A MARKET KEEPS HER COUNTS ON A THURSDAY AND CANNOT SAY WHY, AND HAS NINE PRICES ON SLATES BEHIND HER COUNTER AND HAS NEVER HAD ONE OF THEM WRONG AND HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN ABLE TO CHECK ONE.** She said in a room over a yard on the fifty-seventh day of the Long Month that the two of those are not the same problem and that she has not got an answer to either, and in a market on the sixtieth day that every one of the four people who asked her whether the queue was still on the page asked because they were afraid and not one because they wanted to know whether it worked. *Active, and the two are not the same problem and neither is answered.*
- **A GIRL OF SEVENTEEN STOOD AT THE RAIL OF A YARD FOR ABOUT NINE MINUTES AND WAS NOT ASKED ANYTHING, AND STOOD ABOUT NINE FEET FROM A MAN WHO HAD JUST MADE A FIGURE ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE AND SAID NOTHING.** *Unresolved on purpose. A chapter may not resolve it and may not have anybody ask her whether she is all right as a way of ending a chapter.*
- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY WHO COUNTS WATER ON THE FINGERS OF HER LEFT HAND CAME DOWN ONTO THE FLATS AND LOOKED INTO THE MEASURED HOLE AND NOT INTO THE OTHER ONE, AND SAID SHE WANTS SOMEBODY ELSE TO HAVE TO HOLD IT.** *Open and deliberately unresolved and she is still the only person in this city who keeps one.*
- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO CANNOT GET A RACK ABOVE HER SHOULDER SAID THREE WORDS AT THE TOP OF A CUT, WHICH WERE *you will be refused*, AND THE FINDING IS THAT A REFUSAL WITH A DATE ON IT IS WORTH SOMETHING TO THE PERSON WHO ASKS AND NOTHING TO THE PEOPLE IT IS ABOUT.** *She is not an example of anything and this chapter does not make her one.*
- **A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE FROM THE SETTLEMENT'S WORKROOM REFUSED TO ANSWER WHETHER A CHEST OF SALT IS A WAGE IN A YARD, AND SAID HIS BOOK GOES NOWHERE, AND SAID IN THE SAME WEEK THAT A FIGURE WITH NO OWNER IS A FIGURE ANYBODY CAN ADD TO, AND PUT ANOTHER MAN'S SENTENCE IN HIS OWN BOOK IN HIS OWN HAND AND DID NOT TELL HIM.** *Open, unpaid, and the reason a man with a book of his own that goes nowhere is still on no roster.*
- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WITH A HAND THAT WILL NOT CLOSE ASKED WHAT IT COST AND GOT FIVE WORDS, AND WORKED OUT A CHEAP TEST FOR WHAT THE NINTH LINE IS WORTH AND SAID HE IS NOT GOING TO WRITE THE NUMBER DOWN.** *Open, and he is still the only person in a room who says he does not know out loud without being asked.*
- **A MAN OF FORTY ASKED THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL TO TELL HER THAT A SLATE WENT FACE DOWN, AND HE SAID SHE WOULD HAVE SAID THE TRUE THING ABOUT IT AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO HAVE IT IN A YARD NINE MILES FROM HIS OWN STALL, AND A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL BY A WHARF WAS NOT IN THAT YARD AND NOBODY SENT FOR HER.** *Open, and it is the device of a batch refusing to assemble its own people in one room for a warm scene.*

### The woman of forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms

**Not asked, not approached, not named, and nobody stood outside the fourth house. She is not in these ten chapters at all and the hole in the Reach's own rule that is shaped exactly like her is not mentioned in these ten chapters either.** *Open, deliberate, and Volume 05 has not gone near the house in twenty chapters.*

## Owen Park, the box, the wax, the eight names, the six rooms, the Narrow Mark, the chapel stone, the boards, the twenty-fifth reading, the provisional recognition, the seat of holder of the pull, the compact of eleven lines, the woman of about twenty-nine's one sentence

**None of them moved in these ten days and none of them is mentioned, except the trestle count, which is one hundred and seventy-two in the book and one hundred and fifty-eight and one hundred and sixty-five on the wall, and the clock, which was one day long and ran out on the fifty-sixth day of the Long Month and whose slate is face down.** The word *knock* is at zero in every chapter from Chapter 0091 to Chapter 0220 and **is not at zero in this manuscript: one hundred and sixteen uses in thirty-four files without regard to case, seven in Volume 01 and a hundred and nine in Volume 02, the last of them in Chapter 0090, of which one is a capitalised *Knocks* and a hundred and fifteen are lower case. No state file may say it is.** The room at the far end of the Narrow Mark is unlooked at, in the ninth batch, and this batch did not go and look. **Ivenn Marrow is in no chapter of this batch and nobody asked him about anything, and the line in his book that is not a number is on a page in the narrator in Chapter 0212 and he did not mention it and nobody asked him.**

## What Chapters 0221 to 0230 added to this cast, and only what is on a page

**AND THESE ENTRIES HAVE BEEN THROUGH THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS OF `reviews/volume-05/batch-0003.md`, and two of them were wrong about the page and are corrected in place, and nothing else in this section moved: a stage, a decision, a refusal, a date, a figure, a trade or a descriptor. NOT ONE FIGURE, DATE, ANCHOR, DAY, BEAT, CAST MEMBER, ENDING OR THREAD STATE MOVED.**

**Ten chapters, ten days, days 221 to 230, one chapter to a day. The rules are `bible/power-system.md` §29. Nobody in these ten days got stronger and nothing was resolved in a form. Adrian Vale is in four of the ten chapters (0222, 0225, 0226, 0230) and did ordinary work in all four, which is what `workspace/volume-05/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` instruction 8 required and what Batch 0002's review named as the most consequential prose fault in that batch.**

- **ADRIAN VALE — STAGE 2, PASSAGE HOLDER, IN THE DOING, AND FOUR THINGS HE DID.** He picked a lump of salt up off a pan with both arms on the sixty-seventh day of the Long Month and carried it about nine feet and put it down, and his hands went white at the prints. He walked nine miles to the store's yard on the seventieth day of that month, a Tuesday, where about nine people could have said his name and not one of them did, and said out loud that he had been wrong about a queue and had been wrong since the fifty-fourth day of that month and nobody had corrected him because nobody knew he had said it. **He moved eleven sacks along a wall in the back room of that store on the seventy-first day of that month and did not lift the twelfth and has entered that he came up nine miles and did not help.** He went down into a dry channel on the seventy-fifth day of that month on his own and said that what he saw was a channel and not a sentence. **A share with the post taken out is fifty-three days in a coat on the sixty-sixth day of that month and sixty-two days on the seventy-fifth, and it is unanswered, and he took it out of the coat on the seventy-first day and put it back in about the time it takes to turn a thing over and said he does not know which of two things he is better at.** No chapter of this batch offered him a workway and he asked for none, no chapter turned the name he did not choose into a qualification, nobody wrote him a column, no chapter asked him about a gate, no threshold was opened, he performed no working, and the other world is not named once in the ten chapters. **A man of about twenty-nine from the settlement's workroom wrote on a slate on the seventieth day of the Long Month that he is not going to say Adrian Vale's name, with that day at the bottom of it as the day he wrote it and not the day it stops, and put the slate face up on the boards of that yard for about a minute. About nine people in this city can still fetch him and he was not fetched once in these ten days.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS — A CRACK IN A BEAM, A ROD LYING FLAT, A NUMBER HE DID NOT CHOOSE, AND A PAN IN TWO PIECES.** He made the beam himself out of a length of iron and two lengths of wood on the fifty-eighth day of the Long Month and has carried it nine miles since, and he put his thumb in a crack in the far pan on the sixty-seventh and told about nine people what was wrong with it before he used it. **THE CRACK HAS BEEN IN THAT PAN SINCE THE FIFTY-NINTH DAY OF THAT MONTH AND HE TOLD ONE YARD ABOUT IT ON THE SIXTY-SEVENTH AND HAS TOLD NOBODY SINCE, and at the seventy-third, the day the pan came apart, the crack is fourteen days old and the secret is six. The batch had never separated the two and a review repair pass made the prose separate them.** He found on the sixty-ninth day of that month that about nine people in this city had turned his *in about four weeks* into the seventy-fifth day of that month, and he said that a direction is a figure with the day left off it and that he would be at the bottom of that cut on the seventy-fifth and would not say what he expected to find. He put a rod down flat on the cracked bottom of the cut instead of in a hole. He said he would not name the rack his wood came off and would not name the beam. On the seventy-third day of that month the far pan came apart with a quarter of a sack in the near one and he said he was not going to make another one, because a beam with one pan on it can do one of the two things it was made to do and not the other, and **he left the beam in that yard and did not carry it home.** On the seventy-fifth day he said at the bottom of the dry channel that he has never once said what the four weeks was a direction about. He has a block of stone about the length of his own foot that the beam sits crooked on and has had since the second month of this flood. **He still cannot say whose iron or whose wood either was.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON THE OPEN HAND BOARD — A LUMP AGAINST HIS CHEST.** He bought a quarter of a sack in a market in this city on the sixty-second day of the Long Month and counted it out of his own hand into the other one's, and by the seventy-second day of that month it was a lump that a bar would not go into, and he had taken it to about nine places and about four of them had a bar. He said in a yard on the sixty-sixth day of that month that he was not going to find out by breaking it and that break is not open, and he has not broken it. **He stood at the edge of a yard on the seventy-second day of that month with a length of wood lying on the boards four feet from him and did not ask for it, and at the gate of a yard on the seventy-third day he put his hand on the pan of a beam that was still sound, and he is a party to the compact the posting is bound to. He has still not been asked whether the ninth line works, and has still not answered.**

- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR IN THAT STORE — A SLATE, A CHEST, TWELVE SACKS AND A HOLE IN HER FLOOR.** She hung the slate with a date on the chest on the forty-sixth day of the Long Month and turned it face down on the sixty-fourth, and on the sixty-sixth day of that month she refused to open the chest on a Friday and gave the reason. On the seventieth day of that month, with about a hundred and forty people in her yard, she did not open it at all and said in a yard that a chest opened in a yard is a wage and a chest not opened is not one and that she is not a person who gets to say which. **On the seventy-first day of that month she said out loud that she put the four re-tied sacks at the bottom of that chest on the fifty-sixth day of that month and has not told anybody, and that the only thing that happened is that a woman of about thirty-four could see all four of them at once and count them without a slate.** She has been putting the sacks in a heap alone for fifteen days, has stopped apologising for it, and said that the kind ones would cost her more than the sacks did. The floorboards have a groove in them in the shape of a sack and nobody in that building had made it.

- **A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE FROM THE SETTLEMENT'S WORKROOM — A SLATE PUT FACE UP ON A BOARD.** He carried a slate under his arm from the forty-sixth day of the Long Month because he had nowhere to put it, and on the seventieth day of that month he put it face up on the boards of the store's yard, said out loud that he was not going to say Adrian Vale's name and had written that down with the day he wrote it at the bottom of it, and picked it up about a minute later and put it back under his arm. **It is the first decision anybody in this city has written down against himself, and he said in the same yard that he could not tell that morning why that is different.** On the seventy-first day of that month he stood in the doorway of that back room and did not come in and said that a man standing in a doorway is not a man helping.

- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR FROM A FOURTH HOUSEHOLD ON THE ROAD — A NUMBER SHE DID NOT SPEND.** On the sixty-ninth day of the Long Month she said the seventy-fifth day of that month out loud at the top of a cut, to a man who had said *in about four weeks* and did not know there was a number. She said in the same cut that she was not going to choose another day in a cut or in a yard, and had not chosen one since the fifty-sixth day of that month. She came nine sentences once on the fifty-ninth day of that month and did not finish the ninth, and the ninth was the only one that was not true, and nothing in these ten days finishes it. On the seventy-fourth day of that month she walked eleven miles to the bottom of the Redroot cut, stood at the bottom of a bank, asked for nothing, and said she was not going to be the one who asks because the woman of that house decided as far back as the forty-eighth day of that month that nobody in this city is going to count the sacks on that step. **She has still not asked in a room in daylight with a date on the door and she has still not chosen another day.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO — A BANK HE STANDS ON AND DOES NOT COME DOWN.** He said on the forty-eighth day of the Long Month that he is not going to be the man who walks up nine feet and asks a woman whether a thing she said on a date is still the thing she says on the date. He has stood on that bank five Mondays in a row, 196 to 224, and after that four days in a row that are not Mondays — the seventy-second, the seventy-third, the seventy-fourth and the seventy-fifth days of the Long Month — and the nearest of the five Mondays is six days before the last of the ten days. **THE PREVIOUS VERSION OF THIS LINE SAID THE SEVENTY-FIFTH WAS THE SIXTH DAY IN A ROW AND THE FIRST THAT WAS NOT A MONDAY, AND THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS FOUND IT IMPOSSIBLE, because the five days before the seventy-fifth are the seventieth to the seventy-fourth and not one of them is a Monday. `chapter-0230.md:89` now says the four days and the six days and the page is right.** He has about three hundred days of being angry at himself and has not spent one of them on a length of wood or on a day. He says he has been asked about nine things about his own standing on banks and has answered one.

- **A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO CANNOT GET A RACK ABOVE HER SHOULDER.** She was at the top of that cut on the sixty-ninth, the seventy-second and the seventy-fifth days of the Long Month, and on the seventy-second day she lifted one end of a length of wood about four inches off the boards and put it down again and said nothing about why she could not lift the rest of it, and about four people in that yard worked it out from about nine feet. She said on the seventy-second day that there are about nine racks that can be worked and about four hundred and forty people on eleven miles of flats and that about nine of each of those two things is on somebody's rota, and that she is not going to add them together in a yard because she has been in two rooms where somebody added two numbers and everybody in the room started believing the sum. **She came nine sentences once and did not finish the ninth and that is still the case, and she has not asked and about four people in this city have stopped saying that she is going to.**

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL — A PAGE WITH ONE HANDLE ON IT, TWICE MORE.** On the sixty-eighth day of the Long Month he said that the only thing anybody in that market had done in a week he could have put a figure on was a man carrying a wage four streets twice and not opening it, and he was not going to. He asked whether a sack could be left on a stall's boards overnight and was refused in one word.
