**The woman of about forty-six of the fourth household, with a sieve.** In two chapters (0271, 0278) and **she is the household the volume's finding of day 294 is about.** She worked six hours of a Monday with a sieve, put it down for the first time since the Sunday because a man picked it up, and gave him one Saturday and refused the shape of it: **on Monday they were two people and a sieve, on Saturday they are two people and a sieve and a woman with a butt and a boy, and the same Saturday is not the same Saturday twice and the whole of the Crown's page wants it to be the same Saturday twice.** Her *the sixth day of a nine* is now *the sixth day of the week*, because that is her own count of her own week and is not the man's nine. **Her second handle is the sieve, and it is against the side of the pot at the end of a Saturday so that it will not fall.**

**The man of about thirty-six of the market town.** In eight chapters (0273 to 0280) and **he is the volume's argument in a person and he is a man and not the keeper of the Redroot gate.** He keeps nothing, holds gates on a turn, is not on the roll that went up on the wall, and on day 280 said that a keeper is not a holder and would not say what the difference was worth, **and asked for it to be entered that he is not going to say which and not that he does not know it.** His second handle is a bar stood against a post, a barrow, a spade, and a bootful emptied before he answers.

**The man of about forty-nine who keeps a store, and the first wage this volume has watched stop.** In two chapters (0276, 0277), eleven years at the north end of a market, nine people paid out of a pocket out of a wooden bowl. **He named a fifth less out loud in a market on the Thursday, which is the first time anybody out past the last named house has said a cost of a wage in public, and on the Friday about eight of the nine did not turn up, and he did not shut anything and nobody went to get anybody.** *Open, and a later batch may not repair it by inventing a word and may not convert a figure about a drain into a wage.*

**The man of about thirty-eight who keeps a tally on the flats, and the only page about about four hundred and forty people in four hundred miles.** In one chapter (0279) and **he has put that number in a return four times a year since the winter before the water and nobody has ever answered it and nobody has ever come, and he does not know what it is for and has never been asked.** *Open, and a later batch may not rescue them, may not turn him into a helper, may not put anybody from those flats into a room, and may not say what a wage in salt is worth.*

**The girl of seventeen.** In one chapter (0279), on a page, on no roll, sweeping the boards of a wharf through a whole morning with a plain slate under her arm and a flat of split ash on a handle, **and not asked one question. Being unfindable is not a qualification and has never once been a solution.** *Open, and unresolved, and not sent for.*

**And nobody else is new, and nobody got stronger, and nobody left.** **The keeper of the Redroot gate is at zero across these ten files by name, by year, by count and by day, and the proof is a person-by-person read of every woman's physical signature and not a search:** no woman in these ten chapters has a stick, has a broom, has a board on boards, works alone at an invert, is silent all day, or is at a gate at all. **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall is in two chapters and is a man in both.** The man of about thirty-two who said he was finished and not stopped is in none of these ten chapters and is in no sentence with Adrian Vale. **The delegation has not left and nobody has been asked whether they will go. Marrow is in none of these ten chapters. The Regent is not named and has not been asked. Jonah Pike is on no page in these ten chapters. The ninth line of the posting was not moved, not dated, not read aloud and not resolved, and the two uses of *the ninth line* in these chapters are Adrian's own comparison to a page in a yard and a man of about twenty-six reading the roll of nine names on a wall, which is a different page.**

## Volume 06, Batch 0004 — Chapters 0281 to 0290, days 281 to 290. APPEND-ONLY. The block above is Volume 06 Batch 0003's and nothing in it is superseded. Where this file and a chapter disagree about a day, a name or a figure, the chapter is right. Batch record: `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0004.md`.

**Adrian Vale — 32, protagonist.** Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing, on all ten days, and he is on three of the ten pages and named in two of them. **He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, no chapter asked him a question about a gate, and his passage privilege is not spent, not threatened and not named.** What he did in these ten days: carried a question into three kitchens, in each of them brought nothing, because the instrument says the question goes to everybody who will be in the room and he is not in the room; found out that he is the only person in nine rooms who is never asked anything; and learned that the four days are the instrument and not a courtesy, and that a question given four days early is a question a household can say no to while a question given in a room is a question a household can only be caught by. **He was not fetched by anybody and he fetched himself. The one person sent in these ten days is a carter with nothing in his hand and he is not sent to bring Adrian anything.** He still does not know that a fifth condition exists.

**Hettis Roke — 44, of the Hearthguard of the outer ward, nineteen years in.** Asked about his own eight for the second time in a hundred and forty-six days, on a day he did not choose, and he answered **four words, I know which four**, and would not give a fifth, and the four words are in four mouths and on no page. He has not said which four. His second handle is unchanged, a slate he turns over and turns back, and in Chapter 0283 the four words come instead of turning it.

**The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register.** Turned her second slate face up in a room she has never turned it face up in, asked the question herself, and named the cost out loud first, which is what she does. **She entered the day, that she asked, and that he answered, and no figure and no name, and she will enter nine days and no answers.** She said in Chapter 0290 that of the four places on the Crown's page only the date will take what is coming back, and that a register with nine days in it and nothing under them is the only instrument anybody in this city has that an office nine hundred miles off cannot ask to have produced. **She is not Tamsin Quill, the two are never in one room, and nothing in these ten chapters settles whether they are one woman.** Second handle: the second slate, and the chalk she puts down and leaves lying across a figure.

**The man of about forty-one in a very good coat.** Said nine sentences in a room in daylight with a date on the door, and the ninth is that what a person writes at the seventh gate will be correct. **He declined to describe the room where the third of the four places gets decided and asked for the refusal to be entered.** His second handle is a hand inside a coat with nothing in it, which he puts in and takes out and puts in.

**The woman of about twenty-four, who is the account of documents in this city.** In a market town, writing and reading, and in Chapter 0287 she is asked in her own kitchen, and she names the price of no name out loud and pays it, and her answer is two lines, a day and a mark that is not a name. Second handle: the slate, the chalk, and a box on a shelf with two lines in it.

**The girl of seventeen.** Copied nine sheets in Chapter 0282, signed none of them, left a faltering in, said the reason for one thing in a single sentence, and was asked nothing. **She is on no roll, cannot be fetched, and being unfindable is not a qualification and has never once been a solution. She is not resolved.**

**The man of about thirty-six, who keeps a gate on that road.** In Chapter 0284 he works a drain and says a document and a drain are the same thing. In Chapter 0289 he comes down a lane with a bar and a bootful, stops at the near end, says he has not come down to be spoken to, and goes back up. **He is a keeper and not a holder, he was not asked anything in either chapter, and the gatekeeper of the first gate is in neither.**

**The carter of about thirty-five.** Sent up a lane on the thirty-third with nothing in his hand, gave the question to the ninth place — a household at the end of a lane past two gates, whose kitchen day is the thirty-ninth — **six days** before the day it was to be asked on, with no sheet and so with no four days behind it, was not turned out, and came back with an answer nobody can file. Second handle: a thumbprint of tar, on a sill that is not his and then off his own thumb onto his own belt.

**Ilva Rooke — about forty-five. NEW, and she gave her own name in daylight at a door on the thirty-fourth day of the Short Month.** The first person the Crown has sent anywhere in this manuscript. She chose her day, stood at a door for four hours with a shut case, was let into a kitchen where a household was being asked, heard the household say yes, and went away with two of the four places filled in. **She is not a threat with a face and she is not defeated. Second handle: the case, and a slip of paper in her coat with her own name on it and the day she chose under it in her own hand, which she shows nobody.**

**The woman of about twenty-eight of Long Rigg.** Said yes in the first kitchen and gave a day that is not on anybody's list, and refused to sign, and said the thirty-first is a Sunday and on a Sunday her kitchen is not a room with two people in it. Second handle: a box on a shelf above the fire.

**The man of about forty-seven.** Kept the days of a gate in a stone in his own sill, said yes, and named the thirty-ninth, which is the ninth of the nine. Second handle: the stone, turned over twice while he works.

**The woman of about thirty at the stall.** Spent Chapter 0284 at the head of a drain saying she will carry at four buckets and not at nine, and Chapter 0285 in her own house with a net and a shoulder and a yoke against a door, having decided not to take the pail line back. Second handle: the yoke.

**New descriptors this batch, so that the next volume does not invent them:** a woman of about fifty-one who sells kindling with a cart that goes as far as the third village and knotted the corner of her sacking at a drain; a boy of about fifteen at the north end of that market; a woman of about fifty-three with a chalk date on her own door; a man of about sixty-two with a boot; a woman of about thirty with a yoke, who is on a page in Chapters 0284 and 0285 and is not new to this batch and was not new to the manuscript; and **Long Rigg**, a row of nine houses, named in a kitchen in daylight with a date on the door and not by narration.

**Rulings that had to be applied and are recorded so they are applied again:** the keeper of the Redroot gate is at zero in every form including her working, and the check is a person-by-person read and not a search; the four women of about thirty-four and the man of about thirty-four are at zero across all ten chapters; the two twenty-nine-year-old women are never in one room; the sentence from Chapter 0154 is not said and is not improved on; a notice goes in a hand and not in a mouth; the question about Roke's eight is not printed and is not printed by a later writer; **and the word *holder* is not defined by anybody in these ten chapters and the third of the four places is still empty.**

## Volume 06, Batch 0005 — CHARACTER STATE, Chapters 0291 to 0300

**Newest block wins. Nothing above it was restructured. Day 300 is the last day of Volume 06 and this is the state of the people on it.**

### 1. ADRIAN VALE — unchanged, and smaller than he was

**Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing, on all ten days and on all fifty days of the volume.** He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened a third time, the other world is not named once in the fifty chapters, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **no chapter in the volume asked him a question about a gate**, and no chapter turned the name he did not choose into a qualification. **His passage privilege is not spent, not threatened and not named, and the words *privilege* and *passage* are at zero across the ten files of this batch.** **In the last ten days he is on five pages and named in none of them:** he carries the question into three kitchens (0291, 0292, 0293), he stands at a second gate for four hours on the day the days go back because he has decided not to be in a room (0294), and he is named once in a coda as a man who was in a room for four hours and was not asked one question in it (0296).** He was not fetched by anybody and he fetches himself. **He is on no page in Chapters 0295, 0297, 0298, 0299 or 0300.** What he learned in these ten days: that four days of notice is for the person being asked and not for the person asking, and that he had known that since the twenty-eighth day of that month and had not known what to do with it until the morning of the thirty-ninth. **He was not in the room when the last of the nine households said yes, he said so at the seventh hour before anything happened, and he was told afterwards by a woman of about nineteen who was in it. There is a fifth condition that a woman who reads Crown Law said exists and did not give, and he does not know it exists, and nobody in these ten days told him.**

### 2. HALLA FENN, of about fifty — NEW IN VOLUME 06, and she is the volume's last new person

**Keeps a gate on a road that comes down from the north side of the Marches. Has kept it for about nine years and it was her mother's before it was hers. NEVER NAMED ON A PAGE AND GIVES NO NAME — a search for her name returns zero and the zero is the fact, not a gap, and no chapter of Volume 06 supplies one and no later chapter may either. On three pages: 0295, 0299, 0300.** A bar of ash run in two brackets set in the stone when the second gate went up that road, a wooden hook, a piece of chalk, and a date in chalk on the post every morning with nothing rubbed out. **Second handle: a brass key on a tarred cord about a foot long, in the apron pocket, taken out of the coat at the seventh hour and put back inside the coat at the fourth, and it is the same in all three chapters.** **There is no ring of chalk on the inside of her own door and there is no other object of hers on a page; a state file that once said there was one was repaired against the chapters.** She says a key is not a figure and a keeper is not a holder. She answers three questions out loud on the fortieth with the same answer each time and asks for no thanks for Thursday and does not want any. She told one man with a barrow about the line in the orchard on the forty-fourth and told nobody else, and says the barrow of boards is the only place she has said it and a barrow of boards is going somewhere else. **She is not the keeper of the Redroot gate and must never be read as her: she has no stick, no broom, no board on boards, no invert, no cut and no day of silence, and her light comes over a shoulder of a hill and does not reach the gate, which is a light stopped by a hill and not a light going off the far side of anything. The read is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0005.md` §6.2.1.** She is not a holder, has never been entered in anything, and nobody has ever asked her a question by somebody who was sent.

### 3. The households, and the nine, and what each of them holds

| Where | Who | Second handle | Chapter |
|---|---|---|---|
| the sixth of the nine kitchens | a man of about fifty | a hand flat on a shelf where two answers lie face down, and four times putting a piece of chalk down on a table with a lip on one side | 0291, 0298 |
| the sixth | a woman of about twenty-seven | a hinge-knife put down on the boards and stepped over, and a box on a shelf with two answers in it face down | 0291 |
| the seventh | a man of about forty and a woman of about thirty-five | a pot that had made the decision for her, a tarpaulin with a loose corner, and a heap of nine doors' worth of boards at the front of the house | 0292 |
| the eighth | a man of about thirty-seven and a woman of about twenty-two | a sack put against the wall and not opened, and a pail put on a step and not inside | 0293 |
| the ninth place | a man of about fifty-eight | four sentences said out of a door at the seventh hour on a Monday, to a lane and not to a man | 0294 |
| the ninth place | a woman of about fifty-four | a date in chalk on her own door above a sheet, and two sentences said to a paper after four had been said to a lane | 0294 |
| the last of the nine, at the eleventh hour | a woman of about nineteen | her hands on her knees and not going down them | 0294 |
| the road north, on the fortieth | a man of about thirty-eight with a barrow of boards | a barrow standing against the wall of a gate for six days that nobody has taken away | 0295, 0299, 0300 |
| the eighth place, a Saturday | a boy of about ten with a plain slate and a lump of chalk | about half the thirty-ninth on it and about nine wrong, and nobody is going to correct him | 0294 |
| the last day, going down the lane | a woman of about sixty-five and a boy of about eight | a creel on her hip and then on his and taken off him | 0300 |
| the line in the orchard | a man of about sixty with a handcart | a fence and a length of rope and a *that was fair*, and he is going to come back in about nine days | 0300 |

### 4. The people who are on a page and did not move, and what they did in these ten days

- **The man of about forty-one in a very good coat**: a hand inside a coat that is empty, put in and taken out and put in, and the back of a sheet he does not show anybody. He walked nine days up that road with the page folded inside the coat and did not open it on a gatepost, and he said in a room that the woman at the top of that road is not defeated and not exposed and has not changed her mind, and that she is the only person in the whole thing nobody has refuted. 0296.
- **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register**: the second slate turned round and not written on and then a sentence and a date entered with nothing under the date. She named the cost first in Chapter 0283 and she names it in Chapter 0296. 0296.
- **The woman of about twenty-four, the account of documents in this city**: her own hands flat on a table on either side of a slate, a sheet moved a half inch, and the statement in Chapter 0298 that she has read both rolls of the same gates and has not put them together and is not going to and has not worked out what the difference is worth. 0296, 0298.
- **Ilva Rooke, of about forty-five**: a case set against a wall at the tenth hour, not opened, not sat down beside, her hand flat on it for four hours. She says the third place is still empty on the forty-first and will be empty on the day the sheet is dated, and that nobody has asked her one question about the winter road and nobody has asked her whether she thinks the sentence is a good one, and that a person who is not asked is a person who is not on a page. 0296.
- **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, who wrote the ninth line**: a piece of chalk he does not use, put down four times on a table with a lip on one side, and a slate he brought and did not turn over. He asked not to be thanked, was not thanked, and gave no reason. **The ninth line was not moved, not dated, not read aloud and not resolved.** 0298.
- **The woman of about fifty-one who sells kindling out of a market town**: the corner of her sacking, knotted and then unknotted in a room where nobody is talking to her, a bundle of withy put against a door, and four times through an hour in and out of a room shouting at a boy she cannot see. 0298.
- **The man of about twenty-six, who poled the raft, and the woman of about fifty-nine, who sat on its beam with a rope round her hand**: a list of nine in his head and not on a paper, a pole shifted two inches in a bed that did not move the raft, and a woman who is never out of breath and is not used to the water. 0297.
- **The woman of about thirty-six on a bank**, and a man of about sixty-eight who came out of a door and heard nine words and went back in. 0297.
- **The man of about thirty-six who keeps the gate at the top of the lane past two gates**: a bar put down against a post and left there, and a bootful emptied at the near end of a lane. He comes down a lane where nine households are speaking, does not go up, and carries his bar back to the top gate. 0294.
- **The girl of seventeen**: on no page in these ten chapters, on no roll, and not asked anything.

### 5. The people the volume refused, and where they are at day 300

**The keeper of the Redroot gate is at zero in every form including her working, and the check was a person-by-person reading and not a search.** **Alys Kern, keeper of a gate on the road west, has not been asked a single question in this volume and nobody has been sent to her, and her name is at zero in the last ten chapters.** The man of about fifty-five who keeps the first gate of that road has not been asked anything and this city still will not name him. The Regent is not named in the last ten chapters and has not been asked whether the days on a gate are the Crown's to schedule. **Hettis Roke, forty-four, is not in the last ten chapters and his four words are not improved on anywhere.** Tamsin Quill is at zero and is not in one room with the register-keeper. Jonah Pike is on no page in the last forty-one chapters and is presented as nothing. A woman of forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms is not asked and nobody stands outside that house. The man of about thirty-two who stood on a bank on day 245 is in no room with Adrian Vale in this volume and his days are at zero in the last ten chapters. The man of about fifty-two who digs is named in Chapter 0299 as a man who was not sent for and would have refused and does not appear. **The four women of about thirty-four are at zero in the last ten chapters and the man of about thirty-four is in Chapter 0298 in seven places and in no sentence in this batch with a woman of about thirty-four, because there is not one.**

### 6. And the sentence nobody in this volume is allowed to have improved on

**A line is not owned and what a household has is the days on it.** Said once, in Chapter 0154, at a junction, in the local language. **In none of Volume 06's fifty chapters, in no mouth and in no narration, and not improved on anywhere. The string *days on it* was on the page in Chapter 0294 in a boy of about ten's hand on a plain slate and is at zero.** What is on the page instead is a household deciding, in four kitchens, and nine of them giving their days back out of a door in daylight in four sentences, and a schedule emptied because a column of days belongs to a household and there is nobody who can be shown to be holding any of them. **The household is the finding and the sentence is not said and has no name and is not Adrian's.**

## THE VOLUME 07 OUTLINE PHASE — the cast is chosen, no character changed state, and the next phase is `workspace/volume-07/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`

**This block is the newest in this file and it wins. It was written by the Volume 07 outline phase, which wrote no chapter and moved no character. Every entry above stands. Nothing here is canon until a chapter says it in a room, in daylight, with a date on it, by a person, and the four people below are on NO PAGE yet and are named here as a plan, which is the whole of what this block is.**

**THE DESCRIPTOR MAP IS AT `outline/volume-07.md` §17.11 AND NOT HERE, AND THE REASON IS NOT COSMETIC.** A decision that lives only in a state file has not been taken, it has been typed. And the instrument has a demonstrated false-positive mode: **the standing prohibition that no paragraph carries the man of about thirty-four and a woman of about thirty-four together holds at ZERO on a word-bounded search and appears to be broken at TWENTY on an unbounded one, because *woman of about thirty-four* contains *man of about thirty-four*.** This phase confirmed both figures. A Volume 07 writer who runs the map without boundaries will find what looks like a merge, and **the correct action is to go and read the page and not to record a merge.** The four crowded descriptors of Volume 06 are avoided entirely by this volume's cast: *woman of about twenty-four* at 65 sentences in 20 chapters, *man of about thirty-six* at 51 in 16, *man of about thirty-four* at 27 in 8, and *woman of about thirty* at 27 in 14. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT THE SHUT GATE IS AT 7 SENTENCES IN 4 CHAPTERS AND IS NOT CROWDED, and three of the four are hers and the fourth is a different unnamed woman at a different gate at `chapter-0261.md:65`, and a reader of a paragraph count will think she is crowded and will write a merge.** The nine descriptors Volume 07 gives to people who are on no page were each measured at ZERO across the fifty files before they were taken: **a man of about fifty-seven, a woman of about sixty-nine, a man of about sixty-three, a woman of about twenty-one, a man of about twenty-five, a man of about forty-eight, a woman of about sixty-one, a man of about fifty-two, and a man of about sixty-four**, and the ninth was added by the repair of card 0306, which had put a crowded descriptor on a page and which the repair moved to this one.** Two more are used and are NOT at zero and are not claimed to be: **a man of about thirty-seven at 16 in 4 and a man of about sixty at 9 in 4, and both of them are people already on a page — the man who trades on the Open Hand board and the man with the handcart and the length of rope.** *Man of about forty-four* is at zero and is NOT used, because Hettis Roke is forty-four and the two must not be read as one.

### 1. THE FOUR PEOPLE WHO ARE ON NO PAGE, and how each one gets on one

**The rule is `bible/power-system.md`'s own and it has not moved: no name and no place arrives by narration, and it is checked for a room, in daylight, with a date on it, by a person. Volume 06's state layer broke this by naming a gatekeeper who was on no page and a reviewer caught it, and the name is in this repository only as the record of the strike. The movement that brings these four on is the oldest instrument in this book: a question in a person's own words, in writing, to everybody who will be in the room, FOUR DAYS beforehand, unsigned, with no name at the top of it.**

- **A man of about fifty-seven, who has paid money for half a holding.** On a page in **Chapter 0307**, in a room over a market, in daylight, with a date on the door, put there by the man of about forty-three who reads things and does not interpret them. **He is not a villain. He is right about most of what he says, the volume does not defeat him, he does not leave, and nobody in any chapter of this volume tells him he is wrong, because nobody in this volume is wrong.** He answers a return four times a year since the winter before the water and has never been told what it is for and has never asked. **He offers money for the walking of the line and the room does not take it, and the money stays in his hand through Chapter 0346, which is the state the whole volume is in.** Second handle, invented on the page in Chapter 0307: **a length of new rope he has not used.** In Chapters 0307, 0311, 0334, 0337, 0346 and 0348.
- **A woman of about sixty-nine, who holds the paper.** On a page in **Chapter 0305**, in a kitchen on the lane, put there by the man of about thirty-one who carries things, who puts a sheet on the table and goes out of the room and comes back and says why the question is in her words and not his. **She has not been asked anything either, and the man of about thirty-one visibly decides not to ask her what is in the tin, and the chapter is about that and about four people in four hundred miles work out that a man standing in a kitchen not asking a question is the only instrument in this flood that has never had to be explained.** She writes the question in her own hand and does not sign it. Second handle, invented in Chapter 0305: **a tin.** In Chapters 0305, 0320 and 0346, **and she asks the volume's new question out loud in Chapter 0346 and it is not answered.**
- **A man of about sixty-three, who farms it.** Her brother's son. On a page in **Chapter 0305** and at the centre of **Chapter 0306**, where the chapter is his hands and not his opinions: nine feet of wet silt, a spade, a willow line and a bucket, and a ditch that stops because the ground is firm and not because anybody said so, and about nine hundred paces off a shut gate the ground is not the same ground. Second handle: mud on his boots. In Chapters 0305, 0306 and 0338. **He says four words to his aunt at the end of Chapter 0306 and they are the only four words in that chapter about the boundary and they are not kind and are not unkind.**
- **The four who have not left.** **A woman of about twenty-one, a man of about twenty-five, a man of about forty-eight, and a woman of about sixty-one.** On a page one at a time, in four rooms on four days — 0330, 0331, 0332, 0333, which are days 330, 331, 332 and 333, a Tuesday to a Friday — and the notices for them go out on days 326, 327, 328 and 329 in Chapters 0326, 0327, 0328 and 0329, **four days each, and every one of the six notices in this volume is on the page in the chapter the outline names with the morning named inside it.** **Three of the four say yes in their own words. The fourth says no, in a sentence that is hers, in Chapter 0333, and the sentence is about opening nothing, and it is not anybody's defeat. And the three who said yes do not go, because a party of four is four and a party of four is a word this world does not have: the word *delegation* is at zero in three hundred and ten chapters and stays at zero.** Their ages are at zero across Volume 06's fifty files and were measured before they were taken. **A man of about twenty-five asks out loud in Chapter 0309 what the day after the sixtieth of the month would be, and no one in the room answers him.**

### 2. THE ONE WHO IS ON A PAGE ONCE AND HAS TO BE ESTABLISHED TWICE BEFORE ANYBODY ASKS HIM ANYTHING

**A man of about fifty-two who digs, on eleven miles of flats about the other side of this city, whose whole trade is putting things in holes.** He is named once in Volume 06, at `chapter-0299.md`, as a man who was not sent for and would have refused in the time it takes to shut a gate and would have been right, **and `state/volume-06-close.md` §3 item 8 is explicit that a Volume 07 which needs him has to establish the reason he is sent for in a room, in daylight, with a date on it, or not send him. HE IS ESTABLISHED TWICE AND IS ASKED NOTHING IN EITHER CHAPTER, AND NEITHER ESTABLISHMENT IS A ROOM HE IS ASKED IN. Chapter 0321 is him at work with about nine other men and nobody asks him anything, and the room in which the reason he would be sent for is established is in the same chapter, eleven miles off, in daylight, with a date on the door, and nobody is sent. The second is Chapter 0325, the tenth day, and it is on eleven miles of flats and not in a room: Adrian comes out along the head of the row at about the fourth hour and stops about nine feet off and stands there, and the digger tells him nothing and has not been asked anything, and the chapter says he worked it out and does not say he was right. **An earlier version of this file put the reason in Chapter 0324 and put Adrian beside him in a room at the fourth hour of the afternoon, and both halves of that were wrong: the reason is in 0321 and the yard is 0325 on the tenth and not the ninth.** Second handle, invented on the page in Chapter 0321: **a rod he does not carry.** In Chapters 0321, 0324, 0335, 0339 and 0344.

**AND WHAT HE MAY NOT DO IS FIXED AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF THE PROTECTION.** **He may not put a rod in a third hole, may not clear or measure either of the two holes nine feet apart, and may not touch the line in the orchard.** What he does in Chapter 0344 is put a barrow against **the end of the cut**, at a stone about nine hundred paces from the gate, look along it from there, say one sentence, and go away. **He is the first person in four hundred miles to look at this line and he is not permitted to learn anything a rod would have told him, and the chapter has to survive that.**

### 3. THE WORD, AND THE TWO MEN WHO PUT IT ON AND TAKE IT OFF, AND NEITHER OF THEM IS THE PROTAGONIST

**THE LINE IN THE GROUND IS CALLED A SCAR.** Said out loud, in the local language, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, by a person, in one word, in **Chapter 0310**, in the room over a market — and the chapter does not define it, does not improve on it, and does not say what it means. **It is taken back off the only page it is ever on in Chapter 0342, in the same room, in the same light, in chalk, with a cloth, by the same man, and the reason is four sentences and it is his and it may not be improved on: a name is only a place somebody can be taken to when somebody is going to go, and there is a man with a barrow in this city who is going to go, and I will not have a word on that slate for a thing a man is about to put a barrow against.** **After Chapter 0342 the line has no word on any page in the world, the reader has been told what it is called, and no page can say so, and the protection the naming bought is gone with the name.**

**The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall and wrote the ninth line** is on a page twenty-nine times across eight chapters of Volume 06 and is the fifth person in this manuscript's descriptor map and **is not any of the three women of about thirty-four.** He put the ninth line last, in a harder piece of chalk, on purpose, on the forty-sixth day of the Long Month, so that the word would be the last thing anybody could rub out, **and in Chapter 0310 he finds out that he has just found a way to rub it out and he has done it himself about nine times that afternoon and nobody in the room can tell.** He has refused five times to say whether the ninth line works, **the word in Chapter 0310 is not one of those five and it may not be counted as a sixth, and the word and the ninth line may never be made the same line.** He has a piece of chalk he does not use — **it is on a page already, put on a table in this room on the forty-third day of the Short Month by him and touched by nobody until Chapter 0310** — and it is the same piece in Chapters 0310 and 0342. **He asks in advance not to be thanked and is not thanked.**

**The man of about thirty-seven who trades on the Open Hand board** is on a page in Volume 06, six sentences in one chapter, and **he refuses this volume's one panel out loud in about four seconds in Chapter 0340, day 340, and what it costs him is specific: the board he keeps has had a name off it once, in his own hand, in about the ninth year of this flood, and he has never said so to anybody, and the refusal is what makes him say so in the same room, and saying so is the cost.** The panel is plain, in its own block, with no emphasis marks, gives a rule and answers nothing, and it is the only one in the volume: ***A word may be put to a thing in a room in daylight with a date on it, and a word may be taken back the same way, and a person may not be taken back at all.***

### 4. The four who are on a page and come back, and the state each one is in on day 300

- **Tamsin Quill** is on a page for the first time since the seventeenth day of the Short Month, and she is on it working, and **Adrian does not ask her for anything.** **The consent fracture is not mended in this volume and nobody may mend it, apologise for it, refuse an apology for it, forgive it or ask for it to be forgiven, and the fifth condition a woman who reads Crown Law said exists is not given.** **She leads the volume's route decision in Chapter 0325, day 325, a Thursday, in about six sentences, and the sixth is the one that costs, and Adrian follows it.** Second handle, invented on the page in **Chapter 0311** and the same in every chapter she is in: **a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time.** In Chapters 0311, 0324, 0325, 0336 and 0349. **And the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room and nothing in this volume settles whether they are one woman.**
- **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register** finds out on **day 309, Chapter 0309**, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, that the Short Month is sixty days long, that this is the fifty-fourth, and that **no form in four hundred miles covers a day after its sixtieth** — and she writes the answer on her slate with the day on it in the form she has and says out loud that it is the last answer in this city that will carry this month's word on it. Her second slate is her second handle and it is already on a page. In Chapters 0309, 0313, 0316, 0323, 0342 and 0346, **and it is her second slate the word goes on in Chapter 0310 and comes off in Chapter 0342.**
- **Hettis Roke, forty-four, of the Hearthguard of the outer ward, nineteen years in.** He is on a page from Chapter 0008 and is one of the four witnesses of Chapter 0010. He is asked about his own eight in the last volume on a day he did not choose and answered four words and gave no fifth, **and this volume does not ask him again, and it does not improve on the four words, and it does not settle whether the eight who got a stone got one under a name that was moved.** His roll of the dead of the outer gate, nineteen years, four times a year, a clerk's book, the count eight, nobody came, is on a page in Chapters 0314, 0317, 0318 and 0322, **and the eight names at the foot of a hundred-and-forty-year-old page are not read and the chapter is about the not reading.**
- **The woman of about fifty at the shut gate, who is on three pages and on no name and gives none.** **She may not be asked anything, again, by anybody, INCLUDING BY THE PROTAGONIST, AND NO CHAPTER MAY SEND ANYBODY TO HER FOR ANY REASON.** She saw the line on the forty-fourth day of the Short Month, has not been to look at it, and told one man with a barrow and nobody else. **A volume whose whole method is that people are not asked, which then asks the one person nobody asks, has thrown away the only thing it had.** Her second handle is a brass key on a tarred cord in the apron pocket, a bar of ash in two brackets, a wooden hook, a piece of chalk, and a date in chalk on the post every morning with nothing rubbed out, **and all of it is on a page already and all of it is the same in every chapter she is in.** In Chapters 0301, 0304, 0312, 0341 and 0347.

### 5. The protagonists, and the rule about naming him that this volume added

**Adrian Vale, Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing, on all fifty days. He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened a third time, the other world is not named once, nobody offers him a workway and he asks for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the volume and are not the words to reach for. HIS PASSAGE PRIVILEGE IS NOT SPENT, NOT THREATENED AND NOT NAMED. HE IS AGED NOWHERE IN VOLUME 07 AT ALL, and that is a decision and not an accident: Volume 06 gave him the age *a man of about thirty-two* three times and the first of the three handed him Volume 05's bank man verbatim, and the two are different people and the prohibition that they may never be in a room together is kept nowhere because the page has put the two descriptions on one man.**

**THE NAMING RULE, AND IT IS THE FIX FOR A FINDING.** Volume 06 named him in eight of its fifty chapters while the volume asserted in a coda that he is the one person nobody ever asks, and a close record should say that a thing is asserted rather than shown. **So: he is named in the chapters in which he is reading a page in a room — 0303, 0307, 0311, 0313, 0316, 0318, 0321, 0322, 0323, 0325, 0326, 0329, 0330, 0331, 0332, 0335, 0340, 0345, 0346 — and he is NOT named in the chapters in which people decline to ask him — 0305, 0306, 0308, 0309, 0310, 0312, 0314, 0315, 0317, 0319, 0320, 0324, 0327, 0328, 0333, 0334, 0336, 0337, 0338, 0339, 0341, 0342, 0343, 0344, 0347, 0348, 0349, 0350 — **AND 0321 AND 0324 WERE INVERTED IN AN EARLIER COPY OF THIS LIST AND ARE CORRECTED HERE AGAINST THE PAGES: he is named in 0321 and in 0324 not at all, and 0324 is on the page with him in it and his name off it, and the two lists still cover the same forty-four chapters and the gap below is still three** — and a batch's count of the second kind" — the rule is that the CARDS ARE THE INSTRUMENT AND THIS LIST IS A COPY OF THEM, and where the copy and the cards disagree the cards are right. **AND THE TWO LISTS ABOVE DO NOT COVER EVERY CHAPTER OF THE VOLUME, AND THE GAP IS THREE CHAPTERS AND IT IS A REAL GAP AND NOT AN OMISSION: 0301, 0302 and 0304 are chapters in which there is no page for him to read, so he is not named in them either, and Batch 0001's eight are 0301, 0302, 0304, 0305, 0306, 0308, 0309 and 0310. A chapter in which nothing is read is not a chapter in which a person declines to ask him, and the two must not be counted as one.** His gains in this volume are: a copy of an entry a hundred and forty years old in a clerk's hand in a room, a word he read off it and put to a line in the ground, and then had taken back. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.**

**Tamsin's want, unchanged and not asked for: to keep the Lock of Salt and the Hearth Marches from being claimed.** Jonah Pike is on a page six times in Volume 06, is right about nine parts in ten, is not a villain, is not defeated, does not leave, and the queue he keeps is running without him on it; he is in Chapters 0309 and 0334 and **is not wrong about the thing he is right about in either of them.** The man of forty-one in a very good coat is in Chapters 0318 and 0322 and the Regent is not defeated, not exposed and not converted. The man of about thirty-two on the bank is in no chapter of this volume and is in no sentence with Adrian Vale and **his days are at zero in all fifty chapters and no chapter of this volume prints a figure for them.** The man of about twenty-seven and his board are in Chapter 0336 and the board is not written on and the wall above it is not washed.

### 6. The next phase

**`workspace/volume-07/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, and it is a batch and not an outline.** It writes Chapters 0301 to 0310, ten chapters, days 301 to 310, one chapter to one day, ending on a Wednesday. **Ten of its ten cards name a third party, a task, or a thing carried, and say which, and eight of the ten are chapters in which Adrian is not named, and one of those eight — Chapter 0305, where a man of about thirty-one visibly decides not to ask the woman of about sixty-nine — has to show a person choosing not to ask. THREE OF THE TEN SHOW THE NOT-ASKING, and they are 0303, 0305 and 0307, and two of the three have him in the room and named (0303 and 0307, each reading a page) and one does not (0305). This count was three different numbers in three files before the Volume 07 outline review repair and is now one, and the rule is that the CARDS are the instrument and the state layer is a copy of them.**

## VOLUME 07, BATCH 0001 — Chapters 0301 to 0310, days 301 to 310

**This block is the newest in this file and it wins.** Written by the batch that wrote the ten chapters. **Where this file and a chapter disagree about a day, a name or a figure, the chapter is right.** The record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0001.md`. **Nobody in these ten chapters got stronger and nobody got weaker in a way a stage notices.**

### Adrian Vale — unchanged, and named twice

**Stage 2 on all fifty days of the volume, and aged nowhere in it.** He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark was not opened a third time, the other world is not named once in these ten files, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, and **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files.**

**He is named once in Chapter 0303 and once in Chapter 0307 and in the other eight he is not in a chapter in any form.** In the two he is in, he is standing in a room reading a hundred-and-forty-year-old clerk's hand over the shoulder of the man of about forty-three, and **in neither is he asked one question about the boundary, and in neither does he answer one.** He is named once by the mouth of a woman counting what is being read out in 0303 and once by the register-keeper in 0307, and in 0307 the woman of about thirty-three who has a question about the order of nineteen stones sees him in the room and does not ask it.

**Why that is on a page now and was not before.** The Volume 06 close record found that the volume asserted in a coda that he is the one person nobody ever asks and showed it nowhere. This batch shows it in two rooms and asserts it nowhere, and **in eight chapters he is not present at all, and in five of those the reason is that a person is at work or is not asked, and in three the reason is that there is no page in the chapter for him to read, and the two reasons are not the same reason and a volume-level count that merges them is wrong.** A later batch may name him in the chapters where he is reading a page and may not name him in the chapters where people decline to ask him, and the instrument is the ten cards and not this block.

### Tamsin Quill — not in these ten chapters

**She is in none of Chapters 0301 to 0310 and is on no page in any of them.** The consent fracture is not mended and no apology was made, refused, asked for or given. **Her second handle, a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time, is invented on the page in Chapter 0311 and NOT ONE WORD OF IT IS IN THIS BATCH, because a handle that exists in a state file before it exists in a chapter is a handle a writer will use before anybody has seen it.**

### The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register — the volume's second centre

**She is in six of the ten chapters, 0301, 0302, 0303, 0309, and the last two days of 0308, and she is the only person in this batch who writes.** Her second handle is **her second slate**, and it is the same in every chapter she is in and no other handle is given to her. She carried a notice up a lane in her own hand on day 301 and left it on a gate wall under a stone off a doorstep without calling at a door, and said in a mouth that she had not put a name at the top of a sheet in four years and that a sheet with a name at the top of it is a thing a clerk can send for.

**She is on three refusals and has added none. She refused to define the word in the third place of the Crown's page before the question was finished and stopped there, and the word is still not defined. She has three refusals, each with a cost named out loud first, and the cost of this batch's is that she is the person in every room who can say where a line is not.** She said out loud in a room on day 301 that she cannot say where the line runs and that she is not going to look at it, and nobody asked her why, and a man of about thirty-one who wanted to ask her why more than anybody in that room did not. **She said out loud on day 309 that the answer in front of her is the last answer this city will carry this month's word on, and she said it to nobody in particular, and a man of about twenty-five asked out loud what the day after the sixtieth would be and she did not answer him either.** She is not Tamsin Quill and the two were never in one room.

### The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall and wrote the ninth line — the volume's word

**He is in two of the ten chapters, 0307 and 0310, and his second handle is a piece of chalk he does not use, which is the same in both and which has lain on a table in the room over a market since the forty-third day of the Short Month, and which nobody touched for twelve days.** In 0307 he is in a room with a piece of chalk in his hand and a man of about fifty-seven with a rope, and he gets up and puts his coat on and walks out in the middle of the thing the man came with money for, and nobody stops him and nobody says anything about it.

**In 0308 he is in the room, at a table, and does not speak at all for about five hours, and the man of about fifty-seven is not one of the two men of about thirty-four and may not be read as him.** In 0310 he is asked a question about a mark of about a foot and about two inches and he says the word in one word and does not say what it means, and he says four sentences about what it is not and about a word on a page, and the mark is not a pattern, and he will not say the word a second time so that the register-keeper can get it right, and she gets it right, and no name goes on the line, at his asking, because a line with a name at the top of it is a line somebody can come back to.

**His five refusals about the ninth line stand at five and this batch did not add a sixth, and the two acts he has in this volume are about a word in an orchard and are not about the ninth line, and no chapter may make them the same line.** He was frightened in 0310 and the chapter does not use the word for it, and the sign of it was that he put his hand flat on the table where the chalk had been and left it there. **He is the second person in about a month to ask in advance not to be thanked and he was not thanked.** The ninth line was not moved, not dated, not read aloud, not taken down and not resolved.

### The woman of about fifty at the shut gate — not asked, twice, again

**She is in two of the ten chapters, 0301 and 0304, and she is not asked anything in either and nobody was sent to her.** Her second handles are the same in both and are the ones on a page: a brass key on a tarred cord in the apron pocket, a bar of ash in two brackets, a wooden hook, a piece of chalk, and a date in chalk on the post every morning with nothing rubbed out. In 0301 she says four things out loud, all of them to a post, a bar of ash, a barrow of boards and the lane, and one of them is that she has not read the sheet on her wall and that reading it would be the beginning of having been asked. In 0304 she works the gate the whole time a man talks to her and says four sentences to him and three to a post, and the last line of the chapter is hers and is said to a post and not to him.

**She is on a page in four chapters now, 0295, 0299, 0300 and 0301, and in 0304, and she gives no name on any of them and none may be given.** **The one thing only she and the man with the barrow know — that she does not know what the line is and told him so — is not said in either of these two chapters and nobody asked him about it, and a later chapter may not ask him either until the volume's own climax.** The proof that she was not used is a reading and not a search: her handles in 0301 and 0304 are the same five objects, the light comes over the shoulder of a hill and does not reach the gate in both, and the material in her hands is lime and chalk and a wooden hook in both, and she talks in both.

### The man of about thirty-eight with the barrow of boards

**He is in one of the ten chapters, 0304, and his second handle is a barrow.** He was at that gate on the forty-fourth of that month, when she told him what she had seen in the orchard, and he has not been up that road since, and on the forty-ninth, which is the Thursday the arrangement was for, he came at the sixth hour and did not take the boards up. He gave four sentences and asked in advance not to be thanked and was not thanked and nobody was angry with him. **He is not the other man of about thirty-eight, who is at the salt wharf in Chapter 0308 with a tally of about four hundred and forty, and the two have never been in one room and may not be.** A length of new rope that is not his is hooked on his barrow handle and he puts it back the way he found it, and the man of about sixty with the handcart and the rope is going to come back on the fifty-fourth of that month.

### The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf

**He is in one of the ten chapters, 0308, and his second handle is a tally-board.** He keeps a figure of about four hundred and forty on eleven miles of flats and has put it in a return four times a year since the winter before the water, and **on day 308 he was asked for the first time in nine years what the return is for and he said he did not know and had never known and that the return had never said.** The number was given to him by a man who is dead or gone; he has added to it in his own head about four times a year for nine years; the figure he sends is not the figure he has; **the figure he has was not printed in the chapter and a later chapter may not print it either.** He is not relieved and is not ashamed, and he goes back to the tally on the same day and does not rub it out.

### The man of about fifty-seven — new to a page, and not defeated

**He is on no page before Chapter 0307 and he is put on one by a person in a room in daylight with a date on the door, in daylight, with a man of about forty-three who reads things and does not interpret them.** His second handle is **a length of new rope he has not used**, invented on that page and not before it. He has paid money for half a holding and cannot show anybody where the half is. He has been answering a return four times a year since the winter before the water and has never known what it is for. **He is not angry in this chapter and the chapter says so, and a man who is not angry is harder to say no to than a man who is, and the room's difficulty on that day is that it is being reasonable to a man who is right about most of what he says.** He offered four men at a rate he named out loud and the room did not take the money and did not say no, **and the money is still in his hand and that is the state the whole volume is in.** He is a return, not an introduction, in the sense that a man who paid money for a half a holding is a thing this manuscript has been writing about since day 137; what is new is that he is now on a page in a room and has said nine sentences and the ninth was about a return.

### The woman of about sixty-nine — new to a page, and not asked either

**She is on no page before Chapter 0305 and she is put on one by a person in a room in daylight with a date on it, which in that chapter is the day of the sheet a man of about thirty-one left on her table.** Her second handle is **a tin**, invented on that page and not before it, and there is a folded sheet thirty years old in it. She is at the end of a lane, she is about sixty-nine, she has a right to be exact, and she is a person who has not been asked anything either. **She reads a stranger's paper twice and does not ask what is in the tin, and the man of about thirty-one stands in her kitchen and visibly decides not to ask her and says in a mouth that he was not going to.** She writes the question in her own hand on the back of the sheet he left, does not sign it, and says a question in your own words is worth nine times a question in mine, and that the reason is nine years old and it is the man who carries them and not hers. Her brother's son is a man of about sixty-three and says four words about a ditch and goes out.

### The man of about sixty-three and the man of about sixty-four

**The man of about sixty-three is on no page before Chapter 0305 and his chapter is 0306, and it is his hands and not his opinions.** He farms a half a holding he did not get and did not ask for and cannot show anybody the line of. He takes nine feet of wet silt out of a channel dry since the hundred and ninety-sixth day of this flood, in a bucket, up a board he laid himself, from the fifth hour to the seventh. He says four words to his aunt and goes out. **He is not a mystery and not a villain and not a hero, and the only thing in Chapter 0306 that is about the boundary is what he says about the ground going firm at about as far again as a man can throw a stone.** The man of about sixty-four is his neighbour on the same flats, a year apart, **and the instrument for telling them apart is the work and not the age: one has a bucket and a heap and the other has a bucket and a line of willow laid out ahead of him at the far end of what he has done.** The man of about sixty-four has been digging a ditch that is not his for a house that is not his, without being asked to and without being paid, **and he says so out loud once, unprompted, and the chapter does not discover it and does not make anything of it.**

### The man of about forty-three, the man of about thirty-one, the man of about thirty-three, the man of about twenty-five

**The man of about forty-three keeps other people's papers, has had a deed in a box for eleven years, has never been asked whether one word in it is true, and in Chapter 0303 wants to be asked and does not ask, and refuses to read the eight names at the foot of the copy because being unasked is his one position and he is going to use it.** He is in 0302, 0303 and 0309, and in 0309 he is the man who says that a form which runs out does not fail and that the person who has to make one up is whoever is standing in the room when somebody asks for a day. **He does not know that a notary of fifty is the woman who named the Short Month and does not find out in this batch.**

**The man of about thirty-one carries things for a living, knows a floor by the sound of a box, is in four of the ten chapters, and is in about two hundred rooms and about eleven kitchens a month and is the only person in nine kitchens nobody asked anything in.** He is the man who put the volume's second notice in his coat and did not read it upside down in a cart. **A person nobody asks anything is a person who cannot be put in a column, and the reason is nine years old and it is his.**

**The woman of about thirty-three is in four of the ten chapters and asked the only question anybody has ever asked about the order of nineteen stones, and got an answer that nobody knows whose order it is, and did not get one in Chapter 0307 and put her hand flat on the table instead.** **The man of about twenty-five is on no page before Chapter 0309 and he is put on one by a person in a room in daylight with a date on the door, and he is one of the four people who came to this city with a question and have not left, and he asks out loud what the day after the sixtieth would be and nobody in that room answers him, and about four of the nine in that room had worked it out on the way in and had decided that a man who is not asked anything ought not to be answered either.**

### The man of about fifty-two who digs, and the man of about thirty-seven

**The man of about fifty-two who digs is in NONE of these ten chapters, was asked nothing, and put a rod in nothing.** He is on a page once in Volume 06 as a man who was not sent for. **The man of about thirty-seven, who trades on the Open Hand board, is on a page in one line in Chapter 0309, in the back of a room, with a board of his own under his arm, saying nothing at the point where a room runs out of forms, and he is the man who refuses the volume's one panel in Chapter 0340 and his refusal costs him a name he has had off that board once and never said.**

### The next phase

**`workspace/volume-07/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, and it is a batch and not an outline.** It writes Chapters 0311 to 0320, days 311 to 320. **Tamsin Quill comes onto a page in Chapter 0311, working, and Adrian does not ask her for anything, and her folded sheet of road with about nine places on it is invented there and nowhere else.**

---

## THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0002 — the people of days 311 to 320, and nobody in them got stronger

**This block is the newest in this file and it wins.** Written by the batch that wrote the ten chapters. **Where this file and a chapter disagree about a day, a name or a figure, the chapter is right.** The record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0002.md`. **Nobody in these ten chapters got stronger and nobody got weaker in a way a stage notices.**

### Adrian Vale — unchanged, and named five times out of ten

**Stage 2 on all fifty days of the volume, and aged nowhere in it.** He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark was not opened a third time, the other world is not named once in these ten files, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, and **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files.**

**He is named in five of the ten chapters — 0311, 0313, 0314, 0317 and 0318 — and in the other five he is in no chapter in any form.** In all five of the named ones he is reading a page: her folded sheet of road on a road, the clerk's hand on a sheet pinned to the corner of a deed, a register open at a page about forty days back, and a notary's five lines read twice. **In every one of the five he is asked no question about the boundary and he asks none.** In 0311 he is named once, by Tamsin Quill, on a road, and she says nothing else about him for the rest of the day. In 0312, 0315, 0319 and 0320 he is not in a chapter in any form because there is no page in them for him to read. **In 0316 he is in the room on the morning the month was named and the chapter does not say so, because a chapter in which people decline to ask him is a chapter he is not in.** That is now the fifth of these in this volume and the two reasons are still not the same reason.

**What he did and did not do with the calendar.** In 0313 he said the only thing anybody said about the shape of a form — that the copy is the only page in four hundred miles on which anybody has written one down and that nobody copied the shape, only the words — and then said he was not going to make it an argument, because a room where a woman is going to be asked to make one in three days is not a room for his argument. In 0317 he said that a sheet with a notary's name on it is a page, that he has never seen a notary's five lines before in his life, and that he has watched nine things go onto nine tables this month and none of them knew in advance what it was for and about four of the nine were not asked afterwards. **Nobody in this batch asks him what a month is called, or what a month is for, or what a form is, and he says nothing on any of those, and the naming is a woman's.**

### Tamsin Quill — on a page again, working, and not asked for anything

**She is in Chapter 0311 and in no other of the ten, and she is on it working, on a road, and not in the room over a market — because the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room and nothing in this volume settles whether they are one woman.** Her second handle is invented on the page in that chapter and in no other: **a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time — long way in half, then in half again across, and the corner that came last goes under — and the reason she gave, once, is that a sheet folded some other way can be taken for a notice, and a notice has a day on it, and hers has not got a day on it and is not going to get one.**

**She walked nine places in three days asking one question at each — what is this ground called, and who called it that — and got eight answers, and seven of the eight are the two names the two documents use written seven different ways, and the eighth is the nine acres, from a woman of about thirty-three behind a bread board who said that nobody has ever been asked and that it is not a name anybody writes down because there is nowhere to write it. The ninth place had eight houses in it and nobody came to a door and she did not go back, and she left the ninth line empty rather than writing no answer on it, because an empty line can be filled in and a line that says no answer can be argued with, and there was nobody there to argue with. Eight households asked to keep the sheet.**

**The consent fracture is not mended and nobody apologised in it, and neither of them said a word about why, and the reason is nine years old and it is hers as much as his.** She found out a thing about a room she is going to have to walk into — that a man of about forty-three has nine boxes and neither of the two documents ever asked anybody at a door what they called it — and she says so to the road and not to him, and he walks down the low ground beside her with his hands in his coat and says nothing about it, and about four people who have been in rooms with both of them have worked out that the whole of what he gave her that afternoon was a road back into a city at dusk. **She is in Chapters 0324, 0325, 0336 and 0349 later in this volume and the fold is the same in all of them.**

### The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register — four refusals, and one non-refusal

**She is in eight of the ten chapters and she is the only person in this batch who writes.** Her second handle is her second slate, and it is the same in every chapter she is in and no other handle is given to her.

**She is on four refusals out loud and she has added none to that count, and each has a cost named before it.** This batch's is the day-154 column's cost: on day 316 she wrote a count of the days of the flood on a door in chalk in a place where a month had been, and said the reason out loud before anybody asked — a number a person put on a door is a date until somebody puts a better one on it, and if she is wrong then every day that goes on after is wrong on the same count, and nobody in four hundred miles can check it. **She gave up a blank space on a door, which is the one thing in this flood nobody has ever had to explain, so that a notary could see what a room looks like when it has nothing.**

**And the one thing she did not refuse is a name.** A girl of seventeen wrote her name at the top of a plain slate because of a habit she has had since she was fourteen, and the woman who has not put a name at the top of a sheet for four years looked at it and did not ask for it to be taken off, **and has never once said so out loud to anybody, and the man of about forty-three, who is the only person in that room who could have said something about it, said nothing at all.** She also would neither put a fourth column into a nine-year register nor refuse one, and said both out loud on the same morning, and she is a person who would have said no if she thought a no would hold and she has not worked out yet whether a no would hold. **She is not Tamsin Quill and the two have still never been in one room.** *Open, and the consent fracture is not mended and nobody may mend it.*

### Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, twenty-six years a notary — she named it and she would not say how long

**She is in Chapters 0313 and 0316 and in no other of the ten, and she is on a page already from Chapter 0256 where she named the Short Month on the same reasoning. Her second handle is a date she has to write, and it is the same in both chapters.**

**In 0313 she puts a bag down shut on a table and does not open it all day, and she names the cost of a form before she is asked, and she refuses to make one on a day she has a form for: the day after the sixtieth is the only day a form belongs on, and she is not going to spend one of the nine days of writing she has left on the only thing anybody has asked her for this month.** She is asked the difference between entering a form and making one and answers twenty-six years and one word, and says she has never made one, and that making one is not a thing a notary does in a room with about nine people in it. **She names the second half of the cost unasked, which is that she had a form on Saturday morning and has not got one now, and that she has not told anybody that in nine years, and that she is telling it because she is about to write a form again.**

**In 0316 she is refused by a person and does it anyway, and she says the name once, in the local language, in daylight, in a room with a number in chalk on the door: the Bare Month.** She does not say it a second time and nobody asks her a second question about it. **Asked how long it is, in front of about nine people, she says I am not going to say, and asks for the declining to be entered as hers, and the reason she gives is her own: she has put a number at the head of a month once, the days did not stop where the number stopped, nobody found that out from the paper, they found it out from the days, and she is not going to guess at a number again in front of about nine people.** What she offers instead is in a mouth: write every day of it that somebody is asked to write, and when a clerk writes to her about a date he cannot place she will be able to tell him how many there have been and will not have told him in advance. **She wrote five lines with her name under the first, which is the first page in four hundred miles in about a month with a person's name at the top of it in a room, and she rubbed the count out of the door with a cloth and wrote the date in the new word, and the person at the top of it is a notary of fifty and not a household, and only one of the two is dangerous.** *Open, and no chapter of this volume may state a length for it and no chapter may state what it does at its end.*

### The man of about fifty-seven — he refused the month out loud and then said the only sentence in the chapter that was not about the month

**He is in Chapters 0313, 0316, 0317, 0318 and 0320 and his second handle is a length of new rope he has not used, and it is the same in all five, and it is not the man of about sixty's rope.** He is not angry in any of them and the chapters say so. **In 0316 he stands up off a wall in a room with about nine people in it and says one word and refuses the naming of the month, and not one of his four sentences is about the calendar: a word made in a room is a word somebody can be asked to have made, and he is the man who will be asked, because there is nobody else in that room who could be asked about a boundary and be asked about it honestly.** Nobody argued with him and nobody told him he is wrong and he is not defeated and he does not leave.

**And in 0317 he said the other half of the volume out loud, and it is the most useful sentence anybody has said in this flood about a page: a page they can ask to have produced is the only kind there has ever been that settled anything in his life, and he has been in a house four hundred miles off twice in forty years and both times a man brought out a book and neither time did the book say where the line ran and both times the man with the book was right.** He took four attempts to say it as a sentence and did not improve it between them. **He is the only man in the volume who wants the page that can be sent for, and the only person who has stood to lose is the register-keeper, and neither of them has been told they are on different sides and both of them know.** In 0320 he asked the first question anybody has asked the woman of about sixty-nine, in front of about nine people, and said before he asked it that he had never asked her anything, and then on his way to the stair said that he would have given the whole of what he paid for her paper, and that he is not going to say it again and is not going to be thanked for it. **In 0316, at the end, he asked whether the first day of the new month was a Tuesday, and was told yes, and said nothing else, and did not know until that morning that the days of the week were still standing.** *Open, and the money is still in his hand and nobody has taken it and nobody has refused it.*

### The man of about forty-three — he has the missing column and has never read it back in eleven years

**He is in Chapters 0313, 0314, 0315, 0316, 0317, 0318 and 0320.** He keeps nine boxes, eleven years, and an index of them in his own hand in four columns, one of which is a day. **In 0314 he said the reason out loud in a room on a Sunday: if he read the index back he would find nine mistakes and then have to decide what to do about nine mistakes, and he has never in his life wanted to decide what to do about anything on a Sunday.** He read the notary's five lines twice, the second time more slowly, and found a space under the last line about the width of two fingers with nothing in it, and said that a space is where a number goes when a person has decided not to write one, and that he has found four of those in nine boxes and two of them are a hundred and forty years old, and that he is not going to make a law of it and is not going to improve on it. He is the one who named the volume's calendar fault in a mouth in Chapter 0314: **you are not missing a column for the day the sheet went out, you are missing it because of the four days.** In 0316 he asked the notary why, in the voice a man uses when he has been in the trade for eleven years and has been asked to look at a thing and has found it short. In 0320 he read a woman's sheet standing up with his hands behind him and did not put it on the table, because a page on a table is a page in a room. **In 0315 he said nothing at all when a girl of seventeen wrote her name at the top of a slate, and he is the only person in that room who could have said something about a name at the top of a page.** *Open, and the eight names at the foot of the copy are still not read and he refused them in Chapter 0303 and has not been asked again.*

### Hettis Roke, forty-four, nineteen years in — he refused a room a roll and came back the day after the page was made

**He is in Chapters 0314, 0317 and 0318 and his second handle is a waxed slate in a case, the same in all three, and in 0314 he put it on the table face up and then turned it face down.** On day 314 he told the register-keeper that her three columns have no day in them for the day a sheet went out, and that this is why she cannot check it, and that he has known it for nineteen years and has never once wanted to be able to. **He refused the room his roll outright, said its shape out loud — a gate, a keeper, a set of days, nothing else, no column for a person, for longer than the oldest thing any of the four men who keeps it has — and asked that the refusal be entered, and nobody wrote it down.** He came back on day 317, the day after a page with a notary's name on it was made, and said four sentences into a row with nothing in it and asked that it be entered that a man can refuse a page and be in a room the day after a page is made and both of those are the same man. **He has eight people of his own on no page in four hundred miles and has wanted a page for them for nine years and did not ask for one and gave no reason, and the not-giving is the reason, and he said once in a mouth that a number at the bottom of a thing is the only part anybody ever sends for and that he is not putting eight names under a number on anybody's wall in this city.** *Open, and his roll is in no room and the two rolls of the same gates are not laid together and the word *holder* is not defined.*

### The man of about forty-one in a very good coat — the first person the new form costs something to

**He is in Chapter 0318 and his second handles are the same as they have been: a hand inside a coat with nothing in it, put in and taken out and put in, and nine days of a page in the coat whose front he has not shown anybody.** He stands for two hours and does not sit down. **He came because on Tuesday a woman made a form and he is the only person in four hundred miles already hurt by that happening, and he would rather say it here than have it said for him in about four months by a man who has never met him.** He says the chapter's finding in four parts: a form is for the person who has to fill it in later; the person holding the copy four hundred miles inland is going to have a day in a word that has no number on it and will have to write the number in himself, and whatever he writes will be wrong and will be his handwriting; **a day is not a date**; and until Tuesday he could count and could not name, and now he can name and cannot count, and he is the man in the room who has to hold both. He asked for nothing and said he is not going to be able to say in about four months that nobody warned him. He said he would come back when the room had put something on the form and did not say when, and nobody asked him to. *Open, and he is in Chapter 0322 later in this volume and he is the only person in the volume who is harmed by the calendar.*

### The man of about thirty-six, who holds gates on a turn — he has started saying the day out loud

**He is in Chapter 0319 and in no other of the ten, and he is at a gate where four streets come together, which is not the shut gate and is not the keeper of the Redroot gate, and he keeps nothing and is not on the roll that went up on the wall.** His second handles are a bar stood against a post, a barrow, a spade, and a bootful emptied at the near end of a lane before he answers. **On day 319 he said the day out loud to a boy of about eleven with a yoke and two pails, and the boy said it back, and he said it a second time when the boy came back at the fourth hour, and neither of them asked the other anything.** He has held gates on a turn for nineteen years and has never once said a day out loud at one. The boy told him his mother has had no form for two days and has been doing it out loud at the door, and he said that is right and said he would tell him why, and it is that there is nothing to write it on. A woman of about twenty-eight stopped at the gate, which she has not done since the days went back, and neither of them asked the other anything. **He then said to the bar, not to the lane, that nineteen years of that is the whole of the trade and nobody has ever put it on a wall and he would like the oak to have it.** *Open, and the only other date on a gate in this city belongs to a woman of about fifty at a shut gate on another road who has never met him.*

### The woman of about fifty at the shut gate — she read it and left it up

**She is in Chapter 0312 and in no other of the ten, and she is asked nothing in it and nobody was sent to her.** Her second handles are the same five and unchanged: a brass key on a tarred cord in the apron pocket, a bar of ash in two brackets, a wooden hook, a piece of chalk, and a date in chalk on the post every morning with nothing rubbed out.
