On day 312, the fifty-seventh of the Short Month, **the wind took the asking off the wall at about the second hour of the afternoon and it went into the wet at the foot of it, and she picked it up off the wet stone and read it, having said out loud on the forty-sixth that she had not read it and was not going to, and that reading it would be the beginning of having been asked.** The back of that sheet is blank, and a second sheet in this city has a question on the back of it in another hand, and she has not seen it and is not going to. **She put it back up and left it up and gave the two reasons out loud to a post: a paper with nothing at the top of it cannot be taken off a wall on behalf of a person, and there is nobody to take it off for; and she has read it and has not been asked, and those are two separate facts and she is keeping them apart on purpose.** The man of about thirty-eight with the barrow came down the lane at about the fifth hour, did not stop, put his hand flat on the boards and took it off and went on. **Her name is on no page and may not be given, and the one thing only she and the man with the barrow know is not said in this chapter and nobody asked him about it.** *Open.*

### The girl of seventeen — one use, and she asked a question nobody answered

**She is in Chapter 0315 and in no other of the ten, and she is on no roll of anything and has copied every asking anybody has written in this flood and has signed none of them.** On day 315 she wrote her name at the top of a plain slate before she wrote anything else on it, in the ordinary way, and gave the reason out loud: because of the habit, and it is her slate, and nobody has ever once asked her for it, and that is the only reason it is still hers. She copied the last asking, wiped the slate and copied it again, because a thing that is going to be read out in a room has to be read off a copy and not off the paper it came on, and she has never explained that to anybody and nobody has ever asked her. **She read the sixtieth day of the Short Month out loud, and that it is the last one of those, and that there is a Monday on the end of it and the next one is a Tuesday, and she was made to say the second part again and slower.** She looked at the slate for the length of four words deciding whether reading counts as being asked, and decided that it does not, because reading is not answering and she has been on the wrong side of that line in about nine kitchens. **She asked the only question she asked in that room, which is what am I to copy for tomorrow, and nobody in the room answered her, and nobody explained it to her, and she is on no roll of anything and there is no address at which a sheet could be put in front of her.** *Open, and being on no roll of anything is not a qualification and nothing in this month has turned it into one, and no chapter of this volume fetches her, resolves her, or gives her a second use.*

### The woman of about sixty-nine and the tin — she came up the lane and set a condition

**She is in Chapter 0320 and in no other of the ten, and her second handle is a tin, invented in Chapter 0305 and the same here.** She came up from the end of the lane at about the eighth hour with nobody having sent for her, put the tin on the table and did not open it in front of about nine people, decided that the opening was hers and on her own time, and then opened it. **The sheet inside is thirty years old, in her own hand, with nothing at the top of it, no day on it and no name on it, and it has been opened and shut about four times in thirty years.** It is what two households call their own ground and where the water went. The man of about forty-three says it is more use than either of the two things on the other side of the table and that it settles nothing. **She refused, and named the cost first: a page with her hand on it and a date at the top of it is the thing a clerk can send for, and if somebody four hundred miles off sends for it in four years then it goes and it does not come back.** Her condition is in her own words and has not been improved on by anybody: **it stays on that table, not entered, not copied, not taken out of the room; and if it is ever going to be entered she is to be asked first and told no if the answer is no, and she is not going to be told afterwards that it has already been done because she was in the room.** She went home down the lane with the tin empty in both hands. *Open, and the sheet is still on the table, and nothing on it has been entered.*

### The woman of about thirty-three, the man of about thirty-eight with the barrow, and the man of about thirty-one

**The woman of about thirty-three** is in Chapters 0311, 0313, 0317 and 0318. In 0311 she is the woman behind a bread board who gave the batch's third name for a piece of ground and said that nobody has ever been asked and that it is not a name anybody writes down because there is nowhere to write it. In 0317 she put the finding about the form in a mouth — that there is a name at the top of it and five lines and nowhere to say what a day was for — and said she has been wrong about that room since the forty-eighth of that month and would like to be told. In 0318 she said she had been right about it for a fortnight and the day the man of about forty-three told them was not the day she was right on, and would rather be right for a fortnight than be told on a Wednesday. **Her question about the order of nineteen stones is still unanswered and nobody has read the eight names at the foot of the copy.**

**The man of about thirty-eight with the barrow** is in Chapter 0312 and in no other of the ten. He came down the lane at about the fifth hour with the barrow empty and did not stop at the gate, put his hand flat on the boards for about as long as it takes to fill a pail, took it off, and went on down. **Nobody asked him anything and she asked him nothing and the boards are still against the wall. He is not the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf with a tally, and the two have never been in one room and may not be. He was not asked whether the woman at the gate knows what the line is, and that question is the volume's climax and no chapter of this volume may put it to him before then.**

**The man of about thirty-one, who carries things for a living,** is in none of these ten chapters. **He carried the first notice up a lane and the second one in a coat and is the reason a question in your own words is worth more than a question in mine, and he is not in any of these ten days and the reason is the same as in the last ten: there is no page in these ten chapters for him to carry anything to.**

### The next phase

**`workspace/volume-07/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, and it is a batch and not an outline.** It writes **Chapters 0321 to 0330, days 321 to 330.** **The man of about fifty-two who digs is invented on the page in Chapter 0321 with a rod he does not carry, and a chapter before 0321 may not invent it, and he may not put a rod in a third hole, may not clear or measure either of the two holes nine feet apart, and may not touch the line.** **Tamsin Quill is in 0324 and 0325 and 0325 is the decision about the route, and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register is in 0323, and Adrian is not in Chapter 0326 because a notice goes out in that chapter and a chapter in which people decline to ask him is a chapter he is not in.**

---

## THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0003 — ten days, ten people, and a name read off a page

**This block is the newest in this file and it wins.** It was written by the batch that wrote Chapters 0321 to 0330, days 321 to 330. It changed no day, no weekday, no anchor, no live thread's state and no decision of record, and it wrote no plan. The record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0003.md` and the rules the ten chapters put on a page are at `bible/power-system.md` §43. **Every person below was put on a page in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, by a person, or was already on one. No name and no place arrived by narration in any of these ten chapters.**

### Nev Cutler, a man of about fifty-two who digs — the name, and the rod he does not carry

**He is on a page for the first time in Chapter 0321, day 321, a Sunday, the sixth of the Bare Month, eleven miles the other way, and his name is said out loud in a room over a market in daylight with a date on the door, once, by the man of about forty-three who keeps nine boxes of other people's papers, off an index in his own hand with four columns including a day, off a shelf it had not been off in eleven years.** Fifty-two, in his own hand and not a clerk's. The place the index gives is the flats and not a house. The trade it gives is digging. **A later writer may give him this name and no other and may not give him a surname, and may not put his name at the top of a page, because Chapter 0321 is about a name being worth a person when it can be checked and worth nothing at all when it cannot.** About four of the nine people in that room wrote it down in their own heads and none of them put it on anything.

**His second handle is a rod he does not carry, invented on the page in Chapter 0321 and in no other chapter as an introduction, and it is the same in every chapter he is in.** A length of ash about four feet long with a flat blade on the end of it, ground down on one side and not the other, standing on its end in the corner of a hut at the head of the third row against the north wall where the wall is thickest, there since before the water, not leaning and not in a bag and not under a bench. **He does not carry it out on any morning. It is the only one in four hundred miles and no rod has been put in the line in the ground and no rod has been put in anything at all in any chapter of Volume 07.** He is at work in the third row at the fifth hour putting in willow into ground that has gone hard as a road under a rack's leg, and in Chapter 0325 he has nine in and a hundred yards of row left with a barrow and a bundle on it.

**Nobody has asked him anything in this manuscript and nobody was sent for him in Chapter 0321, and the reason he would be sent for is established in a room and not in a yard.** He is named in Volume 06 once, in Chapter 0299, as a man who would have refused in the time it takes to shut a gate and would have been right, and nobody has improved on that sentence. **In Chapter 0325 Adrian stands about nine feet off him in the wind on eleven miles of flats for the length of about nine spadefuls and does not say anything, and the man of about fifty-two does not say anything either and does not look at him and does not ask him what he wanted, and the chapter says Adrian worked it out and does not say he was right.** Open.

### Tamsin Quill — the six sentences, the four sheets, and the room on the low ground

**She is in Chapters 0324, 0325, 0326 and 0330 of this batch, and in none of the chapters the register-keeper is in, and nothing in this volume settles whether they are one woman.** Her second handle is unchanged and is the same in every chapter she is in: **a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time — long way in half, then in half again across, and the corner that came last went under** — and the reason she gave once in Chapter 0311 stands, and in Chapter 0326 she folds a sheet that is not hers once, and the once is the whole of what she is saying about it.

**In Chapter 0324 she leads the midpoint and says it in about six sentences and the sixth is the one that costs**, and in Chapter 0325 she names the instrument — four sheets, one for each of the four, in the words of each of the four, unsigned, with nothing at the top of any of them, one a day for four days — and gives the reason in a mouth: **a question in your own words is worth more than a question in anybody else's and the reason is nine years old and it is the carrier's and not the asker's.** In Chapter 0330 she is in a room over a shop on the low ground, tells a woman of about twenty-one that the other three exist and are not in the room, tells her she will not be asked what the eighteenth is for, and tells her that one of the four said yesterday in a room over a market that she is not coming to it. **The consent fracture is not mended and she does not apologise to him in either chapter and he does not ask her for anything, and the fifth condition is not given.** Open.

### The woman of about twenty-one — the pen she cut down herself, and the question she asked first

**She is on a page for the first time in Chapter 0330, day 330, a Tuesday, the fifteenth, in a room over a shop on the low ground, in daylight, with the day in chalk on its door, in front of about nine people, and she was put there by a question that went out in her own words on the eleventh of that month, unsigned, with no name at the top of it.** Her second handle is invented on the page in that chapter and in no other: **a pen with the nib ground narrow, cut down with a knife, because the one she was given was too broad and she is not going to be given another one.** She asked *what am I for* before she sat down. She said yes, unasked, in one word. She was told she will not be asked about the eighteenth and was told why. **Nobody in that room asked her whether it would go, including a man who carried a sheet in a coat and said so out loud to the room and not to her. She put her own handwriting flat on the table and read it, and she had the cut-down pen in her hand and did not write anything with it, because there is nothing on that table for her to write on, and she does not yet know that the shape of it is correct.** She is the first of four people and no word for what they are. Open.

### The man of about twenty-five — a return, and a boot mended with a length of cord

**He is a RETURN and not an introduction: he was put on a page in Chapter 0309 by a person in a room in daylight with a date on the door, and a state file that publishes a zero for him is wrong twice.** He is in Chapter 0327 of this batch and in Chapter 0331 and in no other of these ten. His second handle is invented on the page in Chapter 0327: **his left boot, mended at the front with a length of cord that is not new.** He was in the room on the fifty-fourth day of the Short Month and asked out loud what the day after the sixtieth would be and nobody answered him; in Chapter 0327 he has been there since before the light, he says he wrote something and is not going to say what it is and is not going to be asked, and he says out loud in daylight with a date on the door that he is not going to be asked, **and nobody asked him.** Open.

### The man of about forty-eight — a knife with a stiff hinge, and a window

**He is on a page for the first time in Chapter 0328, day 328, a Sunday, the thirteenth, in a room over a market, in daylight with a date on the door, and the movement that put him there is a question going out in his own words four days before a room on the seventeenth, unsigned, with no name at the top of it.** His second handle is invented on the page in that chapter: **a folding knife with a hinge that has gone stiff and does not spring, which he opens and shuts twice with the thumb of the other hand to make the joint take, and then it will hold about nine minutes.** He sits down the moment he comes into a room, because standing about is what a person does when they are expecting to be asked, and he has not been asked anything in a long time. **He mended the catch on a window in that room in about four minutes with the knife and his thumbnail, unasked, and the draught stopped and the wind did not, and nobody in that room will be able to say who did it.** His trade is climbing — ladders, mostly, and a bit of shuttering — about nine days of it since the water and about four of them for people who could not pay, and he said that in a room with a date on the door rather than say it at a door. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register asked him the first question anybody has asked him in this city and it was about his trade and not about the boundary and not about the sheet and not about the four days, and said she would not ask a second one, and he answered.** Open.

### The woman of about sixty-one — a stick she carries and does not lean on, and four words

**She is on a page for the first time in Chapter 0329, day 329, a Monday, the fourteenth, in a room over a market, in daylight with a date on the door, and the movement that put her there is a question going out in her own words four days before a room on the eighteenth, unsigned, with no name at the top of it.** Her second handle is invented on the page in that chapter: **a stick of hazel she carries in her hand and does not lean on.** She is the fourth of the four, and the one who says no, and **she says it unasked, in four words, in a room with a date on the door, before anybody has finished the question she was not asked: I am not going.** The room does not argue with it and nobody in it is going to be told she is wrong or told she is right. She also said out loud, in the same room, **that there are four of them and that there is no word in this city for what the four of them are and that she has looked for one for a long time and there is not one and that that is not an injustice, that is just the language**; and that the whole of what the four of them were told is that they have never once opened anything, given in about four words by a man who is not in the room. **Nobody wrote either of them down, and the string is at zero, and nobody was asked whether it would go and nobody was sent to ask her.** Open.

### Hettis Roke — two chapters, the case turned face up, and the space for a person

**He is in Chapters 0322 and 0323 of this batch and in no other of the ten, and his second handle is unchanged: a waxed slate in a case.** In 0322 he puts the case on the table face down and then turns it face up while another man is finishing a sentence, and does not open it, and says he is not going to be the man who puts a number under a line and would like it entered that he said so before anybody asked and not after. He fills in a second thing on the back of the form at about the fifth hour, and it is a gate, a set of days and a number, and there is a space on it for a person and he does not write in it, and the space is for the man in the office, who has never once written in it. In 0323 he holds the case in his hand rather than on the table, says he is not going to tell her how he keeps his and she is not going to ask him, says one sentence — a gate, a set of days, and a number — and says he is not going to be asked what that is worth. **His roll is in a room in both of these chapters and is not laid beside the Crown's roll of the same gates and the two are not compared and nobody says what the difference is worth, and a keeper is not a holder and the word *holder* is not defined in any of the ten and the request to strike it out of any list is not printed again.** Open.

### The man of about forty-three — the index off the shelf, and the clerk's copy read twice

**He is in Chapters 0321, 0322, 0323, 0324 and 0325 of this batch.** In 0321 he opens an index in his own hand with four columns including a day, off a shelf it has not been off in eleven years, and reads three lines and stops with his thumb on the third and says a name out loud once. In 0323 he reads the clerk's copy out a second time, which took about four minutes and which nobody stopped, and says what he wants said: that there are four instruments in a book in this room and a clerk's hand a hundred and forty years old on a table in this room, and they have got nothing to do with each other, and the nineteen stones and the eight that are different are not resolved. In 0324 he sits through a decision about a boundary for about four minutes without once putting his hand on the only page in four hundred miles that says where the line runs. **He is a reader and not an interpreter and the eight names at the foot of the copy were not read in any of these ten chapters and are not read now.** Open.

### The man of about fifty-seven — a season and a certainty, and one question

**He is in Chapters 0324, 0325, 0326, 0328 and 0329 of this batch, with the length of new rope he has not used, and the rope is the same in every one of them.** In 0324 he takes the rope off a chair back and puts it over his arm and says the only thing he says in that room: **then I have lost a season and I have still got a certainty, and I would like both of those entered in whatever this room writes down, and I am not going to say that anybody in this room is wrong, because there is nobody in this room who is wrong and I have checked.** In 0325 he asks the only question expected of him — is there a day on them — and is told that if it were three days it would not be a notice, it would be a delivery, and then he says nobody is going to tell him it is the right thing to do, and is told that nobody is going to tell him the other either, and asks for that to be entered in a room and not in a book. In 0326 he asks what happens to it if nobody comes, is told nothing happens to it, says that is not what he asked, and is told to ask it in a room on the fifteenth. In 0328 he says the only thing he has said to another person in that room in three mornings, which is *you are not going to be paid for that*. In 0329 he says the room on the eighteenth has three people in it. **No chapter of this volume tells him he is wrong, he is not defeated, he does not leave, and the money is still in his hand.** Open.

### The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register — the first entry, and four instruments

**She is in Chapters 0321, 0322, 0323, 0327, 0328 and 0329 of this batch and is in none of the four chapters Tamsin Quill is in.** Her second handle is her second slate and it is the same in every chapter, and in Chapter 0323 it lies face down on the table all morning and she turns it over to write the day on the face of it. **In 0322 she fills in the notary's form for the first time in four hundred miles, in her own three columns, and says that what came back was nothing and that what came back is always nothing and that she had never minded it and minded it that morning.** In 0323 she says out loud, in daylight, with a date on the door, that there are four ways of writing a day in that book and no column saying which one any row is in, and that she did not leave the column out and has never wanted it, and that there is a worse thing in the same book that they are not going to get to today. In 0321 she says out loud that nobody in this city has ever written down a day on which somebody was not sent for. **In 0328 she asks the man of about forty-eight the first question anybody has asked him in this city and says she will not ask a second one, and in 0329 she does not write down the only thing a woman of about sixty-one said that was worth writing down.** Open.

### The man of about forty-one in a very good coat — the number he will have to write in himself

**He is in Chapter 0322 of this batch and in no other of the ten, with a hand inside a coat with nothing in it, put in and taken out and put in, and nine days of a page in the coat whose front he has not shown anybody.** He came back on a day of his own choosing, having said he would come back when the room had put something on the form and not said when, and said so out loud before anybody asked him. He asked to be in the room when somebody fills the form in and said he was not going to ask anybody to hurry. **He is the only person in four hundred miles already hurt by a form with a notary's name on it, and he said the finding out loud: a day is not a date, and the person holding the copy four hundred miles inland is going to have to write a number in himself, and whatever he writes will be wrong, and it will be his handwriting, and he will not be able to tell anybody how he got it.** He is the only man in this batch who is harmed by the instrument this volume built and nobody in the room can do anything about it. Open.

### The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living — the carrier, and the reason is nine years old and it is his

**He is in Chapters 0326, 0327, 0328, 0329 and 0330 of this batch, and he carried all four of this volume's notices out of a room over a market, and he is the reason a question in your own words is worth more than a question in the asker's.** He said it out loud in Chapter 0326 and had been saying it for nine years and nobody had ever wanted to hear it: **a notice in a hand is not a page, and a hand is not a page, and a hand stays in the hand that made it until that person dies, and a sheet with my hand on it asking a person to do a thing can be argued with by anybody who wants to argue, and a sheet with their own hand on it cannot be argued with at all, and that is not a good thing, and it is the only reason this works.** He took the third sheet out at about the ninth hour in Chapter 0329 and did not look at the woman while he did it, and the not-looking was careful. Open.

### The girl of seventeen — a copy and a question, and no place at which a sheet can be put in front of her

**She is in Chapter 0327 of this batch and in no other of the ten, and she is her second appearance in this volume, and her second handle is unchanged: a plain slate, a lump of chalk and a cloth.** She put her name at the top of the slate before she had anything else on it. **She copied the second of the four onto it, both sides, the way she has copied every asking anybody has written in this flood, and she has signed none of them and she was not going to sign this one, and she asked the only question she asked in that room, which was where does this one go, and was told in front of about nine people that she is not one of the nine and that the person answering does not know the answer to the second half of it.** Then she went out of the room about a quarter of an hour after the carrier and went the other way. **She is on no roll of anything, there is no address at which a sheet can be put in front of her, the notice reached nine places and not her, being unfindable is not a qualification, and no chapter of this volume resolves her or gives her a reading out.** Open.

### The woman of about fifty at the shut gate, and the keeper of the Redroot gate — neither is in these ten chapters

**The woman of about fifty at the shut gate is in none of Chapters 0321 to 0330, was asked nothing, and no chapter of this batch sent anybody to her for any reason.** Her five handles are unchanged and a later writer may not give her a name. In Chapter 0326 the room comes to the difference between a place and a house and the narrator says the first time a room over a market came to it was a woman of about fifty at a shut gate deciding on her own that a sheet on a wall was about nobody, **and that she has not been told any of this and is not going to be.** In Chapter 0328 a man mends a window and the chapter says the man who closed it is not the woman of about fifty at a shut gate on another road and has never met her and is not going to. **The keeper of the Redroot gate is in none of these ten chapters, her days were not counted, her year is not named, and she is not used.** Open.

### Adrian Vale — named in two of the ten, 0321 and 0325 and not 0324, and in no chapter in any form in the other eight

**He is named in Chapter 0321 and in Chapter 0325 and in the other eight of the ten he is in no chapter in any form, and the earlier record in this file said 0324 and was wrong.** In 0321 he is in a room reading a clerk's hand on the sheet pinned to the corner of a deed and he puts a finger about a finger's width above the last line of the list and does not touch it, **and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register says Adrian and then nothing else about him for the rest of the morning, and nobody in that room asks him one question about the boundary.** In 0325 he is eleven miles the other way on eleven miles of flats standing about nine feet off the man of about fifty-two who digs for the rest of an afternoon, **his name is on the page twice, and a man out of doors in the wind is not the case the naming rule was written for, and the departure from the rule is on 0325 and not on 0324.**

**In 0324 he is in the room for the whole morning and the name is not on that page: Tamsin Quill says his name once and the chapter does not print it, so he is *he* and the man who brought the plan from end to end and a reader cannot put a name to him.** He reads the sheet from the top down twice and turns it over to look at the back of it, which is blank, and puts a plan on a table in about nine words, **and Tamsin Quill says his name once and then says nothing else about him for the rest of the morning, and nobody in that room asks him one question about the boundary or about anything else, and the chapter ends with her keeping the count of five mornings in a row in a book nobody else can read.**

The eight whose pages do not carry his name are 0322, 0323, 0324, 0326, 0327, 0328, 0329 and 0330, and the reasons are not one reason: in five of them he is not in the room and nobody came to fetch him; in 0324 he is in the room and is not named in it, which is a reason of its own and is neither of the other two; and in 0326 and 0330 he is in no chapter in any form and the chapter does not say so. **The chapters in which the not-asking is shown rather than asserted are 0321, 0324 and 0325, and that is three, and it is a different count from the number of chapters he is named in and the two must not be merged.** He is aged nowhere in Volume 07, is Stage 2 on all fifty days, performed no working, opened no threshold, was offered no workway and asked for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across these ten files. Open.

### The next phase

**`workspace/volume-07/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, and it is a batch and not an outline.** It writes **Chapters 0331 to 0340, days 331 to 340**, one chapter to one day, ending on a Friday. **It carries the fifth and last notice, on the twentieth of the Bare Month for a room on the twenty-fourth, four days, unsigned, with no name at the top of it, for Nev Cutler, and it is a chapter about four days of a man knowing he is going to be asked, in which there is no form at all.** It carries the second, the third and the fourth of the four in rooms on days 331, 332 and 333, and the fourth of them is a woman of about sixty-one who has already said no. It carries the volume's one panel, on day 340, refused out loud in about four seconds by the man of about thirty-seven who trades on the Open Hand board. **Nobody is asked whether it will go.**

---

## VOLUME 07, BATCH 0004 — ten days, ten people, and nobody in them got stronger

**APPEND-ONLY. The block above is Batch 0003's and nothing in it is superseded. Where this file and a chapter disagree about a day, a name or a figure, the chapter is right. Batch record: `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0004.md`; the ten days are on a page at `bible/power-system.md` §44.**

**ADRIAN VALE.** Named in **one** of the ten chapters, 0339, and in the other nine in no chapter in any form; in it he is in a room reading a page and is asked no question about it. **He is in no form in Chapter 0334, and the batch record said he was in it and was wrong, and the correction is a count of one and not of two.** **In 0339 he reads the clerk's hand on the sheet pinned to the corner of the deed from the top down, both columns, puts a finger about a finger's width above the last line and does not touch it, and says one thing about the shape of a condition and then stops.** He is aged nowhere in Volume 07 and is Stage 2 on all ten days; no working was performed, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark was not opened a third time, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, and *passage* and *privilege* are at zero. The two chapters in which a room's not-asking is shown on the page rather than asserted are 0333 and 0339, and that is a different count from the one chapter he is named in, and the two must not be merged.

**NEV CUTLER, a man of about fifty-two who digs, eleven miles the other way, is now on a page twice.** Chapter 0335, at work at the fifth hour and told there is a room on the twenty-fourth, and Chapter 0339, in a room over a shop on the low ground, saying yes. **He is a RETURN and not an introduction, and the name was read off an index in a reader's own hand in Chapter 0321 and is on no page since.** His second handle is **a rod he does not carry** — a length of ash about four feet long with a flat blade on the end of it, ground down on one side and not the other, standing on its end in the corner of a hut against the north wall where the wall is thickest — and it is the same in both chapters and it does not appear in the room. **In 0339 he says yes and states the condition himself before anybody asks him for it: he will look at it from the end of it and will not put his hands on it. The reason is a trade and not a modesty and it is his: a man whose whole life is putting things in holes is a man people hand holes to, and a man people hand holes to is not asked what is in the ground.** He offered the fact of the rod unasked and nobody asked him about it. Nobody asked him whether it would go. He did not leave and he is not going to.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FIVE, the second of the four who have not left.** Chapter 0331, a room over a shop on the low ground, the sixteenth. **Second handle: his left boot mended at the front with a length of cord that is not new.** He says yes in about four words without being asked and asks that it be entered that he said it and was not asked, and the room does not enter it. He will not have his own sheet read out. **He has nine words he wrote himself, has had them since Chapter 0327, gave the room the number nine and not the contents, and in Chapter 0333 looked at a doorway where the person he wrote them for was standing and did not say them to her or to the two who were in the room.** Nobody asked him whether it would go. *Open, and it is Chapter 0342's.*

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT, the third of the four who have not left.** Chapter 0332, a room at the back of a chandlery, the seventeenth. **Second handle: a folding knife with a hinge that has gone stiff and does not spring, which he opens and shuts twice with the thumb of the other hand to make the joint take, and which then holds about nine minutes.** He says yes in the first nine seconds of the morning, before anybody has sat down properly, and says he would like the room to notice the order of those two things. He mends a skylight nobody asked him to mend, in about four minutes, with a knife, his thumbnail and a length of tarred twine, and asks for no tool. **He is the man in four hundred miles who can tell you that eight of nineteen stones are not the same stone as the other eleven and cannot tell you when, and a mortar tells you how long it has been mixed and not a year.** He is a man who goes up ladders for a living; about nine days of it since the water and about four of them for people who could not pay, said in Chapter 0328 and not repeated.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-ONE, the first of the four who have not left.** Not named as such in these ten chapters but present: Chapter 0333, a room over a bakehouse, the eighteenth. **Second handle: a pen with the nib ground narrow, cut down with a knife, which she has carried into every room since the fifteenth and has not written with in any of them.** She asked what she was for in Chapter 0330 and got no answer, and in Chapter 0333 she announces in a room with a date on the door **that she has stopped waiting to be told what she is for, and that the not-explaining is half of it and she would only be able to do the other half by having it explained, and then it would be telling and not finding out.** Nobody argued with it. She is in two rooms in these ten days and has asked once.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-ONE, the fourth of the four, who has said no.** Chapter 0333, at the door of a room over a bakehouse on the eighteenth, at about the ninth hour. **Second handle: a stick of hazel she carries and does not lean on, and she stands the way a person stands who has decided in advance how long she is going to be standing there.** She read her own date off the door in chalk, said nothing, was not turned out, and went back up the row. **THE NO IS NOT SAID AGAIN, NOT SOFTENED, NOT ARGUED WITH, NOT APOLOGISED FOR, NOT OVERTURNED AND NOT MADE A DEFEAT, and it was said on the fourteenth in four words before anybody asked and it is on a page in Chapter 0329.** She told the whole of what the four of them were told, in other words, in Chapter 0329, and it is on a page and went onto nothing, and the string is not to be printed by any file this repository produces.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING.** Chapters 0331, 0333, 0335, 0337, and referred to in 0326–0330. **He carried all five of Volume 07's notices on this side of the volume, the fifth of them eleven miles on a Sunday, and he did not open it, and about four people have worked out that the one he was least entitled to open is the one he carried furthest.** His instrument and his sentence are on a page in Chapter 0326: a hand is not a page, a sheet with somebody's own writing on it cannot be argued with at all, and that is not a good thing and it is the only reason the instrument works. **In Chapter 0335 he said the reason out loud on eleven miles of open ground, in a place where the man it was about was standing, which is the first time anybody has said it there.** He says in Chapter 0333 that he has carried four of these and has not opened one.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN.** Chapters 0334 and 0337, his second of his two in this batch. **Second handle: a length of new rope he has not used**, which he has carried into about nine rooms since the fifty-second day of that month and used in none. **He refused in Chapter 0334 the only page in four hundred miles that would have got his winter back, in a room where he is right, and said why: a page with his name at the top of it is a page somebody can ask for, and then there is a column, and he has been a column twice in his life and both times it was somebody else's.** In Chapter 0337 he asked at four of eight doors out past the last named house where their own ground stops, and not one of them knows, and he put a receipt with his own name at the top of it on a table and said he is not going to be told he is wrong. **He is not a villain, he is not wrong about most of what he says, he does not leave, he is not defeated, and the money is still in his hand.** Nobody in either room told him he was wrong.

**JONAH PIKE.** Chapter 0334, and in no other of these ten. Nineteen years running a warehouse on a river in another country, right about nine parts in ten for as long as anybody in this city has been able to check, and **he is wrong about a shut case in that room and nobody tells him.** He proposed a good thing and it was correct. He came straight to the part of the room he had come about and put his copy face up. **His queue on the upper road has been running since before the days went back without him on it, and is running this morning, and he walked back down to it and will not be told about the winter by anybody.** He is not a villain, is not defeated, is not leaving and is not converted.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE.** Chapters 0331, 0332, 0333 and 0339, four appearances, **and the batch record said 0335 and was wrong; the phrase *woman of about thirty-three* is at zero in Chapter 0335.** She keeps a room over a shop on the low ground and has a bread board on the sill of the window in it, and she has a question about the order of nineteen stones that she has carried since the forty-eighth day of the Short Month. **In Chapter 0332 she is told one thing taken off about nine hundred and one is not the answer, and she writes nothing on her sheet.** In Chapter 0339 she puts the bread board back at the end of the sill at about the fourth hour of the afternoon, and it is her door that carries the second date in chalk above the first with nothing rubbed out, and the finding is that a door with two dates on it and nothing underneath is still a notice and not a record. **A later chapter may say a room of hers carries two dates; a later chapter may not say she puts one there in a chapter she is not in.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO HOLDS GATES ON A TURN.** Chapter 0338, and in no other of these ten. **His second handle is a bar stood against a post, a barrow, a spade, and a bootful emptied before he answers, and the same in every chapter he is in.** He has been at that gate since before the light, the near sill has gone soft on the low side and the bar has begun to lift out of its drop, and **he puts in a second bar on the far side because the fourth street has begun to run across the worn ground where it used to stop, and a second bar is not a repair and is a doubling, and nobody sent for it.** He has been saying the day out loud at that gate since the fourth day of the Bare Month and has said it nineteen times, and he told the boy of about eleven to say it at the doors and not at the gate. **A keeper is not a holder, the word is not defined and is at zero on all ten files, the two rolls of the same gates are not laid together, and nobody on that road has ever been asked what the difference between the two words is worth.** The boy of about eleven has two appearances in Volume 07 and this is the second.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN AND THE BOARD.** Chapter 0336, and in no other of these ten. He washes the board every morning and has decided not to wash the wall above it, and **in this chapter he gives the reason out loud for the first time: the wall is about nine feet off the boards, it is the one people can read from the bottom of the yard and not the top, about nine feet is a height a man can wash standing on a board and not standing on a ladder, he has washed four walls since the water and the bottom half of about nine of them, and he is not going to be the man who makes a wall look like something is being done to it.** The board carries the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood, is not written on in this chapter, and the wall above it is not washed. **The queue in that yard is not waiting for anybody and nobody can say what it is for, and the chapter does not say it.**

**TAMSIN QUILL.** Chapters 0331 and 0336 and in no others of these ten. **Second handle: a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time, long way in half and in half again across and the corner that came last went under, and the ninth line is still empty.** In Chapter 0331 she says the finding of the morning out loud, that the four days are two four days and not one, and the man of about twenty-five tells her it is the first useful thing anybody has said in this city about a piece of paper and does not say it back. In Chapter 0336 she walks nine miles up a road in about six hours, a man beside her whom the chapter does not name, and does not ask the man of about twenty-seven what is on the wall above his board. **The consent fracture is visible and is not mended and nobody apologised in it and the fifth condition is not given, and nothing in this volume settles whether she and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register are one woman.** The register-keeper is in none of these ten files and is not referred to in any of them.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-THREE WHO KEEPS OTHER PEOPLE'S PAPERS.** Chapters 0337, 0339 and referred to in 0331 and 0334. **He did not open the book again in Chapter 0339 and said why, and the reason is his: the name is on a page, it was read out loud once in daylight in about four seconds, a person wrote it down in their own head and did not put it on anything, and that is the correct way for a name to be in a book, and he is not going to open it again for about nine people.** He is wrong about a shut case in Chapter 0334 and nobody tells him. He reads the clerk's hand out loud for about nine lines twice in Chapter 0337 and the man who has just come in from a gap for a cart does not hear a word of it.

**THE MAN WHO TRADES ON THE OPEN HAND BOARD.** Chapter 0340, and in no other of these ten. **He is given the trade and the board and NO AGE, deliberately, and the reason is a real discrepancy in the plan layer: `outline/volume-07.md` says a man of about thirty-seven, the prose of Volumes 02 to 06 says a man of about thirty-nine at eighteen named chapters, and Chapter 0309 of this volume says a man of about thirty-seven. They are the same man and a batch may not decide which figure is right.** Second handle: **a board, with nineteen rates on it and no names, carried face up because a man who trades on a board has never once carried one with the writing on the underside where the rain can get at it.** He has been asked to be added by name and refused in four seconds four times in four years, and every one of those times a woman who is on nothing and is named on nothing did the refusing. **What is on a page in Chapter 0340 and on no page before it: he refused a woman who is on nothing and is named on nothing at the back of a market, in nineteen words, about a hundred days into this flood, and has never said so to anybody; and about as long afterwards a man asked him to put his own name on that board in about the ninth year of this flood and he wrote it on in his own hand, and the man came and told the woman her name was not going on, and he took it off the next morning with his own cloth. About four hundred people in this city can see the place on the board where the name was. THE TWO DATES ARE SETTLED AND A LATER CHAPTER MAY NOT PUT THE REFUSAL OF THE WOMAN AFTER THE NAME WENT ON, because Chapter 0113 and Chapter 0123 are the page for the refusal and `outline/volume-07.md` §17.7 is the page for the name.** He refused the volume's one panel before a sentence was finished and the refusal is what made him say it.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY WITH A HANDCART AND A LENGTH OF ROPE.** Chapter 0340, and in no others of these ten. A return; he is on a page in Chapters 0300 and 0304 of this volume. **His rope has been used and is not new, and it is not the man of about fifty-seven's rope, which is new and has never been used, and the two are on a page separately and must stay separate.** He was told no at a gate and said he would come back about nine days from a morning, and it has been a good deal more than nine days, and he came back, **and he came into a room rather than to a gate, and he asked one question at about the fourth hour of the afternoon, which is whether a person can be put back, and nobody in that room answered him, and the wall does not answer it either.** He is the last image of the volume in Chapter 0348 and nobody has sent him.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL, THE GIRL OF SEVENTEEN, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT THE SHUT GATE, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WITH THE BARROW, THE KEEPER OF THE REDROOT GATE, HETTIS ROKE, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE IN A VERY GOOD COAT, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR AT THE WHARF WITH THE TALLY, AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-EIGHT** are in none of these ten chapters. **The woman of about twenty-eight is a return and appears once, in Chapter 0338, at a gate, and she says one thing over her shoulder about not having written a name at the top of anything.**

**AND NOBODY IN THESE TEN DAYS GOT STRONGER.** Adrian is Stage 2 on all ten. The gains are a man who read his own sheet and said nothing for four days, a yes nobody asked for, nine words nobody has said, a skylight and a window, eight of nineteen stones and nobody able to say when, a doorway nobody turned a person out at, a refusal of the only page that would have worked, a winter, four doors and a name for a piece of ground, a notice on the rough side of a board because there was no form, a man who will not put his hands on it, a second bar at a gate, a date said out loud to a boy, a rule about words on a wall, and a name off a board that nobody had been told about. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.**

---

## VOLUME 07 BATCH 0005 — the state of every person this batch put on a page, days 341 to 350

**The authoritative block for the current phase is the one headed *VOLUME 07 BATCH 0005*, above, and the next phase is `workspace/volume-07/close/PROMPT.md`. Everything above this block in this file is history and is superseded by it.**

**ADRIAN VALE.** **Named in ONE of the ten — Chapter 0346, a room over a market in which he reads an entry in a register and the empty third column and does not touch either, and is asked no question about it — and in NO CHAPTER IN ANY FORM in the other nine.** The rule is that he is named in the chapters in which he is reading a page in a room, and a chapter that has a page in it and nobody in the room identified does not count, and this one has a man identified. He is aged nowhere in Volume 07. He is Stage 2 on all fifty days. He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened a third time, the other world is not named once across the fifty files, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero. **He is in Chapter 0341, 0344 and 0349 in a room or a field where people do not ask him anything, and that is a different count from the number of chapters he is named in, and the two must not be merged.** **The consent fracture is visible in Chapter 0349 and is not mended and nobody apologised in it: she says he has not asked for the sheet since the second day and that she has noticed every day for nineteen days, and he says he is not going to ask her and is not going to put it in a room, and the fifth condition is not given.** The thing he brought and did not use this batch: a hand on a slate, a finger's width from an empty column, and nothing else.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL AND WROTE THE NINTH LINE.** Chapters 0342 and 0345 and in no others of these ten. **Second handle: a piece of chalk he does not use, and it is the same piece in both chapters and it is the same piece he carried in the inside breast pocket from the fifty-fifth day of the Short Month.** **HE IS ON NO ROSTER IN THIS CITY AND HAS ASKED TO BE ON NONE OF THEM, and a man with a piece of chalk he does not use and no standing is the climax of this volume, and the two are the same person and Chapter 0342 is able to say both.** He is barred from every roster at his own request, he had no standing to take anything back, and the instrument that removed a word from a page in this world is a room, in daylight, with a date on it, and a person. **He said the word in Chapter 0310, in one word, in the local language, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, and did not say what it means, and he took it off the second slate in Chapter 0342 in chalk with a cloth, and he did not use the chalk, because a slate is the one surface in four hundred miles a word can be taken off and a wall nine miles up the road is not.** His reason is four sentences and it is his. He was at the stair in Chapter 0342 and would not be thanked and would not be talked to on a stair. **The ninth line was not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved; he refused it a sixth time in Chapter 0345, he asked in advance not to be thanked and was not thanked, and whether it works is unanswered.** He is not a villain and is not the hero of Chapter 0342 and is not converted and is not leaving.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER.** Chapters 0342 and 0346 and in no others of these ten, and the two women of twenty-nine were never in one room and nothing in this batch settles whether they are one woman. **Second handle: her second slate, and the register, and a piece of chalk she put down beside the slate and did not pick up again.** She has three columns in her form and the third one is where a person goes, and nine years of entries have somebody's name in it. **In Chapter 0342 she put the slate on the table herself, said out loud that she can do this in a room in daylight with a date on the door and that is the whole of what she can do, that she cannot enter it because there is nothing to enter and she could not enter it if there were a column, and that the third thing cannot be done at all and everybody in the room has been working for a fortnight on the assumption that it can.** **In Chapter 0346 she ruled a line and wrote the thirty-first day of that month at the top and, underneath it, that a straight mark in a field may not be dug, with no name and no word for the mark and the third column left empty on purpose, and said out loud that she will leave the room rather than fill it, and that she is not going to write down what the word was even though she wrote it down once.** She told the man of about fifty-seven what he has, in the order of the facts. She did not ask anybody anything and nobody asked her anything.

**TAMSN QUILL.** **Chapter 0349 and in no other of these ten, and this is the only chapter of the last ten days in which she is on a page.** Second handle: **a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time, and a ninth line with nothing on it.** She did not put the ninth place in the nine and nobody saw her leave it out and everybody in the room understood it inside about four minutes. **She has had the sheet in the inside of her coat since the fifteenth of the Bare Month, which is nineteen days, and she has been in this room and that room and has not been asked for it once, and the consent fracture is visible and is not mended and nobody apologised in it.** She said she knows he is not going to ask, and that he has not asked since the second day, and that she has noticed every day, and that she is not asking him why, and that she would like it said in the room that she said she was not asking. He said he is not going to put it in a room because a room has a date on the door and everything that goes in it is on a page by the fourth hour. **She is the lead's want — the Lock of Salt and the Hearth Marches from being claimed — and this volume was hers and he followed it, and her want is not asked for and not answered in these ten days.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT THE SHUT GATE.** Chapters 0341 and 0347 and in no others of these ten. **Second handle, the same in both: a brass key on a tarred cord in the apron pocket, a bar of ash in two brackets, a wooden hook, a piece of chalk, and a date in chalk on the post every morning with nothing rubbed out.** **She gives no name and no name may be given her. NOBODY IN EITHER CHAPTER ASKS HER ANYTHING, INCLUDING BY THE PROTAGONIST, AND NOBODY WAS SENT TO HER FOR ANY REASON, and the carrier went up that lane on the Saturday with nothing in his hands so that if anybody did ask him he would be able to say nothing at all.** She put the twenty-sixth on the post and then the thirty-second, and neither of them rubbed anything out, and about four people on that lane have worked out since that a column of dates with nothing under it is the same shape as the entry that went into a book in this city on Thursday. **The one thing only she and the man with the barrow know is not said in Chapter 0341 and is not said in Chapter 0347 and is not said anywhere in fifty chapters, and the two of them have never spoken about it and neither of them is going to be asked.** She said out loud to a man's back that she is not going to be asked when the boards come off that wall, and she said out loud to the man with the barrow that she is not going to be asked about the boards and is not going to offer anybody anything about the boards, and neither of those is a question. **The cost of the not-asking is not hers and Chapter 0341 says so.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WITH THE BARROW.** Chapters 0341 and 0347 and in no others of these ten. **Second handle: a barrow, with the rope hooked back over the handle where he puts it.** He came up the lane on the twenty-sixth of the Bare Month, went past the gate, put the barrow down about nine hundred paces off it, stood with his forearms on the handles, looked at one side of the road, did not go in, and came back down and stopped at the boards on the thirty-second, which is the first time in about a month he has stopped at that gate. **He is the only person in four hundred miles who knows that the woman at that gate saw a straight line in that orchard and told him and nobody else, and the chapter does not explain it and does not hint at it in a way that explains it, and he was not asked about it in either chapter and is not going to be.** He said the whole of what he did on the Saturday because nobody was going to ask him for it, and he did not say what he stood there for. He took nine boards off a wall one at a time in the wrong order and lost a quarter of an hour, and the two soft ones went on the bottom of the barrow.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS.** **Chapter 0344 and in no other of these ten, and it is the chapter he goes in.** Second handle: **a rod he does not carry — a length of ash about four feet long with a flat blade on the end of it, ground down on one side and not the other — standing against the thick wall of his own hut eleven miles the other way, and he offered the fact of it out loud in a room on the twenty-fourth before anybody asked him for it and did not bring it.** He walked eleven miles. **He put a barrow against the end of a cut at a stone about nine hundred paces off a shut gate, and looked along a mark in the ground the whole way from the end of it, and did not touch it, and did not put a foot in it and did not stand over it and did not go along it, and came back four paces at a time.** He said one sentence and it was to the man with the handcart and not to the field: he cannot tell you one thing about it that he did not know at the seventh hour, and he would like that said out loud before anybody asks him what he saw, and that is all he has and he has walked eleven miles. **The sentence is not an answer. Nothing was measured, nothing was dug, nothing was counted, no rod went into anything, and he is the only character in the last ten days who goes and looks at the line.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY WITH A HANDCART AND A LENGTH OF ROPE.** Chapters 0344, 0348 and 0350, and in no others of these ten. **Second handle: a handcart and a length of rope that has been used and is not new.** He was told no about a fence across the top of that road in the first week of that month, said he would come back, has come back four times, and **he was in Chapter 0344 at the end of the same cut the digger stood at and he built nothing that morning and nobody sent him.** He cut nine willow posts in a copse about four miles the other way and stacked them in a cart, set out from a row of trees, stopped at a stone, and did not measure anything: **four feet is a number he has in his mouth and not a number he took off anything, and he said so out loud, and a fence is not an account of anything and does not know what it is four feet off.** He put nine posts in the ground on the Saturday and ten by the Monday and had three left in the cart and said he would finish on the Thursday. **Nobody sent him and nobody stops him and it will be there on Tuesday.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN.** Chapters 0341, 0343, 0345, 0346, 0348, 0349, 0350. **Second handle: a length of new rope he has not used, and a folded paper with a sum on it and his name at the top of it, which is the only sheet in four hundred miles with his name on it and which he has been carrying since before the water.** He bought half a holding about forty years ago. He asked four doors in a row where their own ground stops and not one of them knows. **He said the naming bought that field one afternoon and cost that field thirty-two days and that he is not going to be told it is a sad one. He was told, in a room in daylight with a date on the door, what he has: a line he cannot have shown anybody and a winter he cannot get back, and the only page that would have shown anybody what he paid for is one he refused on the nineteenth and was right to refuse. He said the cost out loud and nobody in that room told him he was wrong. He is not a villain in this volume, is not converted, does not leave, is not happy, and is right about most of what he says. He could have stopped a fence and did not move out of the way and then moved, and he put his hand flat on the second post the way a man does to a gate post and did not press on it.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE.** **Chapter 0346 and in no other of these ten. Second handle: a tin, carried a mile in both hands, and it was empty.** She came up from the end of the lane at about the eighth hour and nobody had sent for her, which is the only two kinds of coming there is. **Her sheet of paper thirty years old, in her own hand, with nothing at the top of it and no day on it and no name on it, has been on a table in a room over a market since the fifth of that month and has not been moved and has not been copied and does not go out of the room, and the condition she put on it on the fifth is hers and nobody may un-condition it.** She did not look at it a second time. **She asked, once, in that room, on that day, and nobody answered it: what is a person for, on a page, when the only thing anybody needs them for is a journey.** That is the question this volume hands on and it is on a page and it is not answered.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO HOLDS GATES ON A TURN.** **Chapter 0345 and in no other of these ten. Second handle, the same in every chapter he is in: a bar stood against a post, a barrow with a spade lying across its handles, and a bootful emptied before he answers.** He put a second bar in a bracket of his own making on a Wednesday night because a gate that does not shut is not a gate, and nobody sent for it. **A roll in the Marches is a gate, a keeper, a set of days and nothing else, and a gate with two bars is one gate and goes on the roll as one gate, and the second one is on no roll at all, and no clerk four hundred miles off is going to come up that road and count them.** He said he was not going to say what that is worth and that it is not because he does not know. **A keeper is not a holder, the word is at zero across the fifty files, and the two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FIVE.** Chapters 0342 and 0349 and in no others of these ten. **Second handle: a left boot mended at the front with a length of cord that is not new, and nine words in the inside of his coat that he wrote himself on the eleventh of the Bare Month and has not said in any room.** **He was standing in the room on the twenty-seventh of the Bare Month with a person beside him when a word came off a slate in a cloth, and he did not say them, and nobody asked him, and he did not say them in Chapter 0349 either.** He and the woman of about twenty-one had the ninth line of a sheet of road between them on a table and neither of them put it there in front of anybody. **The nine words are on a page at the end of this volume and they are a live thread.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-ONE.** Chapters 0342 and 0349 and in no others of these ten. **Second handle: a pen with the nib ground narrow, cut down with a knife, and she has not written anything with it in any room she has been in since the fifteenth of the Bare Month.** **She said on the fifteenth that she had stopped waiting to be told what she is for, and on the thirty-fourth she said it again in a shorter form, and the second time is not a complaint, and the not-waiting is not a mood and nobody in the room is going to change it by saying something.** She is standing in Chapter 0342 while a word comes off a slate and she is not asked anything.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT.** Chapters 0342 and 0349 and in no others of these ten. **Second handle: a folding knife with a hinge that has gone stiff and does not spring, which he opens and shuts twice with his thumb and that will then hold about nine minutes.** He said yes before anybody in that room had finished asking, in Chapter 0332, and he was not asked anything about it, and he did not hand the knife to anybody in Chapter 0342 when he was asked to hold a pen.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING.** Chapters 0341, 0342, 0343, 0344, 0347, 0348, 0350. He has carried five sheets in this flood and has not opened one. **He said the one sentence that made the Monday afternoon worse, and it was to the room and not to the man, and it was: a man told this room yesterday that there is a man with a barrow who has been up that lane; a man who digs is going to stand at the end of a cut in that field on a day not one of us knows; the word came off a slate in this room on Sunday because of the first of those; there is no form in four hundred miles that holds a thing and what it costs.** He has said out loud that he is not going to ask whether it will go, and he has said it in about nine rooms in a fortnight, and a woman who keeps a register told him it is not a sentence about whether anything works and never was, and he said it has never once done the slightest good and he is going to keep saying it. **He came up that lane on the Saturday with nothing in his hands so that if anybody did ask him he would be able to say nothing at all.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE WHO KEEPS A ROOM.** Chapters 0341, 0345, 0348. She has a bread board at the end of a sill. **In Chapter 0341 she said the one thing she was allowed to say in this city, and it was to the man of about forty-three and not to the room: he has had the deed out and the sheet off it in front of about nine people three times in about a fortnight, and each time he has been right about wanting it and wrong about why, and she has not said so and is not going to be asked.**

**AND NOBODY IN THESE TEN DAYS GOT STRONGER.** Adrian is Stage 2 on all ten. The gains are a word off a slate, thirty-two days, a barrow at the end of a cut, a sentence that is not an answer, a second bar on no roll, a refusal of what it is worth twice, an entry with an empty third column, nine boards and a bare wall, ten posts and a fence, and a hand on a gate post. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.**

**AND THE PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS, AND ARE NOT TO BE PUT IN THEM BY A LATER WRITER.** The man of about thirty-four at the wharf with the tally of the four hundred and forty. The man who trades on the Open Hand board, who is given the trade and the board and NO AGE. Hettis Roke, forty-four, nineteen years in. The man of about forty-one in a very good coat, and the sheet he will not turn over, which is not the Crown's schedule. The keeper of the Redroot gate, whose days are not counted and whose year is not named. The girl of seventeen. The man of about twenty-seven and the board. The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by a wharf, who is a different person from the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, and **no paragraph of Volume 07 carries the two of them together.**
